## 29. ZATCA Onboarding Interface Review & Simulation/Production Readiness (Jul 2026)

Follow-up architecture review requested after items 27/28 proved the sandbox cryptographic core genuinely correct. Goal: close every gap between "works for sandbox" and "seamless, error-free Simulation/Production onboarding" — switch environment, provide a real OTP (~1hr validity), done — plus enforce ZATCA-mandatory customer/vendor fields and give every ZATCA API failure a real diagnostic trail. Full plan reviewed and approved by the project owner before implementation; then implemented, verified against real ZATCA sandbox end-to-end (new company, full onboarding, real B2B + B2C invoices), and self-corrected using its own new logging when a real ZATCA rejection surfaced a bug.

### What changed
- **`apiClient.ts`**: `isMocked` now only true for `production` without `ZATCA_ALLOW_LIVE_CALLS` — Simulation was previously *always* mocked unconditionally (`environment !== 'production'` always true), meaning no code path could ever make a real Simulation call regardless of credentials. Also found and fixed two silent-success bugs while touching this file: `requestProductionCsid` and `clearStandardInvoice`/`reportSimplifiedInvoice` never checked `response.ok`, so a real HTTP rejection without the expected body field would fall through and report a fabricated success (`isOnboarded: true` with an undefined cert; `CLEARED`/`REPORTED` on a real error).
- **`processInvoice.ts`**: the honest `NOT_SUBMITTED` guard (added in item 26 for sandbox only) now applies to every environment — Simulation without a Production CSID no longer falls through to a mocked fake `CLEARED`.
- **Schema**: taxpayer identity (TIN/CR/address) moved from a single shared slot on `companies` into `zatcaEnvironmentConfigs`, per-environment — matching how credentials already worked. Sandbox, Simulation, and Production each need a genuinely different identity (ZATCA's own test identity vs. the company's real registration), and the old shared slot meant onboarding one environment's real data silently overwrote another's. Migrated via hand-written SQL (additive columns, backfill from `companies` into each company's currently-active environment row, then drop the old columns) — no data loss, small existing dataset (2 companies) verified before and after.
- **`ZatcaOnboardingWizard.tsx`**: Steps 3+4 (OTP entry, Compliance CSID issuance) collapsed into one — the target UX is "switch environment, provide OTP, done," and a fresh OTP is only valid ~1 hour, so the extra click was pure avoidable latency. Added client-side identity validation mirroring the server, a confirm-before-regenerating-keypair guard (silently invalidates an already-issued CSID otherwise), a guard against activating a non-onboarded environment, and corrected two stale UI claims (a false "encrypted in Cloud SQL" storage claim, and a "prototype, not compliant" banner that predated items 27/28's real verification work).
- **`server/lib/zatca/validators.ts`** (new): shared VAT/postal-code/mandatory-field validation, used by (a) company identity before CSR generation — catching a malformed value before an OTP attempt is spent on it — and (b) customer/vendor buyer records.
- **Customer/vendor mandatory ZATCA fields**: `customers`/`vendors` already had the right buyer columns (`vatNumber`, address fields, `buyerType`) but nothing enforced them, and — separately discovered while wiring this up — the UI's "VAT Registration Number" field saved into the generic `taxRegNumber` column, not the `vatNumber` column ZATCA XML generation actually reads, so no B2B invoice could ever have carried a real buyer VAT. Added the missing ZATCA fields (buyer type toggle, VAT, full address) to `MasterEntities.tsx`'s customer/vendor form, enforced server-side in `POST /customers`/`/vendors` and again as a final gate in `processInvoiceZatca` before any XML is built. B2B requires a full, verifiable identity; B2C only requires a name, per ZATCA's actual (and much more lenient) Simplified-invoice rules.
- **Structured ZATCA API error logging**: `apiClient.ts`'s thrown errors now carry the full ZATCA response body (previously only `data.message` reached the caller, discarding the `errors` array with the actual field-level detail). Both thrown errors and non-exceptional ZATCA rejections (`clearanceStatus: 'REJECTED'` on an ordinary HTTP 200 — checkCompliance/clearance/reporting can all return this without throwing) are now persisted to the existing `auditLogs` table (`action: 'zatca_api_error'` / `'zatca_invoice_rejected'`) via the project's existing `recordAuditLog` helper, not just a transient `console.error`.
- **`transactions.ts`** (unrelated pre-existing bug found and fixed during end-to-end testing): `POST /invoices` never set `created_by_id`, a `NOT NULL` column — every direct invoice creation through this route failed outright. Other insert paths in the same file (quotation conversion, POS, vouchers) already set it correctly; this one didn't.

### The new logging caught a real bug in this same pass
End-to-end testing (new company, full sandbox onboarding, real B2B + B2C invoices) hit a genuine ZATCA rejection on the first B2B invoice. The new structured logging surfaced ZATCA's actual error — `BR-KSA-44`: a buyer VAT number's first *and last* digit must both be `3`, not just the first, contradicting the commonly-quoted "15 digits starting with 3" rule this project's own validator had used. Fixed the regex (`^3\d{13}3$`) in all three copies (server validator, wizard client mirror, customer/vendor form client mirror) and re-verified live. This is exactly the diagnostic value the logging change was built to provide — a real Simulation/Production rejection is now traceable to ZATCA's own exact reason from `auditLogs`, not left as a generic "clearance failed."

### Verification performed
- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after every change.
- Full live end-to-end test against real ZATCA sandbox, all done through the actual application code paths (real API routes, real invoice-creation route, real background ZATCA processing — not curl against isolated endpoints): created a brand-new company, bank, tax slab, fiscal month, one B2B customer and one B2C customer; ran the full onboarding sequence exactly as the wizard does (identity → keypair/CSR → OTP → Compliance CSID → compliance suite → Production CSID → set active environment) — Compliance CSID issued, both compliance-suite sample invoices genuinely `CLEARED`, Production CSID issued; created two B2B and two B2C real invoices — all four genuinely cleared/reported by ZATCA's real sandbox (`CLEARED` ×2, `REPORTED` ×2), each with a real signed XML, QR code, and UUID.
- Confirmed the mandatory-field gate live: a B2B customer missing VAT/address is rejected with the specific missing fields named; a B2C customer with just a name saves successfully.
- Confirmed the corrected VAT regex against a real ZATCA rejection and re-acceptance (not just unit-level regex testing).
- Dev server restarted after every server-side change before re-testing (no hot-reload in this project's `tsx server.ts` dev script). All scratch/debug scripts (`check_counts.ts`, `migrate_zatca_identity.ts`, `verify_schema.ts`, `debug_invoice_insert.ts`, `check_audit.ts`, `check_audit2.ts`) deleted after use; the test company/invoices created during verification were left in place as evidence rather than deleted.

## 30. ZATCA Key/Certificate Mismatch — Real Sandbox Defect Found, Guarded Against (Jul 2026)

Follow-up to item 29, triggered by the project owner asking for the exact signature failure on a specific invoice (INV-1004, "ZATCA Test Traders Co") to be diagnosed against the real SDK, not assumed fixed from item 28's earlier work.

### Investigation
The SDK's own local `-validate` command reported `[SIGNATURE] FAILED — wrong signature Value` on a real, already-`REPORTED`-by-sandbox invoice. Two hypotheses were tested and ruled out with hard evidence before the real cause was found:
1. **Suspected the main invoice-hash canonicalization** (`hashChain.ts`'s `computeInvoiceHash`, using `xmldsigjs`'s C14N 1.0 vs. the document's declared C14N 1.1) — decompiled the SDK's real `HashingGenerationServiceImpl` (from item 28's earlier CFR decompilation work), found its exact algorithm (an XSLT strip via Saxon, then genuine Apache Santuario C14N 1.1, then SHA-256), reproduced it independently in a standalone Java program using the SDK's own bundled dependencies, and diffed the resulting canonical XML string against this project's TypeScript output for the same document. **Byte-for-byte identical.** This hypothesis was wrong — the digest computation was never broken.
2. **Root cause, confirmed empirically**: the stored ECDSA private key for this company's sandbox environment did not correspond to the public key embedded in its Production CSID certificate — a genuine key/cert mismatch, verified directly via Node's `crypto.X509Certificate` API (`certPublicKeyDer.equals(derivedPublicKeyDer)` → `false`, and a real sign-then-verify round trip using the stored key+cert → `false`). The Compliance CSID certificate, by contrast, matched correctly.
3. **Isolated further**: generated a brand-new keypair, fresh CSR, fresh Compliance CSID (verified matching), fresh passing compliance suite — and requested a fresh Production CSID. **ZATCA's sandbox returned the exact same mismatched certificate again** (identical serial `1100003803C5F74023B3FC5C5F000100003803`, identical public key, subject `CN=TST-886431145-399999999900003, O=Maximum Speed Tech Supply LTD` — not this project's company at all). Reproduced a third time via the newly-added guard's audit log capture, byte-for-byte identical to the first occurrence.
4. **Conclusion**: ZATCA's sandbox `/production/csids` endpoint deterministically returns a fixed, canned certificate for the shared public sandbox test taxpayer identity (VAT `399999999900003` / CR `886431145`, ZATCA's own documented test values), independent of the CSR or key actually submitted. This is a sandbox-side behavior tied to the shared identity, not a defect in this project's cryptography, CSR construction, hashing, or signing code — all of which were independently re-verified as correct during this investigation. It should not recur for Simulation/Production, where each company submits its own real, unique VAT.

### What changed
- **`server/lib/zatca/x509.ts`**: new `certificateMatchesPrivateKey(binarySecurityToken, privateKeyPem)` — decodes a CSID response certificate and compares its SPKI public key against the one derivable from the stored private key.
- **`server/routes/zatca.ts`**: both `request-compliance-csid` and `request-production-csid` now call this check immediately after receiving ZATCA's response and *before* storing anything. A mismatch is a hard failure (HTTP 502, `zatcaStep` tagged `...:keyMismatch`) — the certificate is never stored, and `isOnboarded` is never set to `true` on a broken pairing. Both mismatch errors carry the actual rejected certificate on `err.zatcaResponseBody` so it lands in `auditLogs` (via the existing `logZatcaApiError` from item 29) for future diagnosis without needing to manually decode anything — a gap noticed and fixed mid-investigation, after the first version of this guard didn't preserve the certificate for its own audit log entry.
- Cleared "ZATCA Test Traders Co" sandbox's previously-stored mismatched Production CSID (`productionCsidCert`/`productionCsidSecret` → `null`, `isOnboarded` → `false`) — it had been accepted before this guard existed. The environment now honestly reports `NOT_SUBMITTED` for real invoices via the existing item-29 guard, instead of the false `CLEARED`/`REPORTED` it was silently producing before.

### Verification performed
- Independent Java reproduction of ZATCA's real hash algorithm, verified against both the SDK's own `-generateHash` CLI output and this project's TypeScript output — exact match, ruling out the canonicalization hypothesis with evidence rather than assumption.
- Key/certificate correspondence checked directly via Node's `crypto` module (public key DER comparison + a real sign/verify round trip), not inferred from `-validate` output alone.
- Mismatch reproduced three independent times (original onboarding, immediate retry, full fresh-keypair-and-CSID cycle) — all three returned byte-identical certificates, confirming determinism rather than flakiness.
- Confirmed the new guard rejects the mismatch live (HTTP 502, no DB write) rather than silently accepting it, on all three reproduction attempts.
- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after every change; dev server restarted before each re-test.
- All scratch/debug scripts and Java artifacts (`HashRepro.java`/`.class`, `xslt-output.xml`, `canonical-output.xml`, `our-canonical-output.xml`, `check_keypair_match.ts`, `check_keypair_history.ts`, `check_cert_identity.ts`, `dump_our_canonical.ts`, `fix_bad_prod_csid.ts`) deleted after use; the SDK's `Data/Input/` folder restored to its original contents.

## 31. Comprehensive Sandbox QA Pass — Two Real Concurrency/Data Bugs Found & Fixed (Jul 2026)

Full QA plan at `docs/zatca/sandbox-qa-test-plan.html` (approved by the project owner before execution, results recorded in its §12). Covers the comprehensive B2B/B2C invoice matrix, multi-company onboarding regression (3 companies, one with a deliberately different CR number), and onboarding UX guard checks, run under the same dual-gate protocol (real ZATCA sandbox API + the SDK's independent local validator) established in items 27-30.

### Per-line tax slab was decorative — fixed
The invoice line-item tax-slab dropdown existed in the UI (`InvoiceModule.tsx`) and was collected client-side, but the server discarded it entirely and re-taxed every line at one shared header rate — confirmed live on a genuine mixed-rate invoice (2 lines at 15%, 1 exempt at 0%) before the fix, where the header-level `cac:TaxTotal` only ever reported the first line's rate. Fixed: added `invoice_items.tax_slab_id` (nullable, falls back to the invoice's header slab), per-line rate resolution server-side (`processInvoice.ts`, `transactions.ts`'s `POST /invoices`), and real multi-`TaxSubtotal` grouping in `xmlBuilder.ts` keyed by `(category code, rate)` instead of one hardcoded block. Verified live: two distinct `cac:TaxSubtotal` blocks now emitted correctly, ZATCA's real sandbox cleared it. Surfaced one new legitimate warning, `BR-KSA-23` (VAT exemption reason code) — tracked below, not blocking.

### ICV/PIH hash-chain race condition — a real concurrency bug, found and fixed
Discovered while testing a 20+ line-item invoice case: two invoices for the same company, created back-to-back, both landed on the identical `icv: 7` with an identical `previousInvoiceHash` — a genuine fork in ZATCA's mandatory sequential hash chain. Root cause: `getNextHashChainState` (`hashChain.ts`) had no row lock, and was called from `processInvoiceZatca`, a fire-and-forget background job invoked via `.catch()`, never `await`ed, from the invoice-creation route (`transactions.ts`). Two such jobs starting within milliseconds of each other could both read "the last invoice is X" before either had written its own result back.

**Not a hash-content problem** — worth stating explicitly since it was the first, reasonable hypothesis raised: the hash *values* were already unique per invoice; the bug was a sequencing race in *claiming the next chain position*, not a collision in what got hashed. Mixing additional data (e.g. company id) into the hash formula would not have fixed it.

Fixed by extending the exact row-lock pattern item 22 already proved for the invoice-number counter (`getAndIncrementCounter`'s `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`) to the ICV/PIH reservation: `processInvoiceZatca` now opens a transaction, locks the company row, resolves the next ICV/PIH (`getNextHashChainState` extended to accept a transaction executor), builds and signs the XML, and durably persists `icv`/`previousInvoiceHash`/`currentInvoiceHash`/`xmlContent` — all before releasing the lock. The slow real ZATCA network call happens *after* the lock releases (deliberately outside it — signing is fast/CPU-only, the network round-trip is not, and holding a row lock across it would serialize every invoice for that company on ZATCA's response time for no reason). Re-verified live: two invoices fired at the exact same instant now get sequential ICVs with an exactly-chained hash (invoice 2's PIH byte-identical to invoice 1's current hash), both cleared by the real sandbox.

**SaaS-scale note (asked directly by the project owner)**: the lock is scoped to a single company's row, so it only ever serializes invoices *within the same company* created within milliseconds of each other — different tenants never contend, and thousands of companies can create invoices fully in parallel. Some serialization within one company is unavoidable: ZATCA's own hash-chain spec requires strict sequential ordering, a compliance constraint no implementation can parallelize away. The lock uses standard Postgres `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` via the `pg` driver's real TCP connection pool — not tied to any specific host; it works identically on any real Postgres instance (Hostinger, Aiven, a VPS, etc.), the only thing that would break it is switching to an HTTP-only "driverless" Postgres client, which this project doesn't use.

### Corrupted genesis PIH constant — a second, independent real bug
While investigating an earlier, separate question about a `[PIH] FAILED` result from the SDK's `-validate` command, found that `INITIAL_PREVIOUS_INVOICE_HASH` (`hashChain.ts`) — the well-known constant every chain's first invoice must carry as its previous-hash — was itself wrong. It decoded to a truncated, garbled string (`5feceb66ffc86a38`48`) instead of the genuine `SHA256("0")` hex digest, independently verified two ways: Node's `crypto` module and `openssl dgst -sha256` both computed `5feceb66ffc86f38d952786c6d696c79c2dbc239dd4e91b46729d73a27fb57e9`. Every chain's genesis invoice was therefore carrying a wrong PIH — exactly why the SDK's `-validate` reported `[PIH] FAILED` even for a chain's very first invoice, where ZATCA's real sandbox API stayed silent because it doesn't audit the genesis link's exact value on submission. A second hardcoded copy of the identical bad literal was found in `zatca.ts`'s `run-compliance-suite` test-sample route and fixed too (now imports the corrected constant instead of duplicating the literal).

Also clarified, as a byproduct of this investigation: `[PIH] FAILED` on any *non-genesis* invoice (`icv > 1`) validated alone via `-validate` is a genuine, permanent tool limitation, not a defect — confirmed by checking `fatoora -help`, which has no flag to supply a referenced predecessor XML for cross-check, so the SDK cannot verify chain continuity for a single isolated file. This is expected and should continue to be ignored per the QA plan's triage rules (§09).

Fixed and verified two independent ways: (1) a standalone genesis invoice built with the corrected constant now gets `[PIH] PASSED` from the SDK, and its hash matches the SDK's own independent `-generateHash` output byte-for-byte; (2) Company 3, onboarded fully fresh through this pass, had its real first invoice (`icv: 1`) reach a clean `*** GLOBAL VALIDATION RESULT = PASSED` across every single SDK check.

### Multi-company onboarding regression — confirmed
Company 2 (partial prior onboarding, completed this pass) and Company 3 (fully fresh, deliberately different CR number `1010299999` vs. `886431145` used elsewhere) both reached full onboarding via the automatic sandbox sample-key adoption (item 30) with zero manual cryptographic steps, matching Company 1 exactly. This resolves item 30's open question about whether ZATCA's static sandbox certificate behavior is keyed on VAT alone or VAT+CR together: Company 3's different CR made no difference — the same known static certificate came back regardless, confirming it's keyed on the shared VAT alone.

### Onboarding UX guards — confirmed live
- **Environment-activation guard**: attempting to activate `production` for a company onboarded only in sandbox is correctly rejected (HTTP 400, specific message) — no path exists to activate a non-onboarded environment for live processing.
- **Invalid-VAT-blocks-CSR guard**: a malformed TIN can be saved as a draft, but CSR generation — the actual point of no return before an OTP gets spent — correctly blocks with a precise field error first.
- **Mandatory-field gate**, tested at both layers independently: `POST /customers` rejects a new incomplete B2B customer outright (HTTP 400), and `processInvoiceZatca`'s final gate independently catches a simulated legacy customer record (inserted directly, bypassing the save-time check) — `NOT_SUBMITTED`/`BUYER_INCOMPLETE`, zero ZATCA API calls made, confirming the gate holds even for data that predates the validation.

### Verification performed
- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after every change.
- Full dual-gate protocol (real ZATCA sandbox API + SDK's independent `-validate`/`-generateHash`) run against every matrix case this pass — see the QA plan's §12 for the per-case table.
- Concurrency fix specifically verified by firing two real invoice-creation HTTP requests at the exact same instant (not sequentially) through the actual API, then confirming the persisted `icv`/`previousInvoiceHash`/`currentInvoiceHash` chain directly via SQL — done both before the fix (reproducing the fork) and after (confirming sequential, correctly-chained values).
- All scratch/debug scripts created during this pass deleted after use; the SDK's `Data/Input/` folder restored to empty after each validation batch.

### Known gap carried forward (not fixed this pass, tracked below)
The Browser pane did not composite frames during this session (`document.hidden` was `true` on the active tab throughout), which stalls Framer Motion's `AnimatePresence` exit transition and blocked visually opening the Sales Invoices list/print-preview UI. Every invoice's `zatcaStatus`/`invoiceTypeCode` was independently confirmed correct at the database level instead. This is an environment/tooling limitation encountered during this session, not a reproduced defect in the app — but the visual List UI path itself remains unverified and should be manually clicked through before full sign-off.

## 32. Multi-Track Push — Credit/Debit Notes, App-Wide Correctness Sweep, First Visual Pass (Jul 2026)

Four parallel review passes (POS/Inventory, Expenses/Recurring, Master Data/Reports/Dashboard, Admin Settings/ZATCA wizard/Login) covering every remaining module surfaced roughly 40 real, evidenced findings — ~13 Critical. The project owner directed a full push to fix every Critical regardless of module, ship both Credit and Debit Note, and do correctness fixes plus a first visual pass on Sales/Print, working as parallel engineering "workstreams" (general-purpose agents briefed as the requested roles) with an explicit protected-files boundary around the proven ZATCA core (`hashChain.ts`, `processInvoice.ts`, `xades.ts`, `x509.ts`, `crypto.ts`, `asn1.ts`, `apiClient.ts`, `sandboxSampleIdentity.ts`) so no parallel edit could regress this session's already-verified fixes.

### Foundation fix — one root cause behind most of the "silent failure" Criticals
`App.tsx`'s `handleUpdateDb` writes to React state immediately and fires `/api/migrate` in the background; on failure it set `cloudError`, **which was never rendered anywhere** (confirmed by a full-tree grep — the only match was its own declaration), independently rediscovered by 3 of the 4 review agents. Fixed by rendering `cloudError` as a persistent, app-wide banner in `App.tsx` (visible on every tab, not just one module). This single fix immediately started surfacing real, previously-invisible failures live during this session's own testing (see below).

Also fixed in the same pass, all following the identical pattern (client never checked `res.ok`/awaited the real request before claiming success):
- **Expense save could null out the entire app's `db` state** (`ExpenseModule.tsx`): called `onUpdateDb(result.db)` where `result.db` never existed in the real `POST /api/expenses` response (server only returns `{success:true}`/`{error}`), so `onUpdateDb(undefined)` ran on every successful save. Fixed to stop passing a delta the endpoint never returns; added a submit guard and try/catch (previously absent entirely).
- **Users create/update/delete** (`AdminSettings.tsx`): none checked the response status; delete was genuinely fire-and-forget (`.catch(console.error)`, never awaited). Fixed to check `res.ok` and surface the server's real error; delete now awaits and only proceeds on confirmed success.
- **Fiscal Month open/close**: same gap, worse in effect — the UI explicitly claims a closed month is "permanently closed and locked," so a silently-rejected close previously left the admin believing the books were locked when the server may never have recorded it. Fixed.
- **Customer/Vendor save** (`MasterEntities.tsx`): the server re-validates ZATCA mandatory fields authoritatively; a rejection was previously swallowed and the form cleared/navigated away as if the save succeeded. Fixed.
- **"Force Publish Local to Cloud" button was a literal no-op** — no fetch call at all, just an unconditional success toast. Fixed to genuinely POST the current `db` to `/api/migrate` and only report success on a real 2xx.
- **Category list always showed "Unmapped"/"-- Top Level --"** regardless of real saved data — a field-name mismatch (`c.parentId`/`c.salesGlAccount`/etc. don't exist; real fields are `parentCategoryId`/`salesGlGroup`/etc.). Fixed.

### Dashboard/Reports correctness
- **Dashboard's "Audit Compliance Logs" panel was entirely fabricated** — three hardcoded, always-green claims ("VAT records fully synchronised," "Interbank ledger transfer routes active," "Capital Investor Registry active") with zero backing computation. Fixed: the ZATCA/VAT line now genuinely counts invoices with `zatcaStatus` of `ERROR`/`REJECTED` in the active period and only shows green when that count is zero; the fabricated bank-sync claim was removed outright (no real signal exists for it); the investor-registry line now reuses the real contributed-vs-target reconciliation math already used elsewhere on the same page.
- **Hardcoded fake performance targets** (`quotaTarget = 5`, `salesTarget = 2500` for every tenant) now derive from each company's own trailing 3-closed-month average, falling back to the old placeholder only when a company has no closed-month history yet, with a caption disclosing which mode is active.
- **`ReportViewer.tsx`'s expense payment-status badge was hardcoded rose regardless of actual status**, hiding partially-paid expenses next to an invoice column that correctly distinguished them. Fixed to match the invoice row's existing conditional logic.
- Reports/Dashboard consuming the 500-row-capped list endpoints now show an explicit disclosure banner instead of silently implying the totals are complete (full server-side aggregation is a bigger job, tracked below).

### Purchase/Inventory correctness
- **Hardcoded `"SAR"` currency literal** (~15 occurrences) replaced with the company's real configured currency, matching the pattern `PosModule.tsx` already used.
- **PR/PO/GRN numbering used `array.length + 1001`** — the exact same duplicate-number race already found and fixed for ZATCA invoicing this session. Fixed properly: new `server/routes/inventory.ts` with three transactional routes using `getAndIncrementCounter` (the same `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` row-locked counter, extended with `pr`/`po`/`grn` types).
- **GRN unconditionally marked its linked PO as fully "Received"** even for a partial delivery. Fixed: PO status is now computed from real cumulative received-vs-ordered quantities (`Received`/`Partially Received`), and the GRN PO-picker now also offers partially-received POs, prefilling only the outstanding quantity.
- **~25 invalid Tailwind classes** (`indigo-650`, `gray-750`, `bg-gray-55`, etc. — Tailwind only defines steps of 50 apart) were silently dropping all their styling. Fixed to the nearest valid shade.
- **PO/GRN disagreed on default VAT rate** (17% vs. 15%, and 17% doesn't match Saudi's real 15% standard rate). Unified both to derive from the real `taxSlabs` data.
- Every `onUpdateDb` call converted to the functional-updater form (was using the stale-closure object form 100% of the time, a lost-update risk).

### Sales & Print correctness + first visual pass
- **The QR "VERIFIED ORIGINAL" lie** (`DocumentRenderer.tsx`): a printed invoice with no real ZATCA QR payload yet still displayed a hardcoded verification claim regardless of the invoice's actual `zatcaStatus`. Fixed to only claim verified when `zatcaStatus` is genuinely `CLEARED`/`REPORTED`; every other state shows an honest, distinctly-styled placeholder. Confirmed live against a real invoice.
- **Invoice save showed success and opened print preview before the real save request resolved** (`InvoiceModule.tsx`) — brought up to the same await-then-confirm standard `QuotationModule.tsx` already used, with a loading state on the submit button and a background poll that surfaces a real ZATCA rejection as a toast instead of only being discoverable by reopening the invoice later. Same visibility fix applied to `QuotationModule.tsx`'s conversion path.
- Built one shared `src/components/StatusPill.tsx` (five semantic tones: good/warn/critical/info/neutral) and migrated Invoice list, Quotation list, and POS Sales History onto it, replacing each screen's own ad-hoc badge classes — confirmed live rendering cleanly.
- **POS cash-drawer variance was mathematically guaranteed to always be zero** (`expectedCash` was set equal to the counted `endCash`) — a real fraud/shortage-detection control that could never detect anything. Fixed: `expectedCash` now derives from starting float + the shift's real recorded sales.
- **POS checkout total excluded VAT while the saved invoice included it** — the cashier quoted/collected a pre-tax figure for the same sale the system recorded tax-inclusive. Fixed: cart/pay-modal now compute and display the same tax-inclusive total via `calculateInvoiceTotals`, with VAT broken out explicitly.
- **Received Amount was never validated** — any amount (including blank) was accepted and the full total recorded as paid regardless. Fixed with a real `received >= total` guard, confirmed live (blocked an under-payment, then succeeded on a sufficient one).
- **POS Sales History status badge was hardcoded green** regardless of Cancelled status. Fixed to be conditional, migrated to `StatusPill`.

### Bugs found only by live testing after the fixes above (not part of any agent's original brief)
Live verification of the POS VAT fix surfaced two further, more serious, previously-invisible bugs — exactly the value of testing beyond static code review:
- **POS shift/hold IDs used a legacy `` `shift-${Date.now()}` ``/`` `hold-${Date.now()}` `` string format, not a real UUID** — `pos_shifts.id`/`pos_held_invoices.id` are native Postgres `uuid` columns (item 25's migration), so every new shift or held sale failed to persist with `invalid input syntax for type uuid`, invisible until the Foundation fix's `cloudError` banner made it visible. Fixed both call sites to use `generateId()`; reproduced the failure, applied the fix, and reproduced success (a real UUID row now persists) via direct DB checks.
- **POS always taxed at `db.taxSlabs[0]`, whichever slab happened to be first in the array** — for one real test company that's "Exempt (0%)", meaning **every POS sale was silently recording zero VAT** regardless of the item, not merely displaying the wrong total. Fixed to prefer the company's own standard-rate slab, then the shared 15% standard slab, falling back to the old behavior only if neither exists. Confirmed live: the same test sale went from Total = Subtotal (no tax) to a correct 15%-inclusive total, and the saved invoice's `taxSlabId` now resolves to the real VAT slab.

### ZATCA integration — urgent security fix
**A ZATCA onboarding remount could submit the literal string `'MOCK_CSR_DATA'` to the real ZATCA gateway**, burning a genuine, single-use, ~1-hour OTP on a guaranteed failure (`ZatcaOnboardingWizard.tsx`'s `generatedCsr` is transient component state that doesn't survive a remount, but the wizard could still land on Step 3). Fixed with a hard client-side guard: Step 3 now refuses to submit at all if no real CSR is loaded in the current session, with a clear message directing the user back to Step 2 — closed before any real Simulation/Production OTP gets spent against this bug.

### Credit Note / Debit Note — real, dual-gate-verified, end to end
Built as a variant of the already-proven Invoice XML/signing/clearance pipeline, not from scratch, and verified against the ZATCA SDK's own bundled sample Credit/Debit Note XMLs (`Data/Samples/Simplified/Credit/Simplified_Credit_Note.xml`, `.../Debit/Simplified_Debit_Note.xml`) as ground truth before writing any code — confirmed: same `<Invoice>` UBL root and schema for all three document types; Credit Note = type code `381`, Debit Note = `383`; amounts stay **positive** (ZATCA distinguishes intent via the type code and a `<cac:BillingReference>` to the original invoice, not sign); both carry a `<cac:PaymentMeans><cbc:PaymentMeansCode>10</cbc:PaymentMeansCode>` block with an instruction note.

- **Schema**: `invoices` gained `documentType` ('Invoice'|'CreditNote'|'DebitNote'), `originalInvoiceId` (self-referencing FK), `creditNoteReason` — reuses the exact same table/counter/hash-chain/print pipeline as regular invoices, since Credit/Debit Notes must participate in the SAME per-company ICV/PIH chain (confirmed via the SDK samples, which embed real ICV/PIH `AdditionalDocumentReference`s), not a separate one.
- **`xmlBuilder.ts`** (protected file, edited only by the architect role per this session's boundary): added `documentSubtypeCode`, `billingReference`, `paymentMeansNote` to `ZatcaInvoiceData`, defaulting to plain-Invoice behavior for every existing caller.
- **`processInvoice.ts`** (same protection): reads the row's own `documentType`/`originalInvoiceId`/`creditNoteReason` and passes the right fields through — still flows through the exact same locked ICV/PIH reservation transaction as every invoice, so the concurrency fix from this session's earlier work covers Credit/Debit Notes too, not just invoices.
- **New route**: `POST /api/transactions/invoices/:id/note` — MVP scope is full-document reversal only (mirrors every line from the original invoice verbatim; a partial/line-selectable credit note is a larger feature, tracked below). No automatic refund/reversal voucher generated yet (the accounting treatment of the credit is separately deferred).
- **Minimal UI**: a "Note" action on each invoice list row (only shown for `documentType === 'Invoice'`, preventing crediting a note itself) opens a small modal to pick Credit/Debit and enter a reason.
- **Verified twice, independently**: once via direct API call (Credit Note against INV-1001, Debit Note against INV-1002 — both real ZATCA sandbox `CLEARED`, zero errors, SDK `XSD`/`EN`/`KSA`/`QR`/`SIGNATURE` all `PASSED`), and again through the actual UI end-to-end (the guard correctly refused an itemless POS-originated invoice with a 400, then correctly created and cleared a Credit Note through the real form against a normal invoice — real ZATCA `REPORTED`, zero errors, SDK all green except the known non-genesis `[PIH]` limitation already established this session).

### A significant gap found, deliberately left out of scope (explicit project-owner instruction)
**POS-originated invoices never reach the real ZATCA pipeline at all.** `PosModule.tsx`'s `handlePayInvoice` still uses the legacy client-side `saveInvoice()` (`dbStore.ts`) + generic `/api/migrate` blob sync, not the dedicated `/api/transactions/invoices` route every other invoice-creation path now uses. Confirmed live: a completed POS sale had `invoice_items` = **empty** (items only exist inside the synced JSON blob, not the normalized table `processInvoiceZatca` reads from) and `zatcaStatus` stuck at `NOT_SUBMITTED` forever — no XML, no signing, no submission, ever, for any POS sale. This means every one of this session's ZATCA verifications only ever covered invoices created via the direct Invoice/Quotation paths, never POS. Migrating POS to the dedicated route (mirroring `InvoiceModule.tsx`'s now-fixed save handler) would close this in one move and reuse everything already proven — but the project owner explicitly directed this be kept out of today's scope. **This should be treated as a near-term priority** given POS is a live, real-money sales channel with zero ZATCA compliance currently.

### Verification performed
- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after every change, across every workstream.
- Dual-gate protocol (real ZATCA sandbox + SDK `-validate`) run against the Credit Note, Debit Note, and the UI-driven Credit Note flow — all clean.
- Live browser verification (this session's earlier Browser-pane compositing issue was NOT present this time — `document.hidden` was `false`): confirmed the QR fix, StatusPill badges, print-preview logo, POS VAT/total fix, POS Received Amount validation (blocked then succeeded), and the Dashboard/Foundation fixes all render and behave correctly in the live app, not just in code.
- The POS shift/hold UUID bug and the POS tax-slab-index-0 bug were both reproduced live (confirmed failing/wrong), fixed, and re-verified live (confirmed persisting/correct) via direct SQL checks — not assumed from reading the diff alone.
- All scratch/debug scripts deleted after use; SDK `Data/Input/` folder restored to empty after each validation batch.

### Explicitly out of scope for this pass (tracked below, not silently dropped)
POS→ZATCA pipeline migration (see above, deliberately deferred by the project owner); full app-wide `StatusPill`/`DataTable`/`Pagination` rollout to every remaining module; Master Entities search/pagination; Recurring Expenses' missing dedicated persistence endpoints beyond the Foundation `cloudError` fix; full server-side financial-report aggregation (disclosure banner shipped instead); Credit/Debit Note partial-line selection and automatic refund/reversal voucher generation; a full security audit beyond the ZATCA OTP-guard and Users-CRUD fixes.

## 33. Print Template ZATCA Compliance Fixes, Credit/Debit Note Discoverability, Tooling Portability (Jul 2026)

Follow-up to item 32, prompted by the project owner asking for a genuine code-level review of the print template against ZATCA's real requirements (not just the QR-lie fix already shipped), a direct QR-content test, and a fix for Credit/Debit Notes being effectively invisible in the UI after item 32 shipped them.

### Print template — real gaps found on direct code review, not assumed
- **Fake seller VAT fallback**: `DocumentRenderer.tsx` fell back to a hardcoded, fabricated VAT number (`"300123456700003"`) in the unsigned-invoice QR placeholder text when a company had none configured — the exact same class of bug already fixed for buyer VAT (item 29). Fixed to say "Not Configured" instead of inventing a real-looking number.
- **Buyer VAT number was never shown on the printed document** — ZATCA's own data dictionary marks this Mandatory for B2B invoices (confirmed against the dictionary in item 31); it was correctly in the XML all along but invisible on the human-readable print. Fixed.
- **No per-line VAT rate column** in the items table — invisible which rate applied to which line, a real gap now that mixed-rate invoices are a genuine, working, tested scenario (item 31's per-line-tax fix). Fixed: a `getItemTaxRate()` helper (mirrors `calculateInvoiceTotals`/`xmlBuilder.ts`'s exact resolution order — line override → line's own tax slab → header rate) now drives a new "VAT %" column.
- **Totals only ever showed one blended "VAT (X%)" line using the header rate** — for a mixed-rate invoice this doesn't match what ZATCA's XML actually reports (a separate `TaxSubtotal` per distinct rate, item 31). Fixed: the totals summary now groups by rate the same way `xmlBuilder.ts` does and shows one line per rate when more than one is present, falling back to the original single-line display otherwise.
- **The ZATCA QR block had no gate at all** — it rendered unconditionally on **Quotations**, showing a fake "ZATCA QR Verification... Not Yet Cleared" badge on a document type ZATCA has no concept of whatsoever (quotations are never submitted). Fixed: `qr_code` block now only renders when `isInvoice` is true. (Caught directly by the project owner reviewing the live app, not by code inspection — a good reminder that some gaps only show up when someone actually looks at the thing.)
- Quotation and Invoice share the same `renderQuotationOrInvoice` function, so the buyer-VAT, per-line-rate, and multi-rate-breakdown fixes above apply to both document types for free — no separate Quotation-specific work needed.

### QR code — tested by direct TLV byte-decode, not re-trusted from earlier claims
Wrote a standalone decoder (tag/length/value parser) and ran it against a real, recently-cleared invoice's actual `qrCodeContent`. Confirmed: seller name and 15-digit VAT (format `^3\d{13}3$` per `BR-KSA-44`) both correct; timestamp `yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mm:ss` with no trailing `Z` (confirms item 28's fix holds for current invoices); real non-zero total/VAT amounts; a genuine 96-byte ASN.1 DER ECDSA signature; a correct 88-byte SPKI-DER public key (verified DER prefix for an EC key); and tag 9 (CA signature over the cert) correctly absent for this Standard/B2B document (only required for Simplified/B2C, per item 28's SDK-verified rule). One early test run against `INV-1001` — one of the very first test invoices from early in this session, before the real signing pipeline existed — showed a mock signature and zero amounts; re-confirmed this was stale historical data, not a current defect, by re-running against a freshly-created, properly-signed document.

### Credit/Debit Note discoverability — real gap, not a permissions issue
The project owner, logged in as Super Admin (which bypasses every permission check entirely — confirmed this was never an RBAC bug), couldn't find any way to see which invoices were Credit/Debit Notes, or a clear way to create one. Item 32 had only added a small, ambiguously-labeled "Note" button per invoice row with no visual distinction for existing notes. Fixed in `InvoiceModule.tsx`:
- New **Type** column on every Sales Invoices list row — an `Invoice`/`Credit Note`/`Debit Note` badge (via the shared `StatusPill`), with a small "Ref: INV-XXXX" line under Credit/Debit Notes linking back to the original invoice.
- New **Document Type filter** dropdown (All / Invoices Only / Credit Notes Only / Debit Notes Only) alongside the existing ZATCA-status filter.
- Renamed the row action button from the vague "Note" to **"Credit/Debit"**.

### Tooling portability
The project owner wanted to switch Claude accounts due to a usage-limit issue and asked whether the codebase/docs would carry over. Confirmed: the codebase, git history, `BACKLOG.md`, and the QA docs are all plain files on disk, independent of any account. The one real gap — the ZATCA SDK was sitting in a session-scoped temp scratchpad directory that wouldn't exist for a new session — was closed by copying it into the repo itself at `tools/zatca-sdk/`, verified working from its new location (`fatoora -help` re-run successfully against the new path). Not yet committed to git (30MB of third-party binary/redistributable content) — flagged to the project owner, will commit if wanted for extra durability (e.g. surviving a repo clone elsewhere).

### Verification performed
- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after every change; dev server boots with zero errors after each restart.
- QR content verified via an independent, standalone TLV decoder script (not re-trusting the SDK's own `[QR] PASSED` verdict alone, though that also held).
- Live browser click-through verification was blocked this pass by the same page-visibility/frame-compositing issue noted in item 31 (confirmed again: `document.hidden` stayed `true`, and a `requestAnimationFrame`→`setTimeout` monkey-patch attempt to force it did not help — this is a browser-engine-level throttle tied to real page visibility, not something JS can override). Verified instead via direct inspection of the actual served dev-server bundle (confirmed the compiled output contains the new Type column, filter state, and button label reaching the browser) and direct DB checks (`CN-1001`/`DN-1001` rows exist with correct `documentType`). Code-level confidence is high; a manual click-through is still recommended once the pane is genuinely visible.

### Explicitly out of scope for this pass
Committing the SDK to git (flagged, awaiting project-owner preference); a dedicated top-level "Credit & Debit Notes" sidebar section (current fix keeps them inside the existing Sales Invoices workspace, filterable/distinguishable — a separate section is a bigger UX decision, not made here).

## 34. ZATCA Simulation/Production Readiness — Consolidated Assessment (Jul 2026)

The project owner asked for a single, clear statement on whether Simulation and Production onboarding can be completed without any known code-level blocker. Consolidating everything proven across items 26-33 into one place, since that picture was previously scattered across many dated entries.

### Bottom line
**No known code issue blocks completing Simulation or Production onboarding.** Every piece of the pipeline that sandbox *can* prove has been proven, repeatedly, against ZATCA's real sandbox gateway — not assumed, not mocked. What's left for Simulation/Production is not a code fix; it's supplying the real, environment-specific inputs (registered company identity, a real OTP) that only a live business can provide, and walking the same onboarding flow already verified end-to-end.

### What is proven (high confidence — verified live, repeatedly, against real ZATCA infrastructure)
- **CSR construction**: byte-level ASN.1-correct PKCS#10 structure (item 27), accepted by ZATCA's real endpoint every time since.
- **XAdES-BES digital signing**: reverse-engineered from ZATCA's own official SDK (item 28), verified two independent ways (recomputing a genuine sample's digest from scratch, and the SDK's own `-validate` reporting `[SIGNATURE] PASSED`), confirmed for both Standard and Simplified invoices.
- **Hash-chain integrity**: the canonicalization algorithm matches ZATCA's own (item 30, independently reproduced in Java against the SDK's bundled libraries); the genesis PIH constant was corrupted and is now fixed and verified (item 31); the ICV/PIH reservation is now safe under real concurrency (item 31, reproduced-then-fixed-then-reproduced-clean).
- **Per-environment isolation**: sandbox/simulation/production have always had fully independent credential and identity storage (`zatcaEnvironmentConfigs`, item 26) — onboarding one environment cannot touch another's data, verified across 3 companies onboarded independently this session (item 31).
- **Mandatory-field enforcement**: checked directly against ZATCA's own published data dictionary (item 29/31), enforced at both save-time and invoice-time, verified live at both layers.
- **The onboarding UX flow itself** — identity → CSR → OTP → Compliance CSID → compliance suite → Production CSID → environment activation — is the exact same flow that will be used for Simulation/Production, proven working end-to-end on 3 separate companies (item 31), including the environment-activation guard (can't activate a non-onboarded environment) and the invalid-VAT-blocks-CSR guard (item 31), plus this session's fix closing a real risk of burning a live OTP on a placeholder CSR (item 32).
- **Document coverage**: Standard (B2B) and Simplified (B2C) invoices, single/multi-line, discounts, mixed tax rates, 20+ line items, extreme amounts, **and now Credit Notes and Debit Notes** (item 32) — all dual-gate verified. Credit/Debit Note was the one document-type gap flagged in earlier readiness discussions; it no longer exists.
- **The real ZATCA clearance/reporting network calls themselves**: hundreds of real round-trips to `gw-fatoora.zatca.gov.sa` across this session, never mocked for sandbox.

### What can only be verified with real data (not a blocker — an inherent fact of moving environments, true for any ZATCA integration)
- **A genuine cert/key match under a real, unique VAT.** Sandbox's shared public test VAT (`399999999900003`) makes ZATCA return a canned certificate regardless of the CSR submitted (item 30's root-caused finding) — this is precisely *why* sandbox can't prove this specific step. The code path is ready and will hard-fail loudly (not silently) if a real mismatch ever occurs (the same guard that caught the sandbox anomaly, item 30), but the *first real success* here can only happen with Simulation/Production's real registered identity.
- **The real OTP retrieval-and-entry flow** — a human logging into ZATCA's actual Fatoora portal, copying a genuine OTP, pasting it into the wizard within its ~1-hour validity. Sandbox always accepts the documented placeholder `123456`; this exact human workflow has never been rehearsed end-to-end in this project, though the wizard's Step 3 guard (item 32) now specifically protects this OTP from being wasted on a stale/placeholder CSR.
- **Real company registration data** — format rules are enforced and checked against ZATCA's own dictionary, but no genuine registered TIN/CR/address has passed through yet. Real-world data quirks (exact address-field formatting ZATCA expects, Arabic name encoding, etc.) are the kind of thing that only surfaces with the real thing.
- **The actual Simulation (`.../simulation`) and Production (`.../core`) network endpoints** — correctly targeted in code (verified by reading `ZatcaApiClient`), never actually called; per the project owner's explicit standing instruction, sandbox-only until real data is available.

### Known, unrelated functional gaps that do NOT block Simulation/Production onboarding
These are real, tracked gaps (see the Deferred/Backlog list below) but none of them prevent an admin from completing the onboarding wizard itself:
- **POS sales don't reach the ZATCA pipeline at all** (item 32) — a real gap for POS-originated invoices specifically, unrelated to whether onboarding itself succeeds. Once Simulation/Production is onboarded, POS sales will still need this separate fix before they're ZATCA-compliant.
- ~~**Credit/Debit Note is full-document-reversal only**, no automatic refund voucher~~ — the reversal-voucher half shipped in item 35; partial-line selection remains the one real gap (see Deferred list below).
- **`BR-KSA-23`/`BR-KSA-EN16931-11`/B2C-address-conditional** (item 31) — all warning-level findings observed in sandbox, none of them `errorMessages` that would block a real clearance.

### Recommended next step
Proceed to Simulation with the business's real registered VAT/CR/address and a real OTP from the Fatoora portal, through the exact same onboarding wizard already proven in sandbox. Treat it as "the first real test of the parts sandbox structurally cannot prove" rather than a fresh risk — everything provable without real data already has been, repeatedly.

## 35. ZATCA Enable/Disable Flag, Cancel Invoice Gating, Credit Note Reversal Voucher (Jul 2026)

The project owner raised three real, related gaps in a single design discussion: (1) every new company had ZATCA implicitly "on" from creation, with no way to keep a mid-setup or non-KSA company from silently attempting real submissions; (2) Cancel Invoice could be clicked on any invoice regardless of ZATCA status — since ZATCA has no void/delete API, cancelling a CLEARED/REPORTED invoice would leave a permanent ZATCA record with no Credit Note ever issued to reconcile it, and worse, cancelling could race an already-in-flight submission (fire-and-forget after invoice save) since the invoice sat at a stale `NOT_SUBMITTED` status for the entire async job; (3) a Credit Note structurally reverses an invoice but no reversal voucher was ever posted for the amount actually paid. Delegated as a 3-agent parallel push (isolated git worktrees, manually reconciled back into the main tree to avoid file-collision risk), following [[erp-backlog-tracking]]'s established multi-workstream pattern.

### Shipped
- **`companies.zatcaEnabled`** (boolean, default `false`): `processInvoiceZatca` now returns a `DISABLED` status and skips all submission when false — no schema/environment-config lookups even attempted. Auto-flips `true` the moment a company's **Sandbox** environment finishes onboarding (Production CSID issued); Simulation/Production auto-enable is an explicit TODO, since companies progress through environments one at a time, not simultaneously, per the project owner's explicit instruction. A Super-Admin manual toggle exists in Admin Settings for edge cases (pausing ZATCA during a data migration, etc).
- **A new `SUBMITTING` zatcaStatus**, written synchronously the instant real processing begins — before hash-chain reservation, before the network call — closing the exact race the project owner identified. Every terminal branch (`DISABLED`/`NOT_SUBMITTED`/`ERROR`/`CLEARED`/`REPORTED`/`REJECTED`) overwrites it, so an invoice can never get stuck there.
- **Cancel Invoice is now blocked server-side** once `zatcaStatus` is `SUBMITTING`, `CLEARED`, or `REPORTED` (400, pointing the user to Credit Note instead); still allowed for `NOT_SUBMITTED`/`DISABLED`/`ERROR`/`REJECTED`, where ZATCA never actually accepted the invoice. **Found live during this work**: the frontend Cancel button was never calling the real server route at all — it called a legacy client-only `dbStore.ts` function that mutated local state directly and relied on the generic `/api/migrate` blob sync, completely bypassing any server-side check. Modernized to call the real endpoint (now deleted from `dbStore.ts` as dead code) and to disable itself with an explanatory tooltip once ZATCA-blocked, rather than rendering a button that would just 400 on click.
- **Credit Note now posts a reversal** for the original invoice's paid amount when `amountPaid > 0`, reusing the exact same month-closed-aware pattern already proven for invoice cancellation (`syncVoucherForInvoice`'s reversal branch) rather than inventing new logic — deletes the open-month Receipt outright, or posts a dated Reversal voucher if the month is closed. Debit Notes never trigger this (they represent new unpaid charges, not a reversal).
- **Debit Note disabled in the UI** for now, per explicit project-owner instruction — the note-creation modal only offers Credit Note. Not removed from the schema/backend, just hidden from creation.
- **First real automated test coverage for this project**: `tests/zatcaWorkflow.test.ts` (vitest + real HTTP requests against the running dev server + real Postgres state, matching this project's established "test against real behavior, not mocks" practice) — 7 passing tests covering all three features above, using a dedicated throwaway company/user/customer/bank fixture torn down in `afterAll`, never touching the seeded demo companies. Runs via `npm test`. No test infrastructure existed on this project before this item.

### A real operational bug found while building the tests
The dev server (`npm run dev` → `tsx server.ts`) has **no watch/auto-restart** on backend file changes — only the Vite middleware's client-side HMR was reloading, which made every server-side edit in this item (and, in hindsight, everything server-side merged from the three parallel agents) sit completely inert against the already-running process. This was only caught because the first test run's actual server behavior contradicted the code being read directly. The server was restarted to pick up all pending changes, after which every test passed. **Not yet fixed at the tooling level** — recommend switching the `dev` script to `tsx watch server.ts` so this class of "verified against stale code" mistake can't recur silently; flagged here rather than changed unprompted since it's a dev-workflow change.

### Verification
`npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm test` — 7/7 passing against a live restarted dev server and real DB state. Manual browser click-through was attempted but blocked by the recurring `document.hidden` compositing issue (see Deferred list) — the automated HTTP-level tests are the real verification for this item, not a substitute chosen for convenience.

The project owner asked whether a complete look-and-feel redesign was already part of the plan. It is not — distinct from item 32/35's "component consistency rollout" (extending the already-designed `StatusPill`/`DataTable`/`Pagination` to every module, still tracked as deferred below), this is a genuine new-visual-language initiative: typography, color system, spacing, and layout conventions reconsidered app-wide, not just made consistent with what already exists on Sales/Print. Logged here as its own item rather than folded into the existing rollout entry, since the two are different sizes of work with different open questions.

**Not yet scoped.** Before any design or implementation work starts, this needs answers to: which modules/areas go first (a from-scratch pass across the entire ERP in one go is not realistic); whether there's a specific visual reference or direction in mind, or whether one should be proposed from scratch; and how deep the change goes — a cosmetic refresh of the current structure (colors/type/spacing) versus also reworking layout/information-architecture per module. Scoping conversation in progress.

## 37. Stop Non-Transactional Full-Blob Syncs to Postgres (Aug 2026)

The project owner flagged that switching which company a super-admin is viewing, changing the UI theme, and changing UI language were each triggering a full `POST /api/migrate` — the same generic call that re-upserts every table in the company (users, invoices, quotations, expenses, customers, everything), used elsewhere for genuine explicit Save/Delete transactions. None of these three are transactions: company-view switching changes nothing (a super-admin's `/api/state` already returns every company's data unfiltered, so it's a pure client-side view filter), and theme/language are each a single real column.

### Shipped
- **Company-view switcher** (both the sidebar and header super-admin selectors in `App.tsx`) now calls a plain `setDb` instead of `handleUpdateDb` — no server call at all.
- **Theme** and **UI language** each got a dedicated single-column route (`PATCH /api/companies/:id/theme`, `PATCH /api/users/me/language`) instead of going through the blob sync, wired via two new `App.tsx` handlers (`handleThemeChange`, `handleLanguageChange`) that update local state optimistically and surface failures through the existing `cloudError` banner.

### Not in scope for this item
This only touched the three non-transactional call sites in `App.tsx`. It did **not** touch the ~55 remaining `onUpdateDb(result.db)` call sites across `QuotationModule`/`InvoiceModule`/`ExpenseModule`/`PosModule`/`MasterEntities`/`AdminSettings`/`RecurringExpenses`/`InventoryModule` (e.g. `markExpensePaid`, `cancelExpense`) — those genuinely are explicit user transactions, just inefficiently persisted (still re-syncing every table for a one-record change). See the Deferred list below for why that's a separate, larger effort.

---

## 38. Phase 1 Migration Plan — Tiered Breakdown (Aug 2026)

Full inventory of all 65 remaining `onUpdateDb(...)` call sites across the 8 modules still touching the full-blob `/api/migrate` sync (item 37's follow-up), broken into three tiers by risk and effort so the migration can proceed as independently-shippable, independently-testable batches rather than one large diff.

### Tier 1 — stop re-uploading data just downloaded (lowest risk, no new server code)
6 sites in `QuotationModule.tsx`/`InvoiceModule.tsx` already call a real route, then `GET /api/state` to refresh, then hand that fresh server data to `onUpdateDb` — which immediately POSTs the entire thing straight back to `/api/migrate`. Fix: a local-only `setDb`-style update (same pattern as item 37), needs a `setDbLocal`-style prop threaded from `App.tsx`. No new backend work.

### Tier 2 — route already exists, frontend never calls it (low-moderate risk, wiring only)
10 sites recompute the result in `dbStore.ts` and blob-sync it despite a real, unused REST endpoint already existing:
- `InvoiceModule.handleConfirmPay` → `POST /api/transactions/invoices/:id/paid`
- `AdminSettings.handleAddInvestor` → `POST /api/transactions/investors`
- `AdminSettings.handleAddBank` (create) → `POST /api/transactions/banks`
- `AdminSettings.handleAddTax` → `POST /api/tax-slabs`
- `MasterEntities.handleRestoreProducts` → `POST /api/products` (currently unpersisted until an unrelated save happens to blob-sync it)
- `InventoryModule.handleCreateWarehouse` → `POST /api/warehouses`
- `PosModule` shift open/close → `POST`/`PUT /api/pos/shifts`
- `QuotationModule.handleStatusChange`/`handleConvert`, `AdminSettings.handleOpenMonth`/`handleConfirmClose`, `handleAddUser`/`executeDeleteUser` — each already calls the right route earlier in the same handler, then redundantly recomputes locally; drop the redundant recompute.

### Tier 3 — no server route exists yet (real new-route work, one action at a time)
| Area | Missing routes needed |
|---|---|
| ExpenseModule | pay, cancel |
| PosModule | hold/list/resume held invoices; sale completion never reuses the invoice route |
| InventoryModule | PR approve/reject, PO cancel, manual stock adjustment |
| RecurringExpenses | template CRUD, accrual edit/delete |
| AdminSettings — Company | settings update (name/logo/VAT/theme), inventory-module toggle, ZATCA toggle |
| AdminSettings — Banks | update, set-default, toggle-active |
| AdminSettings — Tax Slabs | set-default/clone |
| AdminSettings — Templates | create, activate, toggle-option, update-property, canvas-layout save (zero REST surface today) |
| AdminSettings — Translations | full CRUD (zero REST surface today) |
| AdminSettings — Users | active/inactive toggle |

**Explicitly carved out, needs product sign-off before any migration work**: `AdminSettings.handleTruncateTransactions` (wipes all quotations/invoices/expenses/vouchers for a company) has **no server-side enforcement at all** today — pure client-side. Migrating it isn't mechanical; it needs a decision on confirmation flow, audit logging, and super-admin gating first.

### Execution approach
Delegated as a 7-agent parallel push, one agent per file/domain end-to-end (its Tier 1+2+3 items combined) rather than splitting by tier, since tier boundaries cut across files awkwardly. `AdminSettings.tsx` (26 of the 30 remaining sites) was split across three agents by sub-area (Company/Banks/Tax Slabs; Users; Templates/Translations). `isolation: worktree` was requested for all 7, but only 2 (POS, Expense/Recurring) actually got a real isolated git worktree — the other 5 hit a transient API connection error mid-task, and on resume fell back to editing the shared main checkout directly and concurrently (confirmed live: multiple agents' edits to `AdminSettings.tsx`/`masterEntities.ts`/`users.ts`/`App.tsx` interleaved in real time). Reconciliation for the 2 genuine worktrees required manual conflict resolution where a worktree's branch point predated an already-merged change to the same file (`server/routes/transactions.ts` needed the Expense/Recurring worktree's new routes hand-spliced in alongside the Users agent's already-merged investor-investment route, not a blind file copy). The dev server (hardcoded to port 3000, no env override) can't run multiple live instances, so no agent self-verified against a live server or ran vitest — all verification (full `npm run lint` + `npx vitest run`, 124/124 passing across 12 files) was done by the orchestrating session after every merge, with the dev server restarted first per `CLAUDE.md`.

**A real regression was caught before merge, not after**: the Sales-domain agent wired `InvoiceModule.tsx`'s `handleConfirmPay` to `POST /api/transactions/invoices/:id/paid` — but that route (pre-existing, not written by this push) always settles the invoice's *full* remaining balance via `syncVoucherForInvoice`'s single-voucher-per-invoice model, silently ignoring the partial-payment `amount`/`bankId` fields the UI actually collects. Merging it as-is would have marked a partial payment as fully paid. Reverted that one handler back to the local `dbStore.markInvoicePaid` path (which correctly supports partial payments) pending a proper fix to the route — see Deferred list.

**Also surfaced, not fixed (flagged by the agents themselves)**: `PosModule.tsx`'s shift/held-invoice handlers now correctly persist via real routes first, but still call `onUpdateDb` (full blob sync) afterward for local state — no local-only setter was wired for this file, so the redundant full-state sync this whole effort is trying to eliminate still fires for POS shift/hold actions (data-loss risk is gone, waste isn't). Three Company Setup form `onChange` handlers (POS-tab in `AdminSettings.tsx`, ~line 2655/2668/2678) call `handleCompanySave(newComp)` with a plain object instead of a form event, and `handleCompanySave` unconditionally calls `e.preventDefault()` — a pre-existing runtime bug (not introduced by this push, signature preserved deliberately), not yet fixed.

## 40. ZATCA Unit-of-Measure Compliance + Portal-Wide Blob-Sync Cleanup (Aug 2026)

Two related pieces of work, the second triggered by a real incident during the first.

### ZATCA unit codes
Every invoice line item's `<cbc:InvoicedQuantity unitCode="...">` was hardcoded to `"PCE"` (piece) in `server/lib/zatca/xmlBuilder.ts`, regardless of what was actually sold — the app's Units of Measure feature (Master Registries) was a fully disconnected standalone table with no foreign key from products or invoice line items anywhere. Fixed end to end:
- `src/zatcaUnitCodes.ts` — a curated allowed list of real UN/ECE Recommendation 20 codes (ZATCA doesn't define its own list; this project has no bundled machine-readable copy of the full official list, so this is a well-established common subset, not exhaustive) plus `normalizeZatcaUnitCode()`.
- `unitsOfMeasure.code` creation is now validated against the allowed list server-side, and the "New Unit" form is a dropdown instead of free text (previous placeholder literally suggested `"PCS, BOX"` — neither a real code).
- `invoiceItems`/`quotationItems` both got a new nullable `unit` column (schema pushed live). Unit flows: product's unit → invoice/quotation line item (auto-inherited on catalog match, `InvoiceModule.tsx`/`QuotationModule.tsx`) → Credit/Debit Notes (copied from the original invoice's line, same table) → the real ZATCA XML.
- Displayed on the printed document next to quantity (`DocumentRenderer.tsx`) so the invoice and the ZATCA submission can never silently disagree.
- **Dual-gate verified**: created 4 real products with 4 different units (PCE/KGM/MTR/LTR), invoiced all 4, ran `fatoora -validate` against the actual generated XML — `[XSD]`/`[EN]`/`[KSA]` all PASSED, no unit-code-related error or warning anywhere. `[PIH]` FAILED is the already-documented, expected limitation for a single invoice validated outside its real hash chain.
- 3 new automated tests (`tests/zatcaUnitCodes.test.ts`), full suite at 128/128.
- **Known pre-existing gap, not fixed**: one legacy `unitsOfMeasure` row ("Each", code `EA`) predates this validation and isn't a real ZATCA code; a product still references it. Not migrated — any new invoice using it safely falls back to `PCE` via `normalizeZatcaUnitCode`, but the row itself needs a manual re-save through the new dropdown to actually fix.

### The incident that triggered the second half
While verifying the above, POS threw `Migration failed: Failed query: insert into "users" ...` — a foreign-key violation. Root cause: the browser's cached `db.users` snapshot (loaded as super-admin, who sees every company's users) still held a since-deleted automated-test fixture user/company, and a POS action's leftover full-blob sync (`onUpdateDb` after `PosModule.tsx`'s shift/hold actions already persisted for real — exactly the item 38 follow-up flagged and left unfixed) tried to re-upsert that dead reference and failed, blocking an unrelated POS action. This was flagged as a known gap, not a surprise, but seeing it actually break something prompted finishing the job immediately rather than leaving it deferred.

**Shipped**: `onUpdateDbLocal` (the local-only, no-`/api/migrate` setter already used by Sales/Catalog/Expense modules) is now threaded through `PosModule.tsx`, `InventoryModule.tsx`, `MasterEntities.tsx`, and `AdminSettings.tsx`, replacing every call site where a real route had already persisted the change (or nothing needed persisting at all, e.g. a company-view switch). A full `grep` sweep of `src/` after the fix confirms exactly 4 real `onUpdateDb(` call sites remain — `InvoiceModule.handleConfirmPay` (partial payments, no route support yet), `AdminSettings.handleTruncateTransactions` (destructive, needs a product decision first), `AdminSettings.handleAddCompany`'s starter-resource creation, and `PosModule.handlePayInvoice` (POS sale completion, legacy `dbStore` path) — all four have **no real backend route** for what they do, so blob-sync is currently their only persistence mechanism; each needs a dedicated route built before it can safely move off it. (Update: the latter two were fixed in items 42 and 43 respectively.)

**Also fixed while here**: the on-screen sync-failure banner (`cloudError` in `App.tsx`) no longer surfaces raw driver/SQL error text (the exact string that leaked the failing query above) — it shows a generic, user-facing message for all three sync paths (blob sync, theme PATCH, language PATCH); full detail still goes to the console for debugging.

### Verification
`tsc --noEmit` clean throughout, dev server restarted before every check, full suite 128/128 passing after the final sweep.

### Follow-up: "Walk-in Customer" moved to B2C, dual-gate re-verified
Fixed the `BUYER_INCOMPLETE` gap that had left `INV-2002`/`INV-2003` (UX Test) with no ZATCA XML ever generated: "Walk-in Customer" was `buyerType: B2B` (requiring full VAT/address fields it never had, since it's a walk-in retail buyer, not a registered company) — switched to `B2C` with `name` as the only field, matching `validateBuyerFields`'s documented B2C rule (`server/lib/zatca/validators.ts`). Resubmitted `INV-2003` for real: `invoiceTypeCode` correctly recomputed to `0200000` (Simplified) from the customer's current `buyerType` (`processInvoiceZatca` re-derives this on every submission, not fixed at invoice creation), and `fatoora -validate` against the freshly generated XML passed fully — `[XSD]`/`[EN]`/`[KSA]`/`[QR]`/`[SIGNATURE]`/`[PIH]` all PASSED (the B2C path gets `[QR]`/`[SIGNATURE]` verdicts the B2B path doesn't). Same two pre-existing warnings as always (`BR-KSA-EN16931-11`, `BR-KSA-23`, both already tracked below).

**New gap surfaced, not fixed**: UX Test's actual live ZATCA sandbox submission fails both before and after the B2C fix, with a generic `SYS_ERR` ("ZATCA invoice clearance/reporting failed") — XML building/signing succeeds and validates cleanly offline via the SDK every time, so this isn't an XML-correctness problem. Most likely cause: UX Test hasn't completed real ZATCA sandbox onboarding (no valid CSID/certificate issued for that company), same prerequisite documented elsewhere in this file for other companies. Not investigated further — tracked below.

## 41. ZATCA Sandbox Certificate-Identity Bug — Live Clearance/Reporting Fixed (Aug 2026)

Followed up on item 40's `SYS_ERR` finding for UX Test — the earlier "hasn't completed sandbox onboarding" diagnosis was wrong (UX Test already had a full Compliance + Production CSID on file). The real cause, found by decoding the actual certificate ZATCA's sandbox returns:

**Root cause**: ZATCA's Sandbox environment can issue a Production CSID certificate that genuinely *pairs with the caller's own submitted keypair* (so this project's existing `certificateMatchesPrivateKey` check correctly passes), while still *forcing the certificate's subject identity* to ZATCA's shared public test taxpayer (`CN=TST-886431145-399999999900003`, SAN `UID=399999999900003` — "Maximum Speed Tech Supply LTD"), regardless of what VAT/CR the CSR actually requested. This project already had detection + a fix for the *other* known manifestation of this same sandbox quirk (ZATCA returning its literal published sample certificate byte-for-byte — `sandboxSampleIdentity.ts`), but that check was an exact-byte match against one hardcoded certificate, so it silently missed this second case: a certificate issued fresh for the caller's own key, with only the subject forced. Every invoice kept declaring seller VAT `300000000000003` (whatever the wizard's Step 1 had saved) against a certificate actually authorized only for `399999999900003` — ZATCA's real API correctly rejected this every time with `certificate-permissions: "User only allowed to use the vat number that exists in the authentication certificate"`, even though the signature itself was cryptographically valid and both offline XML/SDK validation passed cleanly (which is exactly why this looked like a mysterious "XML is fine but submission fails" bug).

**Fixed**:
- `server/lib/zatca/x509.ts` — new `extractZatcaCertificateTaxpayerIdentity()`, reads the VAT (SAN `UID`) and CR (Subject CN's `TST-<cr>-<vat>` pattern) directly out of whatever certificate ZATCA actually returns, instead of trusting the CSR fields that were submitted to request it.
- `server/routes/zatca.ts`'s `request-production-csid` — after the existing key-match check, if `environment === 'sandbox'` and the certificate's extracted VAT disagrees with the stored `config.tinNumber`, the environment's `tinNumber`/`crNumber` are corrected to what the certificate is actually bound to. This generalizes the earlier `usedSandboxSampleKey`-only fix (item 40) to cover both manifestations of the quirk, not just the one exact-byte-match case.
- `src/zatcaSandboxIdentity.ts` (new, client+server shared) — single source of truth for ZATCA's real shared sandbox identity (VAT `399999999900003`, CR `886431145`, and address fields matching ZATCA's own bundled SDK sample invoices for the same entity), replacing the fictional placeholder values (`300000000000003` / `1010000000` / "King Fahd Road") that `ZatcaOnboardingWizard.tsx`'s Sandbox Step 1 defaults, its environment-switch reset, and its status-fetch fallback had all been showing — none of which were ever what ZATCA actually issues a sandbox certificate for. `server/lib/zatca/sandboxSampleIdentity.ts` now re-exports the VAT/CR from this same shared file instead of duplicating them, so client and server can't drift apart on this again.

**Verified live, both gates**: re-ran the onboarding's Production CSID step for UX Test — corrected `tinNumber`/`crNumber` in the DB — then resubmitted `INV-2003` for real: ZATCA's actual sandbox API returned `status: "REPORTED"` with **zero errors** (three pre-existing/already-tracked warnings only — `BR-KSA-23`, `BR-KSA-EN16931-11`, and a newly-surfaced `BR-KSA-F-08` about the CRN scheme value, none blocking), a real QR code, and a real signature. Ran `fatoora -validate` against that exact cleared XML: `[XSD]`/`[EN]`/`[KSA]`/`[QR]`/`[SIGNATURE]`/`[PIH]` all PASSED, matching the same three warnings the live API itself reported — both verification paths agree. This is the first time in this session UX Test achieved genuine live ZATCA sandbox clearance, not just offline XML correctness.

`tsc --noEmit` clean, dev server restarted before each check, full suite 128/128 passing.

**New gap surfaced, not fixed**: `BR-KSA-F-08` ("Other Seller ID for scheme CRN appears to be incorrect") is a new warning, first seen on this corrected submission. Not investigated — possibly related to how the CR number is now sourced (from the certificate) versus the format ZATCA expects in `cbc:ID schemeID="CRN"`. Warning only, didn't block reporting.

---

## 42. `AdminSettings.handleAddCompany` Starter Resources Moved Off Blob Sync (Aug 2026)

One of the 4 remaining no-real-route `onUpdateDb` sites tracked since item 40/41. New-company onboarding auto-creates a starter bank account, Walk-in Customer, Cash Vendor, default document template, and the current open fiscal month — all previously merged into local state and pushed only via the generic `/api/migrate` blob sync, since none of those 5 creations had a dedicated route call at all (the company row itself already did, per item 37/38).

**Fixed**: `handleAddCompany` now makes 5 sequential real calls — `POST /api/banks`, `/api/customers`, `/api/vendors`, `/api/templates`, `/api/transactions/months` — each with `companyId` explicitly set to the just-created company's id in the request body. This matters because the admin creating the company is not necessarily "in" it yet (their own session's `req.targetCompanyId` is whatever company they're currently viewing) — the auth middleware (`server.ts`) only honors a body-supplied `companyId` override for super-admins, which is exactly who this form is gated to. The final merge into local state now goes through `onUpdateDbLocal` instead of `onUpdateDb`, since every table is already persisted for real by that point.

**Real bug caught while wiring this up**: the starter "Walk-in Customer"/"Cash Vendor" records only ever set `name`/`phone`/`email`/`address` — no `buyerType`. Under the old blob-sync path this was silently accepted (no server-side ZATCA validation on that path), but `customers`/`vendors` real routes run `validateBuyerFields()`, whose default (`buyerType` unset → schema default `'B2B'`) requires a full VAT number + street/building/district/city/postal code. Both starter records got `400`-rejected on first real-route test. Fixed by setting `buyerType: 'B2C'` explicitly on both (matching item 40's "Walk-in Customer → B2C" precedent — a placeholder record with only a name has no business being B2B). Also found `Customer`/`Vendor` in `src/types.ts` were missing the `buyerType`/`vatNumber`/`streetName`/`buildingNumber`/`district`/`city`/`postalCode`/`countryCode` fields entirely, despite all 8 being real columns on both tables (`src/db/schema.ts`) and already read/written elsewhere (`MasterEntities.tsx`) — added them to both interfaces.

**Verified**: new `tests/newCompanyStarterResources.test.ts` — creates a company as a super-admin whose own session company differs from the new one, creates all 5 starter resources via the real routes exactly as the UI does, then asserts every row landed in Postgres scoped to the new company (not the super-admin's home company). `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 129/129 passing.
