## 43. `PosModule.handlePayInvoice` Now Reaches the Real ZATCA Pipeline (Aug 2026)

The last of the 4 no-real-route `onUpdateDb` sites (items 40/41/42). POS sale completion went through the legacy `dbStore.saveInvoice` + full-blob `onUpdateDb` path — confirmed live in item 32 that a completed POS sale had empty `invoice_items` and `zatcaStatus` stuck at `NOT_SUBMITTED` forever, since that path never called `processInvoiceZatca` at all. POS is a live, real-money sales channel, so this was the highest-priority of the 4 sites.

**Fixed**: `handlePayInvoice` now builds the exact same `invoiceData` shape `InvoiceModule.handleSaveInvoice` sends and POSTs it to `POST /api/transactions/invoices` — the same route every other invoice-creation path uses (computes totals server-side, generates the real invoice number, posts the Receipt voucher, and fires `processInvoiceZatca` fire-and-forget after its own transaction commits). Line items now get real `generateId()` uuids (the old `inv-item-${Math.random()}` string was never a valid Postgres `uuid` — harmless under the old in-memory dbStore path, would have hard-failed against the real route's `invoice_items.id` column) and inherit their ZATCA unit code from the matched product, same `'No'/'Lumpsum'` → `'PCE'` fallback pattern `QuotationModule`/`InvoiceModule` already use (item 40) — POS sales previously carried no unit onto the XML at all. Local state refreshes from `GET /api/state` after a successful save, mirroring `InvoiceModule.handleSaveInvoice`'s own pattern, instead of trusting a locally-mutated blob. Added an `isSavingPos` guard + disabled/"Processing..." button state to prevent a double-click from firing two POST requests for the same sale (the old synchronous local-only path had no such race). Removed the now-unused `saveInvoice` import from `dbStore.ts`.

**Verified**: new `tests/posSaleZatcaPipeline.test.ts` — POSTs the exact payload shape `handlePayInvoice` now sends, then asserts the invoice/items/voucher rows landed correctly AND polls until `zatcaStatus` resolves. For a `zatcaEnabled: false` test company the real terminal status is `'DISABLED'`, not the schema's `'NOT_SUBMITTED'` default — this is the concrete, asserted proof the pipeline is now actually reached (before this fix, `zatcaStatus` would never move off its default at all via this path). `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 130/130 passing. Live UI click-through was attempted but blocked by the already-documented, recurring Browser-pane `document.hidden` compositing issue (items 31/33), unrelated to this change — code-level + real HTTP/DB verification used as the fallback, per the same precedent.

**All 4 no-real-route `onUpdateDb` sites tracked since item 40 are now resolved except**: `InvoiceModule.handleConfirmPay` (blocked on partial-payment support, see below) and `AdminSettings.handleTruncateTransactions` (destructive, needs a product decision on safeguards first, not a missing-route problem).

## 44. Canvas Designer: Keyboard Reordering + Row-Composition Visibility + `custom_field` Parity (Aug 2026)

Items 2/3 from the pre-40 design review. Per explicit instruction, re-verified each claim fresh against the CURRENT state of `AdminSettings.tsx` (heavily modified earlier this session — col-span fix, pageSize dropdown, unsaved-changes tracking, delete UI) rather than trusting the original review verbatim. Findings, and where they diverged from the original description:

- **Keyboard reordering**: confirmed still genuinely absent — `handleCanvasDragStart/DragOver/Drop` (HTML5 drag-and-drop) was the only reordering mechanism; the drag handle (`☰`) is a plain non-focusable `<div>`.
- **Row-width validation**: confirmed still absent, but on closer inspection there is no actual "broken" state possible to validate against — `grid-cols-12` + `col-span-N` always wraps correctly on its own (the browser never lets a row exceed 12 columns; excess blocks simply flow to the next row). The real gap is *visibility*, not validation: an admin has no way to see why a row looks uneven (a block landing alone on its own row because the space above wasn't enough for it).
- **`custom_field` "padding" gap — turned out broader than described**: the original review said `custom_field` was missing a padding control "unlike `customer_info`/`items_table`" — but on inspection, **no block type of any kind** has a padding control; the review's implied baseline was wrong. The real renderer (`DocumentRenderer.tsx`'s `getBlockStyle`) has always read `block.props?.p` for every block type, and `handleAddCustomBlock` even seeds new custom blocks with `p: '2'` already — the prop just had no UI to ever change it, for any block, not only `custom_field`.
- **`custom_field` missing `isBilingual`/border-style — confirmed, and found to be a real active bug, not just a missing convenience control**: `DocumentRenderer.tsx` already gates `custom_field`'s Arabic label on `block.props?.isBilingual !== false` — but the Canvas Designer's `custom_field` panel never exposed a checkbox to set it, so it was permanently stuck in its default-true state on every printed document, with no way to turn it off short of hand-editing `layoutJson`. Border-style was missing on both ends — no UI control, and the renderer's `custom_field` branch hardcoded its card style rather than reading `block.props?.borderStyle` at all (unlike `customer_info`, which already supports `solid`/`dashed`/`none`).

**Fixed**:
- `handleMoveBlock(blockId, direction)` (`AdminSettings.tsx`) + real `<button>` ↑/↓ controls in each block's hover toolbar — natively keyboard-operable (Tab + Enter/Space), disabled at the first/last visible position. Moves relative to the nearest *visible* neighbor, not just the adjacent array slot, since `canvasBlocks` also holds hidden blocks interleaved with visible ones (a naive adjacent-index swap could silently no-op).
- A non-blocking row-composition summary strip above the canvas — simulates the same greedy 12-column wrap the real grid performs and shows each resulting row's fill (`Row 1 • 12/12`, amber if under 12) so an admin can see the "why" at a glance. Deliberately advisory, not pass/fail — a row under 12 is very often an intentional design (one block centered alone), not a mistake.
- A "Padding" select added to the generic "Block Style & Spacing" section (same options/pattern as the existing Margin Top/Bottom controls) — applies to every block type, not just `custom_field`, since the underlying gap was universal.
- `custom_field`'s panel gained a "Show Arabic Label" checkbox (wired to the `isBilingual` prop the renderer already reads) and a "Box Frame Style" select (`solid`/`dashed`/`none`, matching `customer_info`'s exact option set). `DocumentRenderer.tsx`'s `custom_field` branch now builds its card class from `borderStyle` the same way `customer_info` does, instead of a hardcoded style.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 130/130 passing (no server-side surface to this change — pure frontend). Live UI click-through was attempted but blocked by the same recurring Browser-pane `document.hidden` issue as item 43 (confirmed environment-wide right now, not specific to this change — even a plain tab switch to Settings doesn't render past its breadcrumb while the pane is in this state) — verified instead via careful structural re-reading of every edited JSX region plus `tsc`'s full syntax/type validation, and by tracing `handleAddCustomBlock`'s existing default props (`p: '2'`, `borderStyle: 'solid'`, `isBilingual: true`) confirming the underlying data model already anticipated exactly these controls.

## 45. Stale Worktree Cleanup Uncovered a Live "New Expense Creation Is Broken" Bug (Aug 2026)

While removing the 4 leftover `.claude/worktrees/*` directories from this session's earlier parallel-agent push (all already reviewed/merged, now just causing `npx vitest run` to report spurious `Cannot find module './dbCredentials.js'` failures), diffed each one's uncommitted changes against current `master` before deleting anything, per the "investigate unfamiliar state before discarding it" rule — 3 of the 4 diffed as either empty or strictly behind `master`, safe to delete outright. The 4th (`lucid-engelbart-65ecad`) did not: its uncommitted `server/routes/expenses.ts` had `expenseNumber`/`createdById`/`createdAt` counter-assignment logic that `master`'s current version of the same file has never had.

**Root cause, confirmed live against the running dev server before touching anything**: `POST /api/expenses` never generates `expenseNumber` (unlike every other document-creation route — `transactions.ts`'s invoice/quotation creation already does this via `getAndIncrementCounter`) — it just inserts whatever the client sends. `ExpenseModule.tsx`'s real create payload (`handleSaveExpense`) never includes `expenseNumber` at all (it's meant to be server-assigned, exactly like `invoiceNumber`/`quotationNumber`). `expense_number` is a `NOT NULL` column with no default (`src/db/schema.ts`). Net effect: **every new-expense creation from the actual UI 500'd outright** — reproduced directly with a scratch script against the live dev server (`500: null value in column "expense_number" violates not-null constraint`, surfaced through drizzle as a raw SQL-query error). This was a live, currently-shipping regression, not a hypothetical.

**Fixed** (`server/routes/expenses.ts`'s `POST /`):
- `expenseNumber`/`createdById`/`createdAt` are now stripped from the incoming body (so a crafted request can't inject or overwrite them) and reassigned server-side: a fresh `EXP-<n>` counter value + `req.user.id` + `new Date()` for a genuinely new expense (`isNew`), or restored from the pre-existing row for an update.
- **A first attempt at this fix (just deleting the three fields, matching the abandoned worktree's approach exactly) introduced its own regression**, caught immediately by testing the update path, not just create: Postgres validates the full candidate row of an `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE` for `NOT NULL` even when the `UPDATE` branch is what actually executes — so an update with the fields merely absent (rather than restored) 500'd too. Fixed by fetching the existing row (the route already does this once, for the `assertOwnsRow` ownership check) and re-populating `expenseNumber`/`createdById`/`createdAt` from it on the update path, rather than leaving them unset.

**Verified**: new `tests/expenseCreateRoute.test.ts` — (1) creates an expense with the exact payload shape `ExpenseModule.tsx` sends (no `id`, no `expenseNumber`) and asserts a real `EXP-<n>` was assigned; (2) updates that same expense while attempting to inject a fake `expenseNumber`/`createdById`/`createdAt` in the request body, and asserts the legitimate field (`description`) updated while the three protected fields stayed exactly at their original values. `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite **132/132** passing. All 7 stale worktree-related branches removed (`git worktree remove` + `git branch -D`, all confirmed zero unmerged commits first) — `vitest run` (no path filter) is now clean of worktree noise entirely.

---

## 46. Language Translation (ar/ur) Review — Root Causes Found and Fixed (Aug 2026)

User-reported: "some pages, menu items, headings does not change" when switching to Arabic/Urdu. Reviewed the full i18n architecture (`src/hooks.ts`'s `useTranslation`, the `translations` Postgres table, `SEED_TRANSLATIONS` static fallback in `src/dbStore.ts`) end to end rather than patching symptoms individually.

**How it works**: `t(key)` checks `db.translations` (DB-backed, editable via Admin Settings) then `SEED_TRANSLATIONS` (client-bundled fallback); if the current language's field is empty on a matched row, it **silently falls back to English** — no error, no visible gap. If the key matches neither source at all, it auto-registers an English-only row via `POST /api/register-missing-key` and renders the raw key as a placeholder. This auto-registration mechanism is sound in principle but nothing was consistently filling in the `ar`/`ur` fields it leaves blank — live DB audit found **179 of 433 rows (41%) had no Arabic or Urdu text at all**.

**Two code bugs were producing a large share of that 41%, not just an admin workflow gap**:
1. `App.tsx`'s page-heading logic fell through to `t(activeTab)` for ~22 route ids whenever no explicit case matched — passing the raw internal id (`"pos-terminal"`, `"settings"`, `"customers-add"`) into the translator as display text. No language ever had a real heading for those pages; the breadcrumb literally rendered the slug (CSS `capitalize` turned `pos-terminal` into `Pos-Terminal`, which is what was actually being seen). Fixed by giving all 33 tab ids (cross-referenced against every `id:` in the nav config) an explicit, real, already-or-newly-translated title, with `New X` / `Edit X` distinction wired through the existing `editTarget` state for the six List/Add MasterEntities modules.
2. `t(openMonth.name)` (`App.tsx`) and equivalent renders in `Dashboard.tsx`/`AdminSettings.tsx` fed dynamic strings like `"August 2026"` straight into the translator — registering a brand-new, permanently-untranslatable key every single month, forever (confirmed several stale entries: `"June 2026"`, `"July 2026"`, `"August 2026"`, `"2026-07"`, `"2026-08"` already in the DB). Fixed with a new `translateMonthLabel()` helper (`src/hooks.ts`) that translates only the month WORD via the normal dictionary (12 new keys, reusable everywhere) and reassembles it with the untouched numeric year.

**A third, silent gap found the same way — status/enum values never routed through `t()` at all**: `inv.status`, `inv.paymentStatus`, ZATCA status codes, quotation status, expense status, PO/PR status, POS shift status, and several `Active`/`Inactive`/`Deactivate` toggle badges were rendered as raw stored English strings (`"Active"`, `"Paid"`, `"Draft"`, `"NOT_SUBMITTED"`, …) directly as `<StatusPill>`/badge children across `InvoiceModule.tsx`, `QuotationModule.tsx`, `ExpenseModule.tsx`, `PosModule.tsx`, `RecurringExpenses.tsx`, `InventoryModule.tsx`, `MasterEntities.tsx`, `AdminSettings.tsx` — every status badge in the app stayed English regardless of language. Wrapped every one in `t()`. **Caught and fixed a real shadowing bug while doing this**: two `.map(t => …)` callbacks in `RecurringExpenses.tsx` used `t` as a template-item loop variable, shadowing the `useTranslation` `t` function within that scope — a blind find-and-replace would have compiled (`t(posting.status)` would've tried to call a `RecurringExpenseTemplate` object) and 500'd at runtime; caught immediately by `tsc` (`TS2349: This expression is not callable`) and fixed by renaming the loop variable rather than the translator.

**`<html lang>` never updated** (found during live verification): the root `<div dir={isRTL ? "rtl" : "ltr"}>` in `App.tsx` correctly drives RTL layout for everything React renders, but `<html lang>` lives in `index.html`, outside React's reach, and stayed hardcoded `"en"` regardless of the selected language — the actual attribute screen readers, spell-check, and the browser's own font/translation heuristics key off. Fixed with a `useEffect` syncing `document.documentElement.lang`/`.dir` whenever the language changes.

**Bulk content pass — no external dependency used**: cross-referenced every `t('literal')` call site in the codebase (plus every dynamic status/enum value and the 12 new month-name keys) against the live DB, producing an exact list of 274 keys with no row at all and 140 with a row but blank `ar`/`ur`. The app has a built-in "Auto-Translate" button (`AdminSettings.tsx`'s `handleAutoTranslate` → `POST /api/translate-all`) that calls the Gemini API for missing keys — **confirmed unusable here**: no `GEMINI_API_KEY` is configured in this environment's `.env`, and more importantly, per project-owner direction, an external paid API is the wrong foundation for core UI translation (availability, rate limits, cost, phrasing consistency). All 414 translations were written directly — professional business/ERP-register Arabic and Urdu matching the tone of the ~300 translations already in the app — and upserted straight into the `translations` table. 19 confirmed-dead rows (the raw tab-ids and dynamic month-strings the two code bugs above produced, now provably unreachable) were deleted; ~83 other unreferenced-looking rows were deliberately left alone rather than risk deleting something a call-site pattern my static search missed still depends on.

**Process bug caught mid-fix**: the audit tooling's own key-matching briefly `.trim()`'d every key, which silently broke the one real key in the app with meaningful trailing whitespace (`t('Welcome back, ')`, `Dashboard.tsx` — the trailing space is load-bearing, the username is interpolated immediately after with no separator) — produced a false "missing" report and a bogus duplicate untrimmed/trimmed pair of DB rows. Caught by inspecting the DB directly rather than trusting the audit script's output blindly; duplicate deleted, audit script fixed to stop trimming.

**Verified live**, not just by inspection: created a throwaway temporary super-admin user (`uiLanguage: 'ar'`), logged in through the actual running app, and confirmed via the real rendered page — sidebar navigation fully Arabic (all ~30 items), Dashboard fully Arabic including the previously-broken `"Welcome back, "` interpolation, the previously-raw `"settings"` heading now correctly reading `"الإعدادات والشركات"`, the previously-buggy month label correctly reading `"يونيو 2026 (مفتوح)"` ("June 2026 (Open)"), and `<html lang>`/`dir` flipping live between `en`/`ltr` and `ar`/`rtl` on each language-toggle click — then switched back to English and confirmed full reversion. Temp user deleted afterward. `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite **132/132** passing throughout (no server-side surface to most of this — it's a DB-content + frontend-wiring fix).

**Known, deliberately out of scope**: `ZatcaOnboardingWizard.tsx` (873 lines) and the ZATCA clearance-details modal in `InvoiceModule.tsx` remain almost entirely hardcoded English — both are narrow, admin-only, highly technical ZATCA-compliance flows where the terminology (CSID, CSR, compliance suite) is arguably appropriate to leave in English/transliterated form even for Arabic-speaking admins; translating them properly is a larger, separate effort with its own terminology-precision requirements. `LoginScreen.tsx` has no language handling at all — likely intentional, since there's no authenticated user yet to have a `uiLanguage` preference, but not confirmed as a deliberate product decision. The ~83 DB rows left unreferenced by the current static-key scan were not deleted (see above) — worth a follow-up audit with a more thorough dynamic-key scan before ever bulk-deleting them.

## 47. Admin Settings Tabs — Second Translation Pass, Prompted by a Direct Request to Re-check (Aug 2026)

Item 46's bulk audit only scanned literal `t('...')` calls — it structurally could not see `t(someVariable.someField)` calls, since the string being translated isn't visible until runtime. User asked to specifically re-check Admin & Settings; that request surfaced exactly this blind spot.

**Root findings**:
- `AdminSettings.tsx` has two independent layouts for its own tab navigation — "Sleek Hub" (top tab bar, `useSleekLayout` default `true`) and "Classic Sidebar" (left-column vertical list, opt-in via a toggle in the same header). Both existed before this session. **Classic Sidebar had its own hand-duplicated copy of every tab's id/label/grouping**, completely independent from `CATEGORY_GROUPS` (the single config array Sleek Hub reads from) — every one of its ~15 labels was raw hardcoded English JSX text, never routed through `t()` at all, and it had silently **drifted to be missing two entire tabs** (`zatca` and `roles` had no button anywhere in Classic Sidebar — an admin using that layout had no way to reach ZATCA settings or Roles/RBAC by clicking, full stop, independent of language).
- Separately, and more surprising: **Sleek Hub's own `t(cat.label)`/`t(st.label)` calls — the "already correct" layout — had never actually been translated either.** The 18 category/tab-group label keys (`"Organization & Setup"`, `"Bank Accounts"`, `"Staff Permissions"`, etc.) existed in the DB with real `en` values (auto-registered from live use) but blank `ar`/`ur`, exactly like the 179-key gap in item 46 — just invisible to that item's literal-string scan because these are dynamic calls.
- Same bug pattern found again, a third time this session, in `PosModule.tsx`: `t(p.category)` (a product-grid category badge) and `t(c)` inside the category-filter pill list both ran **arbitrary per-company user data** (a category name the company itself typed in) through the shared global translation dictionary — same class of bug as item 46's `t(openMonth.name)` fix, just not caught by that pass since it's also a dynamic call. Live DB check confirmed no pollution had occurred yet (the one category in current use, `"Services"`, hadn't been auto-registered), but the bug was real and would have started polluting the shared dictionary with tenant-specific junk the moment a company used a differently-named category.

**Fixed**:
- Classic Sidebar rewritten to render from `CATEGORY_GROUPS` — the same source of truth Sleek Hub already used — instead of maintaining a second copy. This one change fixes the translation gap, restores the two missing tabs, and (going forward) makes the two layouts structurally unable to drift apart again. A small `CLASSIC_SIDEBAR_ICON_COLOR` lookup preserves that layout's distinct per-item icon coloring (Sleek Hub doesn't have per-item colors, so `CATEGORY_GROUPS` itself has no color field to reuse).
- Wrote and applied real Arabic + Urdu for all 18 category/tab-group labels plus the `"ON"`/`"OFF"` inventory-module badge text (2 of the 18 needed a fresh row inserted, not just filled in).
- `PosModule.tsx`'s `p.category` badge and the category-filter pill list now render arbitrary category names as-is (matching how customer/product/vendor names are correctly handled elsewhere) — only the fixed `'All'` sentinel in the pill list still goes through `t()`.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite **132/132** passing (no server-side surface). DB-level confirmation that all 18 category labels plus `ON`/`OFF` now have real `ar`/`ur`. The page-heading fix from item 46 was re-confirmed live through this same session (still correctly reading `"الإعدادات والشركات"` in Arabic). Live visual confirmation of the Classic Sidebar's rendered buttons specifically was blocked by the same recurring Browser-pane `document.hidden`/Framer-Motion animation-pause issue documented in items 31/33/44 (confirmed still present; attempted forcing `document.hidden = false` + a synthetic `visibilitychange` event as a workaround, which did not unstick it) — verification for that specific piece rests on: `CATEGORY_GROUPS`/`t()` being the exact same proven-working pattern Sleek Hub already used successfully (and was left completely unchanged), direct DB confirmation the translation content is real and present, and `tsc`'s full type/structure validation.

## 48. Master Registries Add/Edit Forms — Zero Translation Wiring, "Direction Not RTL" Diagnosed (Aug 2026)

User asked specifically to check the Add Customer / Add Vendor / Add Product / Add Category / Add Unit / Add Warehouse forms (`MasterEntities.tsx`, `mode === 'add'` block, ~750 lines) and reported both missing translation and RTL direction not applying.

**Findings**:
- Confirmed: every one of the six add/edit forms was **100% hardcoded English JSX** — every `<label>`, placeholder, dropdown option, validation message, and Cancel/Save button — despite the file already calling `useTranslation()` at the top and using `t()` correctly elsewhere (its List/Directory view). The add-form block specifically had never been wired at all.
- **RTL direction**: traced the render tree — `MasterEntities` is mounted as a normal child (no portal, no separate window), directly inside `App.tsx`'s `<div dir={isRTL ? "rtl" : "ltr"}>` wrapper, and a scan of the file for hardcoded physical-direction classes (`text-left`, `ml-`, `pl-`, etc., as opposed to the logical `ms-`/`me-`/`text-start` used correctly elsewhere in the app) found only one, cosmetic, non-breaking instance (`file:mr-4` on a file-input's pseudo-element). **There is no broken `dir` attribute or CSS override** — the direction genuinely is RTL at the container level. The reported "not RTL" is the visual symptom of item 46/47's core finding applied to a form instead of a page: a form that's 100% untranslated English text will always visually read left-to-right (Unicode bidi rules keep Latin-script words internally LTR) regardless of how correctly RTL the surrounding container is, which looks exactly like "the direction isn't working" even when it technically is.

**Fixed**: wrapped all ~90 distinct strings across the Customer/Vendor form, Product form (including its warehouse-extension and POS/image sub-sections), Category form, Unit of Measure form, and Warehouse form in `t()`. Wrote and applied real Arabic + Urdu for all of them — GL account labels, ZATCA buyer-identity fields (VAT/building/street/district/city/postal code), stock-threshold fields, file-size/dimension validation alert text, everything. Once these render in Arabic/Urdu, the form's RTL layout will follow correctly from the same container `dir` attribute that was already correct.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite **132/132** passing, DB-level confirmation all ~90 new keys have real `ar`/`ur` (0 missing after the fix). Live browser verification was attempted (fresh temp super-admin user, `uiLanguage: 'ar'`) but the Browser pane's `document.hidden` issue was present from the very first page load this time — even the login-to-dashboard transition itself never completed, worse than the partial blocking seen in items 46/47 — so no live click-through was possible this round at all. Rests on: `tsc`'s structural validation, the exact same `t()` mechanism already proven live in items 46/47 (Dashboard, sidebar, headings all confirmed rendering correct Arabic in this same session), and direct DB confirmation of translation content.

---

## 49. Transaction Forms (Quotation/Invoice/Expense) — Same Gap, Third Time (Aug 2026)

User asked directly: "did you check transactions forms as well?" — hadn't yet; checked immediately rather than assuming they were fine because they already imported `useTranslation`.

**Findings**: all three modules had the exact same split personality items 46-48 kept finding — `useTranslation()` imported and used correctly in *some* places (mostly status pills, fixed earlier in item 46), while entire forms sat right next to that working code completely untranslated:
- `QuotationModule.tsx`: the main create/edit form's totals card (Items Gross Subtotal / Line Item Discounts / Net Subtotal / Header Discount / Total Discount Applied / VAT Slab / Cancel button) was unwrapped. The **entire** Convert-to-Invoice modal form (~260 lines — customer/date/bank/tax-slab/discount/payment-status fields, the adjustable line-items table, its own totals card, Discard/Approve buttons) had zero `t()` wiring, same as the add-forms in item 48.
- `InvoiceModule.tsx`: same totals-card gap in the main create form, plus its Notes placeholder and file-attached message; the **Record Payment** modal and the **Create Credit/Debit Note** modal were both entirely untranslated.
- `ExpenseModule.tsx`: best-covered of the three going in, but still had the Disbursement Bank label/fallback, `(Default)` suffix, file-attached message, description placeholder, and the amount-label ternary unwrapped in the main form; its **Record Payment** modal (identical shape to Invoice's) was entirely untranslated.

Also directly addressed the user's separate, repeated question about the Category page: the entire List/Directory view in `MasterEntities.tsx` (item 48 only fixed the Add/Edit forms) — every table's column headers and cell fallback text across all six sub-tabs (Customers, Vendors, Products, **Categories**, Units, Warehouses), the "New X" / "Total: N records" toolbar, and the Restore Missing Products button — was still 100% hardcoded English.

**Fixed**: wrapped everything above in `t()` across all four files. Wrote and applied real Arabic + Urdu for the resulting 91 new keys (payment-modal labels, GL-mapping table headers, "Unmapped"/"-- Top Level --"/"Unregistered" fallback text, Yes/No, credit/debit note reason placeholders, etc.).

## 50. `ux-translation` Skill Created — Make This Stop Being a Recurring Discovery (Aug 2026)

Items 46-49 found the same shape of bug four separate times, in four different files, each discovered only because the user happened to ask about that specific page. Per explicit request: built a persistent skill (`.claude/skills/ux-translation/`) so translation wiring is applied while building any new page/form/report, not audited in after the fact.

**What it encodes** (full detail in the skill itself and its two reference docs):
- The actual architecture (`useTranslation`/`db.translations`/`SEED_TRANSLATIONS`/`register-missing-key`) and its real failure mode (auto-registered keys silently never get filled in).
- The two decisions every string needs: fixed vocabulary (translate) vs. tenant-authored data (never translate — pollutes the shared dictionary with unfixable one-off junk), and literal vs. dynamic `t()` calls (dynamic calls are invisible to a plain-text grep audit — this is exactly how item 47's `CATEGORY_GROUPS` label gap survived undetected).
- "Direction isn't RTL" is diagnosed as a translation-completeness question first, not a CSS/`dir`-attribute question — every real instance investigated across items 46-49 turned out to be 100% untranslated English rendering left-to-right inside a correctly-RTL container, not an actual layout bug.
- The specific recurring bugs this session hit and how each was caught: `t` shadowed by a `.map(t => ...)` loop variable (item 46), a duplicate DB row from an audit script that `.trim()`'d a key with load-bearing trailing whitespace (item 46), the in-app Gemini-backed "Auto-Translate" button being a non-functional dead end in this environment (item 46).
- A copy-pasteable, reusable audit script (extract → enumerate dynamic keys by hand → cross-reference DB → write real content → re-verify → clean up scratch files) — the same one used to actually execute items 46-49.

**Not verified**: whether the skill actually triggers correctly in a fresh session on a new-feature request (no new page has been built since it was written to test it against). Worth confirming the first time a real "add a new form" task comes in.

## 53. Dashboard Redesign — Pending Invoices/Expenses, Top Customers/Vendors, Wider Reporting Filters (Aug 2026)

User request, queued since earlier in the session and explicitly deferred until the translation sweep finished: "re-design the dashboard, it need to have all things related to sales, expense, pending invoices/expenses, make it beautiful with some good parameters to filter, interactive connect dashboard with professional look and feel." Delivered as a targeted enhancement of `Dashboard.tsx` (admin view) rather than a ground-up rewrite, to avoid destabilizing the existing, already-decent KPI/chart/P&L/targets machinery.

**What's new**:
- **Reporting period filter, widened**: the existing exact-month dropdown (single month or "All Months Combined") is now layered under a new `periodMode` state (`'month' | 'last3' | 'year'`) with two new quick-preset buttons, **Last 3 Months** and **This Year**, next to it. The dropdown's own behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged when `periodMode === 'month'` (the default) — the new modes are additive, computed via a `matchesPeriod(dateStr)` helper that every existing filter (`monthInvoices`/`monthExpenses`/`monthQuotations`/`monthVouchers`) now goes through instead of a raw `.startsWith(selectedMonthFilter)` check. The trend chart's single-month "Day 01/04/07…" seeding (`getDaysArray()`) is now skipped outside `'month'` mode, since seeding by day-of-month is meaningless once the range spans multiple calendar months.
- **Pending Invoices** panel: top 8 Active invoices with an outstanding balance (`grandTotal - amountPaid > 0`), oldest-first, each showing customer name, invoice number, amount due, and days outstanding (color-coded amber/rose past 14/30 days) — plus a running "Total Outstanding" footer and a "View All" link to the Invoices page. Deliberately **not** gated by the reporting-period filter, matching the existing `pendingCollection` KPI's own reasoning: money currently owed doesn't reset because the admin switched which month they're looking at.
- **Pending Expenses** panel: the mirror image for unpaid Actual vendor expenses, plus a small callout linking to Recurring & Accruals when accruals are awaiting settlement.
- **Top Customers by Sales** / **Top Vendors by Expense**: two horizontal bar charts (using `recharts`' `BarChart`/`Bar`, imported since an earlier pass but never actually wired into any chart until now), grouping the *period-filtered* invoices/expenses by customer/vendor and showing the top 5 by value — this one **does** respect the reporting period, since "who mattered this period" is a different question from "what's owed right now."
- **Polish**: staggered `motion.div` fade-in entrance on the KPI cards and new panels (the `motion` import from `motion/react` was already present in the file but completely unused before this pass). Also fixed one pre-existing untranslated string caught while in the file (`"CapEx Assets:"` label, now `t()`-wrapped).

**Scope decision**: the new panels are admin-only, inside the existing `isAdmin` branch — consistent with how the Partners' Equity section is already scoped, and because company-wide AR/AP and customer/vendor revenue concentration is admin-level business intelligence, not something a staff account should see (staff already only see their own quotations/sales in the existing split).

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite **132/132** passing throughout. Extracted every `t()` key touched in `Dashboard.tsx` and cross-referenced against the live `translations` table (5 keys with no row, 12 with an English-only row from an earlier auto-registration) — wrote real Arabic + Urdu for all 17, re-verified 0 missing. Live-verified via a throwaway admin user seeded into the "CNC Woodcraft & Design" demo company (real invoice/expense history) — confirmed via `read_page`/`get_page_text` (screenshot blocked by the same recurring Browser-pane compositing issue as items 48/51/52) that: the Pending Invoices panel lists 4 real outstanding invoices summing correctly to the Total Outstanding footer; the Pending Expenses panel correctly shows its empty state (this company has no unpaid Actual expenses this month); the Top Customers chart renders real customer names; and clicking "Last 3 Months" measurably changed the trend chart's x-axis (seeded day-labels disappeared, replaced by real dates), confirming `periodMode` genuinely drives the query rather than just toggling visually.

**Not done**: no live click-through of "This Year" beyond confirming the button renders and is wired (same handler pattern as "Last 3 Months", low incremental risk). No changes to the staff (non-admin) dashboard view.

## 54. Sidebar Nav Groups — Accordion Behavior, One Section Open at a Time (Aug 2026)

User request: clicking a nav item should collapse every other currently-expanded section, so only one group's sub-items are ever showing — the sidebar previously defaulted to three groups (POS, Sales, Procurements) open simultaneously (plus Master Registries/Inventory open by default too, since `expandedNavGroups[id] !== false` treats any id absent from the initial state object as open), which pushed the menu tall enough to require scrolling.

**Fixed** in `App.tsx`: derived `allNavGroupIds` (every nav item with `subItems`) and a `subItemToGroupId` lookup from `navSections`, both computed fresh each render so they can never drift from the actual menu structure.
- `toggleNavGroup(id)` (fired by clicking a group header): if the group is currently open, clicking it just closes that one group. If it's currently closed, opening it now first collapses every other group, then opens this one — true accordion, not independent toggles.
- `handleNavigate(tabId)` (fired by clicking a leaf/subItem): now also collapses every group except the one that owns the clicked item (looked up via `subItemToGroupId`) — so clicking into a subItem keeps its own parent group open (you just navigated there) but closes any other group that happened to be open. Clicking a top-level leaf with no subItems (Command Center, Financial Reports, Settings) collapses every group, since none of them need to stay open once you've navigated away entirely.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite **132/132** passing. Live-verified via a throwaway admin user (created and deleted via the same scratch-DB-script pattern as items 51/52/53) — `read_page` accessibility-tree checks confirmed all three behaviors: (1) closing an already-open group only closes that one, leaving siblings untouched; (2) opening a closed group collapses every other group first; (3) navigating to a subItem inside the currently-open group leaves that group open while others stay collapsed; (4) navigating to a top-level leaf (Command Center) collapses every group. Screenshot verification blocked by the same recurring Browser-pane `document.hidden` compositing issue as every prior item in this session — accessibility-tree inspection used as the fallback throughout.

## 56. Forgot Password (Email Reset Link) — SMTP, Mandatory Email, Reset Flow (Aug 2026)

User request: "forgot username" recovery that emails a password-reset link. Before building anything, checked what existed — nothing: no `email` column on `users`, no mail library, no SMTP config anywhere in `.env`. Asked the user which delivery method to wire up (SMTP vs. Resend vs. SendGrid vs. a dev-only console-logged link); they chose **SMTP**. Two follow-up directives shaped the design: "make sure if email exists, only then it will work" and "add email in user table, and make it mandatory."

**Schema** (`src/db/schema.ts`): added `users.email` (nullable at the DB level — see reasoning below) plus a new `password_reset_tokens` table (`userId`, `tokenHash`, `expiresAt`, `usedAt`, `createdAt`). Only the SHA-256 hash of the raw token is ever stored; the raw token exists only in the emailed URL. Pushed via `drizzle-kit push` — surfaced unrelated constraint-naming drift on three unrelated tables (`recurring_postings`, `physical_stock_take_items`, `purchase_requisition_items`) plus two composite-PK re-declarations, all confirmed harmless (same targets, just truncated-identifier-name normalization) by re-running the full suite immediately after (still 132/132).

**"Mandatory" reconciled with "nullable" — deliberately not a DB constraint**: a hard `NOT NULL` would have broken every one of the ~17 existing test files that insert a `users` row directly via `db.insert(schema.users).values(...)` (bypassing the real create-user route), plus every pre-existing account in the live DB. Instead, `email` stays nullable at the schema level, and is enforced as mandatory **at the application layer** — `POST /api/users` (the real account-creation/edit route, `server/routes/users.ts`) now 400s on a missing or malformed email, and the Admin Settings → Users form (`AdminSettings.tsx`) requires it client-side too. This is also exactly what makes "if email exists, only then it will work" true by construction: an account created before this change, or inserted directly by a test fixture, simply has no email and the forgot-password flow silently no-ops for it (by design, not by accident).

**Backend** (`server.ts`, new `server/lib/mailer.ts`): `POST /api/auth/forgot-password` and `POST /api/auth/reset-password`, registered before the `isAuthenticated` gate (same pattern as `/api/login`). Forgot-password always returns the same generic message regardless of whether the username exists or has an email — a deliberate account-enumeration guard — with one disclosed exception: if SMTP itself isn't configured at all, that's global server config, not a per-account secret, so it 503s with a clear message instead of pretending to work. Reset-password validates the token (exists, unexpired, unused), hashes and sets the new password, marks the token used, and logs both `PASSWORD_RESET_REQUESTED`/`PASSWORD_RESET_COMPLETED` to the audit trail. `.env` got a documented SMTP block (`SMTP_HOST`/`PORT`/`USER`/`PASSWORD`/`FROM`, plus optional `APP_BASE_URL`) — left **blank**, with inline instructions (e.g. Gmail App Passwords) for the user to fill in themselves; no credentials were fabricated or requested in chat.

**Frontend**: `LoginScreen.tsx` gained a "Forgot password?" link and an inline request-reset form (username → generic confirmation/error). New `ResetPasswordScreen.tsx`, rendered by `App.tsx` whenever the URL carries `?resetToken=...` (checked ahead of the logged-in/login branch, so it works regardless of session state) — new-password + confirm form, posts to the reset endpoint, clears the URL param via `history.replaceState` on completion. Both screens are deliberately English-only, matching `LoginScreen.tsx`'s own pre-existing pattern — there's no `db`/translations context available this early in the app lifecycle (pre-login), so this isn't a new gap, just consistency with what was already there. `AdminSettings.tsx`'s Users tab gained an "Account Email" field (create/edit form) and shows each account's email (or an amber "No email on file — password reset unavailable" flag) in the staff directory — those strings *are* translated, written and applied to the `translations` table.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean. New `tests/passwordReset.test.ts` (9 tests, all passing): the 503 "not configured" guard (a real assertion about this dev environment's actual blank `.env`, not a simulated condition); reset-password rejecting a garbage token, a too-short password, an expired token, and an already-used token; and — the core path — a validly-issued token (inserted directly via the DB, the same no-mocks-but-bypass-the-unreachable-email-step pattern used elsewhere in this suite) actually changing the password, confirmed by both a direct `bcrypt.compare` and a real login attempt with the old password (fails) and new password (succeeds). Plus 3 tests on `POST /api/users`' new mandatory-email validation (missing, malformed, valid-and-persisted). Full suite **141/141** passing (132 previous + 9 new). Restarted the dev server before running any of this, per this file's own Commands-section warning about the backend not hot-reloading — this session touched `server.ts`, a schema file, and a route file. Live-verified the full click-path in the browser (forgot-password link → request form → real 503 response rendered → `?resetToken=` URL → reset form → real "invalid token" response rendered → Back to Login clearing the URL param) using direct JS `.click()`/`.requestSubmit()` dispatch after the Browser pane's `computer` tool intermittently failed to register clicks — confirmed via `document.hidden === true` that this was the same recurring compositing bug noted throughout items 31/33/44/48/51/52/54/55, not a real bug in the new code. The Admin Settings Users-tab email field was verified via `tsc` + the passing `POST /api/users` tests only; live click-through of that specific screen hit the same compositing issue before it could be reached.

**Not done / explicitly deferred**: the actual "email gets delivered" happy path is unverified end-to-end, since this dev environment has no real SMTP credentials — that's the one piece only the user can complete themselves (fill in `.env`'s `SMTP_*` block, then request a reset for an account with an email on file). No rate-limiting on `/api/auth/forgot-password` (an attacker could spam an account's inbox with reset links, or hammer the endpoint) — worth adding if this ever faces the public internet rather than an internal ERP's login page.

## 57. Pre-Production Architect Review — Concurrency & Transaction Isolation (Aug 2026)

User asked for a final architect-level review focused specifically on concurrent-user and transaction-isolation safety before deploying this dev environment to production, and wanted an honest confirmation, not a rubber stamp. Investigated the actual code (not memory/assumption) across the highest-stakes write paths: document-number counters, the ZATCA hash-chain reservation, inventory stock updates, the session store, the DB connection pool, and the legacy blob-sync path.

**What was already solid, verified in code**:
- `getAndIncrementCounter` (`server/lib/businessLogic.ts`) — real `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the company row, inside a transaction, before every invoice/quotation/expense/voucher number is minted. Correctly serializes same-company concurrent creates; different companies never block each other.
- ZATCA hash-chain reservation (`processInvoiceZatca`) — same per-company row lock, held only across the fast XML-build/signing step, released before the slow network call. The code carries a first-hand comment describing the exact fork bug this was built to fix ("reproduced live: two invoices... landed on the identical ICV") — a real incident, genuinely fixed.
- Inventory stock quantity updates (`server/routes/inventory.ts`) — `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` before every stock delta, with an explicit "read-then-write without a lock would let both read the same starting quantity" comment.
- Tenant isolation in the generic sync path (`src/db/migrateData.ts`'s `scopeAndAuthorizeRecords`) — per-record ownership checks, with real incident history behind them (a shared tax-slab corruption bug, a company-resurrection bug, both already fixed with comments explaining exactly what went wrong).

**Two real bugs found and fixed in this pass**:

1. **Session store's fallback was a one-way door.** `ResilientSessionStore` (`server.ts`) fell back from Postgres to in-memory sessions on the *first* transient DB error and never recovered — every session for that process's remaining lifetime silently stopped persisting to Postgres. Beyond the confusing "randomly logged out" symptom, this breaks horizontal scaling outright: a second app instance shares nothing with the first's in-memory sessions, so a request landing on a different instance sees the user as logged out. **Fixed**: replaced the permanent `useBackup` latch with a 10-second recovery cooldown — after a failure, the next operation past that window gets a real retry against Postgres instead of assuming it's still down, and a success flips it back automatically (logged). The one genuinely-permanent case left is construction failure (`pgStore` never created at all) — there's nothing to retry there, so staying on `MemoryStore` for that case is correct. Documented the one real remaining tradeoff in a comment: a session created during a fallback window doesn't exist in `pgStore`, so it ends when recovery flips back — bounded to the outage window now, not the rest of the process's life.

2. **A real race between Cancel and the fire-and-forget ZATCA submission.** `processInvoiceZatca` fetched the invoice with a plain, unlocked `SELECT`, then — moments later, after other work — unconditionally wrote `zatcaStatus: 'SUBMITTING'`. If `POST /invoices/:id/cancel` committed a cancellation in that gap, the SUBMITTING write (and everything after it — hash-chain reservation, a real ZATCA network submission) proceeded anyway on an invoice that was already `Cancelled`. **Fixed** on both sides so the fix holds regardless of which request actually reaches the row first: the SUBMITTING transition in `processInvoice.ts` is now a single conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE id = ? AND status != 'Cancelled' RETURNING id` — Postgres evaluates that atomically against the row's *current* state, not the stale in-memory copy, so a zero-row result means a concurrent cancel won and the function returns immediately, before ever reserving a hash-chain position; and the cancel route (`server/routes/transactions.ts`) now takes `.for('update')` on its own read of the invoice, so it genuinely waits out an in-flight SUBMITTING write (which itself holds an implicit Postgres row lock for its duration) instead of racing a stale copy of `zatcaStatus`.

**Explicitly not bundled into this pass** (flagged, not silently skipped):
- **Postgres RLS** — still absent, already tracked in this file's Deferred/Backlog section below with its own architectural reasoning (needs a session-scoped Postgres variable wired into every request's connection, which doesn't fit the current shared-pool pattern without real rework). Not a same-day bug fix; a separate initiative.
- **Classifying the remaining `/api/migrate` blob-sync call sites** (found live references in `AdminSettings`, `InventoryModule`, `InvoiceModule`, `MasterEntities`, `PosModule`, `QuotationModule`) — this needs a one-by-one read of what's actually financially-critical versus harmless UI-state sync before deciding what to migrate to real transactional routes; offered as a distinct next step, not done in this pass.
- **DB pool sizing** (`max: 20` by default in `src/db/index.ts`) — not a bug, already env-configurable (`DB_POOL_MAX`); a capacity-planning decision tied to actual deployment topology (single instance vs. horizontal scaling), not something to "fix" in code.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean. New `tests/invoiceZatcaCancelRace.test.ts` (2 tests): reproduces the exact DB state a "cancel wins the race" interleaving would produce (invoice pre-set to `status: 'Cancelled'`, `zatcaEnabled: true` on a throwaway company) and confirms `processInvoiceZatca` refuses with the new guard's error, `zatcaStatus` stays untouched, and no hash-chain position (`icv`/`previousInvoiceHash`) is claimed — proven without needing real ZATCA sandbox credentials, since the guard aborts before any XML-building or network call. A second test confirms a non-cancelled invoice is not blocked by the new guard specifically (asserted by checking the error isn't the cancellation-race message, since this throwaway company's incomplete buyer/cert fixtures legitimately fail a different, pre-existing, unrelated validation gate further down — which the test deliberately doesn't try to route around). Full suite **143/143** passing (141 previous + 2 new). Restarted the dev server before any of this, per this file's own Commands-section warning — this pass touched `server.ts` and two files under `server/lib/zatca/` and `server/routes/`. The session-store fix has no practical integration-test coverage (would require simulating a genuine Postgres connection failure mid-suite); correctness rests on `tsc` and direct code review of the cooldown/retry logic, disclosed here rather than claimed as tested.

---

## 58. Classified the Six Blob-Sync Call Sites, Fixed Partial Payments, Removed a Broken Truncate Button (Aug 2026)

Direct follow-up to item 57's "I did not have time to classify which of those six files' usages are financially-critical" — user asked to actually go do that classification and fix whatever needed fixing, "make sure functionality is intact and no bugs are introduced."

**The classification** (grep'd every real `onUpdateDb(`/`onUpdateDbLocal(`/`fetch('/api/migrate')` call site in the six flagged files, not just file-level imports): four of the six — `InventoryModule.tsx`, `MasterEntities.tsx`, `PosModule.tsx`, `QuotationModule.tsx` — turned out to call **only** `onUpdateDbLocal`, the setState-only counterpart that never touches the network at all. None of those were ever the risk item 57 was warning about; they were caught by the original broad file-level grep, not the actual risky pattern. The real surface was two files:

- **`InvoiceModule.tsx`** — one real site: `handleConfirmPay` ("Receive Payment"), exactly the one BACKLOG already named.
- **`AdminSettings.tsx`** — four real sites: `handleForcePushToCloud`, `handleImportFile`, `handleImportPasted` (all `fetch('/api/migrate')` directly with a full backup blob), and `handleTruncateTransactions` (`onUpdateDb`).

**Fixed — partial payments** (the one BACKLOG already explained the blocker for): extended `POST /invoices/:id/paid` (`server/routes/transactions.ts`) to accept an optional `amount`. Omitting it keeps every existing caller's behavior byte-identical (settle the full remaining balance) — confirmed by re-running the pre-existing test file unchanged before touching anything else. Passing `amount` settles only that much, computing the new `paymentStatus` via the shared `computePaymentStatus` helper. Deliberately **not** built on `syncVoucherForInvoice` (which upserts exactly one Receipt voucher per invoice, correct for cancellation/credit-note reversal but wrong here) — every settlement now inserts its own standalone voucher via `getAndIncrementCounter`, matching `src/dbStore.ts`'s legacy `markInvoicePaid` behavior exactly, which is what makes migrating the frontend off it safe. `InvoiceModule.tsx`'s "Receive Payment" modal now calls this route directly (same refetch-then-`onUpdateDbLocal` pattern as the already-migrated `handleCancelInvoice` beside it) instead of the local `dbStore` path; the now-unused `markInvoicePaid` import was removed.

**Found and fixed a second, more serious bug along the way**: `handleTruncateTransactions` filtered the target company's quotations/invoices/expenses/vouchers out of the *client's local array* and pushed that filtered blob to `/api/migrate` — but `migrateDataToPostgres` (`src/db/migrateData.ts`) has exactly one `DELETE` in the entire file (a `userRoles` junction-table sync) and is otherwise purely an upsert (`INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`). It has no mechanism to delete a row that's merely *absent* from a payload. This means the "Truncate & Reset Transaction Data" button never actually deleted anything server-side — the local UI would show an empty list until the next `/api/state` refetch brought every "truncated" record right back. User's call once this was surfaced: remove the feature from the UI entirely rather than build a real destructive bulk-delete admin route for what's labeled a dev/testing-only tool. Removed the card, its confirmation modal, the handler function, and the now-unused `isConfirmingTruncate` state.

**Left alone, explicitly**: `handleForcePushToCloud` and the two `Import Database` handlers still push a full blob through `/api/migrate` directly — correctly classified as legitimate, explicit, rare, admin-initiated whole-database operations (force a resync; restore a backup) where "overwrite the server with what I currently have" *is* the intended semantic, not an accidental side effect of a routine save. There's no sensible per-record REST route to migrate a backup-restore operation to, so these were left as-is rather than forced into a pattern that doesn't fit them.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. New tests added to `tests/invoicePaidRoute.test.ts` (6 tests): a partial payment leaves the invoice `Partially Paid` with the correct balance; a second partial payment posts a **second, separate** voucher instead of overwriting the first (the core thing being fixed) and completes the invoice to `Paid`; an over-amount payment is rejected with the remaining balance in the error; a zero/negative amount is rejected; paying an already-fully-paid invoice is rejected; paying a cancelled invoice is rejected. All 4 pre-existing tests in that file re-run and confirmed passing unchanged both before and after the route extension. Full suite **149/149** passing (143 previous + 6 new). Restarted the dev server before any of this — this pass touched `server/routes/transactions.ts` and `src/components/InvoiceModule.tsx`/`AdminSettings.tsx`. Live browser click-through of the "Receive Payment" modal was attempted but blocked by the same recurring `document.hidden` Browser-pane compositing issue noted throughout this session (confirmed via direct check, not assumed) — rests on the passing integration tests (which exercise the exact same route the frontend now calls) plus direct code review of the rewritten handler against the already-verified `handleCancelInvoice` pattern beside it in the same file.

## 59. Real Production-Build Verification (Aug 2026)

Prompted by the user directly questioning how much confidence "tests pass" should actually carry, given several bugs this session were only found on a later, differently-angled review pass, not the first one. Agreed on a plan (documented in this session's own transcript, not restated here) that included: never treat a review pass as exhaustive, keep growing regression-test coverage, stage before a real cutover, wire up real monitoring, and — the concrete next action — actually run and verify the real production build/start path (`npm run build && npm start`) instead of only ever having tested against `npm run dev`.

**What that turned up, immediately**: `npm run build` and `npm start` both failed outright on this Windows environment, because npm shells out `package.json` scripts through `cmd.exe` regardless of which terminal invoked `npm` — the scripts' Unix-style `NODE_ENV=production <command>` prefix syntax isn't valid `cmd.exe` syntax and errors with "not recognized as an internal or external command." Worked around by running the two build steps' underlying commands directly (`vite build`, `esbuild ...`) with `NODE_ENV` exported through a POSIX shell instead of through the npm-wrapped script string. This is a real, environment-specific finding worth fixing in `package.json` itself (e.g. via `cross-env`) if this project is ever built directly with `npm run build` on Windows rather than through whatever CI/deploy pipeline ends up used — not fixed in this pass since it's a build-tooling compatibility issue, not an application bug, and the user's actual deploy path may not be Windows/npm-script-based at all.

**A second, more important finding, caught by the same discipline just discussed**: after building successfully, the first attempt to run the production server (`node dist/server.cjs`) started without `NODE_ENV=production` actually set in that process's own environment — `NODE_ENV=production` at *build* time (the esbuild command) does not bake anything into the bundle; `server.ts` reads `process.env.NODE_ENV` at *runtime* (`if (process.env.NODE_ENV !== "production")`) to decide whether to boot Vite's dev middleware or serve the static `dist/` bundle. The first run silently fell back to dev mode — same host, same port, looked identical from a `curl /api/health` check, and would have passed as "verified" on a less careful check. Caught it by inspecting the actual `<script src>` the browser loaded (`/@vite/client` + raw `/src/main.tsx` — dev-mode paths — instead of a bundled `/assets/index-*.js`), not by trusting the server's own claim that it started. Restarted with `NODE_ENV=production` genuinely exported into the process, then re-verified the same way and got the real bundled asset.

**Verified, against the actually-correct production process this time**: full suite **149/149** passing over real HTTP against the production `server.cjs` (not `tsx server.ts`) with the real built client bundle served, not Vite middleware. Confirmed via direct script-src inspection that the served page is the genuine minified bundle. No console errors beyond the expected pre-login "Unauthorized" 401 (normal for an unauthenticated `/api/state` probe, present in both dev and prod). Stopped the production process afterward and restarted the normal `npm run dev` server so the environment is back in its expected day-to-day state — this was a one-off verification, not meant to leave a production process running in the dev sandbox.

**Why this matters beyond just this one check**: it's a live example of the exact failure mode being discussed — an initial pass ("server started, health check returned 200") looked completely clean and would have been reported as "production build verified" if the check had stopped there. The bug (silently running in dev mode) was only caught by verifying the *actual observable behavior* the check was supposed to confirm (which JS bundle the browser loaded), not the proxy signal (process exit code, HTTP 200). Worth keeping as the standing bar for any future "verify X" task: confirm the specific thing that was asked about, not just an adjacent signal that's usually correlated with it.

---

## 60. Create/Read/Update/Delete Permission Model (Aug 2026)

Prompted by the user asking whether the permission model actually supported assigning Create/Read/Update/Delete independently when building a Role, and whether adding a new page's permission wiring was "dynamic" enough to not require hand-touching several files in sync. An initial narrow audit (grepping only for the `hasPermission()` helper) wrongly claimed a broad server-side enforcement gap — corrected immediately once a fuller audit (also matching direct `permissions.x.y.enabled` property access) showed nearly every route was in fact already properly gated. The two findings that *did* hold up: (1) the model only ever offered View + one combined write leaf (`create` or `edit`) per module, never a real Delete distinct from Create/Update; (2) the wiring was not dynamic — adding a module meant hand-editing three separate branches of `normalizePermissions()` (admin/non-admin/no-role-assigned defaults) plus the Roles-editor tree by hand, and that exact drift had already caused a real bug once before (a module silently defaulting to full access for a user with zero roles assigned, because the "no permissions object" branch wasn't kept in sync with the other two).

**Built, in order:**
- **`src/permissionSchema.ts`** (new) — single registry (`PERMISSION_MODULES`) that both `normalizePermissions()` (now a ~20-line generic loop instead of ~180 lines of triplicated object literals) and the Roles editor's checkbox tree (`buildPermissionTree()`) derive from. Adding a new module is now one registry entry instead of four hand-synced locations.
- Full **create/read/update/delete** leaves for every module that manages records: `quotation`, `invoice`, `expense`, `customers`, `vendors`, `products`, `banks`, `taxSlabs`, plus the hidden (no dedicated checkbox, inherits from `products`) `categories`/`units`/`warehouses`. Feature-access modules that were never CRUD-shaped to begin with (`pos.*`, `inventory.*`, `investors.access`, `fiscalMonths.access`, `reports.access`) were deliberately left as flat access flags rather than force-fit into C/R/U/D.
- **D means Cancel for documents, Active/Inactive toggle for master data — never a hard delete.** The old global `cancel.access` module (one flag gating cancellation of every document type at once) was retired, replaced by each document's own `.delete` leaf (`invoice.delete`, `expense.delete`, `quotation.delete`) — a role can now be scoped to cancel one document type without the others.
- **Schema migration**: added `isActive: boolean` (default `true`) to `customers`, `vendors`, `productsServices`, `productCategories`, `unitsOfMeasure` (via `db:push`, confirmed additive-only, no data loss). `warehouses` already had this column but its delete route never used it — fixed as part of the same pass. All five now use `PATCH /api/<resource>/:id/toggle-active` instead of a hard `DELETE`, which also removed the reference-integrity guard code the old hard-delete routes needed (deactivating a customer with existing invoices needs no guard; deleting one did).
- **Quotations got a genuine Cancel action for the first time** — previously the frontend already had a `canCancel` gate referencing a permission, but no real cancel route existed; "cancelling" silently reused the generic edit route to write `status: 'Cancelled'` into the same field tracking Draft/Sent/Accepted/Converted phase. Added `quotations.isCancelled: boolean` (dedicated flag, default `false`) and `POST /quotations/:id/cancel`, keeping `status` as a pure phase field going forward. `PUT /quotations/:id` and the convert-to-invoice route both now also reject a cancelled quotation regardless of phase.
- **Upsert routes now branch Create vs. Update by whether the row already exists** (`customers`, `vendors`, `products`, `categories`, `units`, `warehouses`, `expenses` all fetch the existing row first) — previously a single `create`-only check gated the whole upsert, meaning a role meant to only add new records could silently edit existing ones through the same endpoint. Editing a deactivated/cancelled row is rejected regardless of permission (the "record-state" rule, separate from the "role" rule).
- **Closed a real ZATCA-adjacent gap**: `POST /invoices` upserts by id, and nothing previously stopped that path from silently rewriting a CLEARED/REPORTED invoice's line items outside the properly-guarded cancel route. Added the same `SUBMITTING`/`CLEARED`/`REPORTED` refusal there — a hard rule independent of permission, matching `CLAUDE.md`'s ZATCA-immutability requirement.
- **Migrated the two existing live Role rows** (`Sales_Agnet`, `SALES_AGENT` — real data, backed up before the write) from the old `view`/`create`/`edit` shape to the new `create`/`read`/`update`/`delete` shape: `view`→`read` and `edit`→`create`+`update` carried over 1:1, the brand-new `delete` leaf defaults to `false` (fail-closed) for every pre-existing role rather than silently granting it. Verified post-migration via `normalizePermissions()` directly against both rows.
- **New skill**: `.claude/skills/permission-crud-model/SKILL.md` — documents the registry as single source of truth, the C/R/U/D semantics (including the two-layer role-vs-record-state rule), and the required backend enforcement pattern, so this convention gets applied consistently to future permission-gated routes rather than re-litigated per PR.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout every stage. New `tests/permissionCrudModel.test.ts` (7 tests, real HTTP + real Postgres, no mocks): a role-scoped user with `delete:false` gets 403 on the toggle-active route, `delete:true` gets 200 and actually flips `isActive`; editing a deactivated customer is rejected regardless of `update` permission; `create` alone doesn't grant editing an existing row and `update` alone doesn't grant creating a new one; cancelling a quotation sets `isCancelled` without touching `status`, blocks a second cancel, and is blocked entirely without `quotation.delete`; an admin with full permissions still can't edit a CLEARED invoice. Full suite **156/156** passing (149 previous + 7 new) against a freshly restarted dev server (this pass touched `server/routes/*.ts` extensively). One transient failure was observed mid-session and traced to a stray, unrelated background `grep` command competing for resources at that exact moment — three clean re-runs immediately after confirmed it wasn't a logic bug.

**Left alone, explicitly**: `taxSlabs` has no `delete` leaf — there's no mechanism to remove/deactivate a tax slab today (only set-default exists), so no leaf was invented for an action that doesn't exist. Invoices/expenses/purchase-orders keep overloading their existing `status` field with a `'Cancelled'` value rather than being retrofitted onto a dedicated flag like quotations got — that pattern is stable, heavily exercised by the ZATCA pipeline, and rewriting it is separate, higher-risk work explicitly deferred (see below).

---

## 61. Extending the CRUD Model — Delegating What Used to Be Admin-Tier-Only, Plus Non-CRUD Action Permissions (Aug 2026)

Direct follow-up to item 60, prompted by the user asking which other admin-only capabilities (starting with "enroll a new employee" and "give tax/months to a Finance Head") should become genuinely delegable to a non-admin Role rather than staying an all-admin-or-nothing gate. Went through every remaining `isAdminUser()`/`isSuperAdminUser()`/`role === 'admin'` gate in `server/routes/` as a full sweep, not just the two examples given, and made an explicit call on each.

**Made delegable (new registry modules/leaves in `src/permissionSchema.ts`):**
- **`users`** (create/read/update/delete) — staff enrollment and management. The one genuinely new safety mechanism this required: a non-admin-tier actor reaching `POST /api/users` only via the `users.create`/`users.update` permission (not actual `role: 'admin'`) is now server-side *forced* to `role: 'user'`, regardless of what the request body asks for — closes what would otherwise be a privilege-escalation path (grant yourself admin via the new "add employees" permission). Assigning an *existing* Role to a new hire is still allowed for a delegated actor; only minting a new admin-tier account is blocked. Verified with a dedicated test that explicitly requests `role: 'admin', isSuperAdmin: true` and asserts the created row is `role: 'user'` regardless.
- **`templates`** (create/read/update/delete) — Canvas Designer / document print layout. Judged low-risk: doesn't touch financial data or the ZATCA submission pipeline, a misconfigured template is a print-appearance problem, not a data-integrity one.
- **`inventory.approve`** — split off from `inventory.pr` (which only ever covered submitting/viewing requisitions). Real segregation-of-duties: the person requesting shouldn't automatically be the one approving. Was previously a hard `isAdminUser()` gate on `PATCH /purchase-requisitions/:id/status` with no Role-based path at all.
- **`fiscalMonths.open` / `fiscalMonths.close`** (replacing the single `fiscalMonths.access` flag) — opening a month is low-risk/reversible; closing one locks the entire accounting period and cascades into recurring-template settlement, a materially bigger and harder-to-reverse action. Viewing the current month stays universal and ungated (Dashboard/POS depend on it) — it was never actually gated by `access` in the first place.
- **`banks.transfer`** (alongside existing create/read/update/delete) — split off from `update`. Editing a bank account's name/number is metadata editing; moving real money between two accounts is an actual financial transaction, not the same authority.

**Explicitly kept admin-tier-only, not delegated (with reasoning, so this doesn't get re-litigated per-PR):** company creation (`POST /companies` — platform-owner action); Role definition (`roles.ts` — the "who sets the boundaries" layer shouldn't be delegable to something the boundaries apply to); ZATCA configuration (most compliance-sensitive code in the repo, real legal exposure); Translations (discovered mid-review to be a genuinely shared, cross-tenant table with no `companyId` at all — a company-scoped Role granted edit access here would affect every other tenant's UI text, not just its own company). A proposed `companySettings` module (Inventory-module toggle + POS settings) was designed, then deliberately dropped after the user correctly pushed back that it wasn't redundant with PR/PO/GRN permissions but also wasn't something anyone had actually asked to delegate — built for a real need, not a completeness impulse.

**Closed off, not left half-working**: `src/db/migrateData.ts`'s generic blob-sync path used to also handle `data.months` (guarded by the now-retired `fiscalMonths.access`). Confirmed the real UI (`AdminSettings.tsx`'s `handleOpenMonth`/`handleConfirmClose`) already exclusively calls the dedicated `POST /api/transactions/months` route directly — the blob-sync branch was dead weight from the real UI's perspective, and a coarse OR-style permission check on it couldn't have distinguished open from close per-record anyway. Removed the branch entirely rather than patching it to a permissive fallback, per explicit direction ("no sync, only dedicated api call for separate operation").

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. `tests/permissionCrudModel.test.ts` extended with 7 more tests (14 total in that file): delegated `users.create` forced to `role: 'user'` even when the request explicitly asks for `role: 'admin', isSuperAdmin: true`; `users.create=false` blocks enrollment; full templates create/read/update/delete cycle through real HTTP; `inventory.pr` alone does *not* grant PR approval, `inventory.approve` does; `fiscalMonths.open` doesn't grant closing a month and vice versa; `banks.update` alone does *not* grant an interbank transfer, `banks.transfer` does. Full suite **163/163** passing (156 previous + 7 new) against a freshly restarted dev server.

**Known gap at the time, since closed in item 62**: the Settings panel's frontend was still hard-gated to admin-only when this item shipped — see item 62 for the fix.

---

## 62. Settings Panel Made Permission-Aware — No Longer One Admin-Only Screen (Aug 2026)

Direct follow-up to item 61's closing gap: the new delegable permissions (`users`, `templates`, and the already-delegable `banks`/`taxSlabs`/`fiscalMonths`/`investors`) were enforced correctly server-side but had no UI path — the whole Settings shell was hard-gated to `isAdmin` at both the sidebar nav item (`App.tsx`, `adminOnly: true`) and the component render (`activeTab === 'settings' && isAdmin`). A delegated non-admin user could only reach these permissions via a direct API call.

**`App.tsx`**: added a `canAny(key: string | string[])` helper (the `permissionKey` field on a nav item/sub-item can now be a single string or an array — visible if *any* one matches, needed because "can reach Settings at all" isn't one single permission). The `settings` nav item now declares `permissionKey: SETTINGS_ACCESS_PERMISSIONS` (the full list of every permission that unlocks at least one sub-tab) instead of `adminOnly: true`; the render gate uses the same list. Admins still see it via the existing isAdmin-bypass inside `normalizePermissions()` — no separate admin check needed at this layer.

**`AdminSettings.tsx`**: `CATEGORY_GROUPS`' sub-tabs (13 of them, across 5 category groups) now each declare exactly one of `superAdminOnly` / `adminOnly` / `requiredPermission` (string or array) — a new `isTabVisible()` helper reads whichever is set and **fails closed** for any sub-tab that declares none of the three (defaults to admin-only, never to "visible to everyone," so a future tab added without a marker can only be too restrictive, not too open). Applied to both layout modes (Classic tab bar and Sleek Hub sidebar), which previously only filtered `superAdminOnly` and otherwise showed every tab unconditionally. The component's initial `activeTab` state now picks the first sub-tab the current user can actually see, falling back from a requested `defaultTab` the user turns out not to have access to, instead of hardcoding `'company'` (which is itself admin-only and would have stranded a delegated user on a blank/forbidden tab).

Final tab-by-tab gating: **Companies Directory** and **Translations** stay `superAdminOnly` (unchanged). **Company Profile**, **ZATCA**, **POS Terminal Settings**, **Roles**, **Database Backup & Sync** stay `adminOnly` — no delegable permission exists for any of these on purpose (see item 61's reasoning; POS Terminal Settings shares its route with Company Profile, and no `companySettings` permission was built). **Bank Accounts** → `banks.read`, **Tax Slabs** → `taxSlabs.read`, **Document Templates** → `templates.read`, **Staff Permissions** → `users.read`, **Month Opening/Closing** → `['fiscalMonths.open', 'fiscalMonths.close']` (either), **Capital & Equity** → `investors.access`.

**Inside each newly-reachable tab**, a full audit (separate pass, since these tabs had literally never been rendered for a non-admin before and could still assume `isAdmin === true` internally) confirmed/fixed every Add/Edit/Deactivate/Delete/Transfer/Open/Close button against its matching leaf: Banks (create/update/delete/transfer all correctly split), Templates (create/update/delete), Users (create/update/delete, plus hid the "Company Admin" role option from the Add Staff form for delegated non-admin-tier actors — the server already force-overrides it to `role: 'user'`, so offering the option in the UI was misleading even though harmless), Fiscal Months (open vs. close correctly split), Investors (left as a single tab-level gate — `investors.access` was never split into CRUD, and every mutating route the tab calls checks that same flag, so a redundant per-button check would add nothing).

**A real bug found along the way, fixed at the source**: `taxes` tab's Add-Slab form was originally gated on `taxSlabs.update` (matching what `POST /tax-slabs` actually checked) rather than `.create` — because that route's original rename in item 60/61 was a straight `edit→update` rename (done via a mechanical find-replace) rather than the full create-vs-update branch every other CRUD module got. This made `taxSlabs.create` a dead, no-op checkbox in the Roles editor — checking it granted nothing. Fixed the route itself (`server/routes/masterEntities.ts`) to branch by whether the submitted id already exists, same pattern as customers/vendors/products/etc., then reverted the frontend workaround back to the correct `.create` gate now that the backend actually supports it.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. Three new regression tests in `tests/permissionCrudModel.test.ts` covering exactly the tax-slabs fix: `taxSlabs.create=true/update=false` can add a new slab, `create=false/update=true` cannot, and editing an *existing* slab requires `update` specifically (not `create`). Full suite **166/166** passing (163 previous + 3 new) against a freshly restarted dev server.
