## 63. Translation Gap in This Session's Own New Work, Then a Broader Sweep of the Same Files (Aug 2026)

Prompted by the user directly asking whether the `ux-translation` skill had actually been applied to everything built in items 60-62, given this app's standing rule that new UI text is wired for translation as it's written, not as a follow-up. It hadn't. Root cause, stated plainly rather than deflected: item 60-62's work was done across a very long session focused on permission-model correctness (CRUD semantics, the anti-escalation guardrail, segregation-of-duties splits, the Settings-panel restructuring), and the `ux-translation` skill was never invoked at the point new label/dialog text was being written — including by the three background agents used for the MasterEntities.tsx migration and the Settings-tab audit, none of which were briefed on it. This is the same failure class the skill's own `references/known-bugs.md` was built from (a dynamic key invisible to a literal-string grep), just a new instance of it.

**Confirmed bug**: the Roles editor's permission tree rendered `{node.label}` completely unwrapped — every one of the ~90 permission labels from `src/permissionSchema.ts` (item 60's registry) displayed in English regardless of the admin's selected UI language, with no `t()` call anywhere in the render path. `PermissionTree`/`TreeNode` didn't even receive a `t` function as a prop — the whole component had no translation access at all.

**Fixed**: threaded `t` into `PermissionTree` (new required prop, passed from the Roles editor's existing `useTranslation` call) and wrapped `node.label` — this alone covers every module and leaf label in the tree, current and future, since the tree is generated from the registry rather than hand-built. Also wrapped the tree UI's own chrome text ("Permissions Tree", "Expand All", the subtitle) while in the same component.

**Then swept every file touched by items 60-62** (`MasterEntities.tsx`, `QuotationModule.tsx`, `InvoiceModule.tsx`, `ExpenseModule.tsx`, `InventoryModule.tsx`, `AdminSettings.tsx`'s Users handlers) for the same class of gap, prompted by the user reporting "some pages don't change [language]" after the first fix. Found and fixed: all 6 of the new toggle-active confirm dialogs (one per master-data type) plus the product-warehouse-mapping removal confirm; a Master Registries "Access Restricted" empty-state (new, gated by the item-62 `.read` permissions) with its heading and body text; six inconsistently-worded permission-error messages (`'Admin only.'`, `'Only Administrators can deactivate categories.'`, etc. — also factually stale now that these are delegable, not just untranslated) standardized to one accurate, translated string; action-row button titles/labels and two payment-recording modal messages across the Quotation/Invoice/Expense modules (mostly pre-existing gaps this pass happened to touch, not newly introduced — fixed anyway since they were found while already in the file); the Users tab's toggle-active/delete handlers' confirm dialogs and all their error/success toasts.

**Explicitly out of scope, flagged rather than silently fixed**: four `window.confirm()` calls in `AdminSettings.tsx`'s Canvas Designer code (unsaved-layout-changes warning, reset-to-default confirm, permanent-block-removal confirm) — genuinely pre-existing, unrelated to anything touched in items 60-62, not swept in this pass to keep it bounded to what this session's own work actually touched. Real gap, tracked below.

**A design question surfaced mid-fix, resolved without code changes**: whether permission keys/module identifiers should be redesigned around database-stored UUIDs instead of the current human-readable strings (`quotation.create`) — raised as "is this the same kind of gap as the translation issue." Concluded no: permission keys are never rendered to a user in any language (only the separate, now-translated `label` field is), so this isn't a translation gap. A related but distinct question (should permission *identity* be UUID-based for rename-safety, should the module/permission catalog itself live in a database table for a 50+-module future) was discussed and explicitly decided against — string keys colocated with their enforcement code remain the right call at this app's target scale (thousands of users, 50+ modules is still a tiny, static catalog; AWS IAM runs vastly larger permission surfaces the same way), and a DB-driven catalog would add a request-path query with no corresponding reduction in the actual (irreducible) work of adding a page. No architecture change made; this is recorded here so the reasoning doesn't need re-deriving if asked again.

**Verified**: 97 permission-tree keys + 46 more across the swept files (143 total new/fixed translation rows, real professional Arabic and Urdu written directly, not the auto-translate button) — cross-referenced against the live `translations` table before and after, zero missing at completion. `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. Full suite **166/166** still passing (translation wrapping is a display-layer change, doesn't touch server logic or test assertions).

---

## 64. Critical Fix: Super-Admin Company Selection Was Never Actually Honored — `/api/state` Returned Every Tenant's Data Unfiltered (Aug 2026)

Triggered by a live user report: newly-created invoices for "CNC Woodcraft & Design" weren't appearing anywhere — Sales Invoices, Dashboard, nowhere — despite being correctly saved (verified directly against Postgres: the rows existed, correctly scoped, correctly timestamped). Root-caused to a genuine architecture bug, not a display glitch, with three independent, compounding causes. This is one of the most consequential fixes in this project's history — it affected every super-admin session's company-switching behavior across the entire portal, not a single page.

**Cause 1 — `/api/state` bypassed company scoping entirely for a super-admin.** Every business-data array (`invoices`, `customers`, `expenses`, `vouchers`, everything) used a `isSuper ? unfiltered : filter(companyId)` pattern — a super-admin got literally every tenant's data in one payload, unfiltered, and the client was left to filter it all itself. Fixed: every business array is now scoped by `companyId` unconditionally, super-admin or not. The one deliberate exception is the `companies` list itself, which stays unfiltered — a super-admin needs the full list to populate the company switcher they use to pick a company in the first place.

**Cause 2 — the company a super-admin selected was never actually persisted anywhere the server would honor.** `isAuthenticated`'s target-company resolution was: explicit per-request `?companyId=` param → **the user's own home company** — `req.session.companyId` (set correctly at login) was never read at all. Since only a couple of call sites out of ~15 ever bothered to repeat `?companyId=` on every request, nearly every request silently reverted to the caller's own home company, discarding whatever they'd actually selected. Fixed: resolution order is now explicit param → `req.session.companyId` (the persisted selection) → home company, and a new `POST /api/switch-company` endpoint (super-admin only) persists the selection into the session when the UI's company switcher changes. A real subtlety surfaced here: on the `x-session-id` auth path (used by automated tests and any non-cookie API client — see CLAUDE.md), `req.session` is a *fresh, disconnected* session object express-session allocates per-request; writing to it and calling `.save()` silently writes to the wrong row. Fixed by resolving `req.activeSessionId` in `isAuthenticated` (the cookie's `req.sessionID`, or the raw `x-session-id` value when that path is in use) and having `/api/switch-company` write directly to that exact `user_sessions` row, correct for both auth paths.

**Cause 3 — a stale-closure bug in the frontend, found only after causes 1 and 2 were fixed and verified, and the UI *still* showed the wrong data.** The company switcher's `onChange` handler called `setDb(prev => ({...prev, selectedCompanyId: target}))` (schedules a React state update, doesn't apply until the next render) then immediately `await triggerDbRefresh()` — which read `db.selectedCompanyId` from its own closure, still holding the *previous* company, and passed it as an explicit `?companyId=` override on the refetch. That explicit param then took priority over the session (correctly updated by cause 2's fix moments earlier), silently reverting the just-applied switch. Fixed by removing the query param from `triggerDbRefresh` entirely — the session (updated by `/api/switch-company` before this call, or at login otherwise) is the reliable source of truth; the client closure never needs to repeat it.

**How this was actually found, not guessed**: initial hypotheses (stale client cache, wrong company selected in the UI) were each checked directly against real data and ruled out — a direct Postgres query confirmed the invoices existed and were correctly scoped before any code was touched, which is what turned this from "try refreshing" into a real investigation. Cause 1 and 2 were found by reading the actual `/api/state` handler and `isAuthenticated` line by line, not by assumption. Cause 3 was found only because a dedicated regression test (below) initially failed against a live server, was root-caused with temporary debug logging rather than dismissed as a fluke, and — critically — a first "it's fixed" report after causes 1+2 turned out to be wrong once actually re-tested against the real company, which is exactly why cause 3 got found instead of the investigation stopping one bug too early.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. New `tests/companyScoping.test.ts` (5 tests, real HTTP + real Postgres): a super-admin whose home company differs from the company they've switched to gets *that* company's data by default at login; `POST /api/switch-company` persists across a subsequent `/api/state` call with no query param; a non-super-admin is rejected (403); every business array is scoped, not just invoices, while `companies` correctly stays unfiltered; an explicit `?companyId=` still overrides the session when deliberately passed. All 5 initially failed against a stale/incompletely-restarted server process — re-verified clean against a freshly-confirmed server (process start time checked against file mtime) before treating it as passing. Full suite **171/171**. Independently re-confirmed against the real, named company in this bug report (not a synthetic fixture) via a throwaway super-admin test account: before switching, sees only its own home company's data; after switching to CNC Woodcraft & Design, correctly returns all 13 real invoices and all 3 real fiscal months, matching a direct Postgres count exactly.

**Also fixed while auditing this area**: an unrelated, pre-existing data-integrity finding surfaced during verification — CNC Woodcraft & Design has duplicate `invoiceNumber` values (`INV-1005`, `INV-1006`, and `INV-1007` each appear twice, on different dates). Not caused by this fix and not remediated here — flagged below as a separate, real gap.

---

## 65. Root Cause Fixed: Legacy Blob-Sync Endpoint Was Silently Rolling Back Document-Number Counters (Aug 2026)

Root-caused item 64's incidental finding — duplicate `invoiceNumber` rows (`INV-1005`/`1006`/`1007`, each appearing twice) for CNC Woodcraft & Design.

**The mechanism**: `POST /api/migrate` (`src/db/migrateData.ts`'s legacy blob-sync path, still used by seven frontend components — see the "`dbStore.ts` retirement" deferred item) always carries the caller's *entire* in-memory client state, including the `companies` array, and upserts it with a blind `onConflictDoUpdate({ set: record })` — a full overwrite of every included field, no merge. The `companies` record builder included `counters: c.counters` straight from the client. Actual document numbering, by contrast, is correctly generated through a *different*, row-locked path (`getAndIncrementCounter()` in `server/lib/businessLogic.ts`, `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`) — safe against concurrent requests by construction. But that safety only holds if nothing else can write `counters` out-of-band. Any browser tab left open across a save action (editing a customer, an inventory item, a POS setting — anything routed through the generic sync) would carry a `counters` snapshot from *before* other invoices were created elsewhere, and silently write it back over the current, correct, higher value. The next real invoice creation would then reissue an already-used number. This explains the observed timing exactly: the duplicate pairs were created hours to a day apart (not milliseconds), which had already ruled out a simple concurrent-request race condition — consistent with "a stale tab got saved later," not a locking failure.

This is the same bug class already documented (and partially fixed) in this exact file: a comment a few lines above the fix site records that a stale tab's blob-sync previously *resurrected a deleted company* twice via the same unconditional-overwrite mechanism, fixed at the time by filtering to `existingIds` only. `counters` was the same disease, unpatched.

**Fix**: dropped `counters` from the client-supplied `companies` upsert record entirely (`src/db/migrateData.ts`). Since Drizzle's `onConflictDoUpdate({ set: record })` only touches fields present in `record`, omitting the key means this sync path can no longer write to `counters` under any circumstance — it stays exclusively owned by `getAndIncrementCounter()`. Confirmed safe for the (documented, pre-existing) theoretical insert case too: `getAndIncrementCounter` already tolerates a `null` `counters` column, initializing `{ quotation: 1001, invoice: 1001, ... }` defaults on first use.

**Not fixed, deliberately**: the three existing duplicate `INV-100x` rows themselves. At least one pair includes an invoice already at ZATCA `REPORTED` status — its `invoiceNumber` is embedded in a signed, hash-chained XML document already submitted to ZATCA. Renumbering or deleting a reported document is a compliance action, not a data cleanup, and is out of scope for a silent fix; if this needs correcting it should be a deliberate, explicit decision, not a drive-by edit.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean. Confirmed by direct code reading that `counters` had exactly one write site in this file before the fix (the one removed) — no other path in `migrateData.ts` touches this column. New `tests/migrateCountersRegression.test.ts` (2 tests, real HTTP + Postgres): a stale client `counters` snapshot sent via `POST /api/migrate` after the real DB value has moved on is silently ignored (the DB value wins), while every other field in the same sync call (e.g. `name`) is still correctly updated — proving the exclusion is scoped to `counters` alone, not a blanket break of the sync endpoint. Full suite **173/173**.

---

## 66. `dbStore.ts` Dead-Code Retirement — the Dangerous Half Removed, Confirmed Nothing in the Portal Still Depends on It (Aug 2026)

User asked to "remove all debts," scoped down (via explicit direction call) to legacy-code-path retirement specifically, on the condition that nothing live in the app still depends on what gets removed. `src/dbStore.ts` was the target CLAUDE.md flags by name: "a full parallel reimplementation of invoice/quotation/expense/voucher business logic... synced back via a generic `POST /api/migrate` that overwrites rows from whatever the client currently holds in memory... more than one 'fix' has landed here while the equivalent real server route sat untouched" (the Cancel Invoice incident, item 35).

**Inventory before touching anything**: grepped every one of the 14 files importing `dbStore.ts` for every mutating/dangerous function name (`saveQuotation`, `updateQuotation`, `saveInvoice`, `convertQuotationToInvoice`, `markInvoicePaid`, `saveExpense`, `markExpensePaid`, `cancelExpense`, `saveInterBankTransfer`, `postRecurringExpense`, `settleAccrualExpense`, `saveInvestor`, `saveCapitalInvestment`, plus the client-side `getAndIncrementCounter` and `ensureSalesProductExists`) across `src/`, `tests/`, and `server/`, excluding `dbStore.ts`'s own definitions. Result: **zero real call sites for any of them.** Every reference outside the definition file was either a dead import (`QuotationModule.tsx`, `ExpenseModule.tsx`, `AdminSettings.tsx`, `PosModule.tsx`) or a comment documenting that a real server route "ports"/"matches" the old dbStore logic — i.e. the actual retirement (moving every real call site onto dedicated, transactional, row-locked REST routes) had already happened across earlier sessions/items (22, 40, 43, 58 among others); what was left in `dbStore.ts` was just the corpse of the old implementation, never deleted.

One specific finding worth flagging: the Deferred list's own "Recurring Expenses has no dedicated backend persistence endpoints" entry was **stale** — `RecurringExpenses.tsx` already calls real dedicated routes exclusively (`recurring-templates`, `recurring-postings`, `settle-accrual`, `accruals/:id`, all in `server/routes/transactions.ts`) and imports nothing dangerous from `dbStore.ts`. Corrected in the Deferred list below rather than left to mislead a future pass.

**What was removed**: `saveQuotation`, `updateQuotation`, `saveInvoice`, `convertQuotationToInvoice`, `markInvoicePaid`, `saveExpense`, `markExpensePaid`, `cancelExpense`, `saveInterBankTransfer`, `postRecurringExpense`, `settleAccrualExpense`, `saveInvestor`, `saveCapitalInvestment`, the client-side `getAndIncrementCounter`, `ensureSalesProductExists`, and `validateTransactionDate` (dbStore's own copy — distinct from and no longer risking drift from the real server-side one in `server/lib/businessLogic.ts`). `src/dbStore.ts`: **1796 → 923 lines.** Dead imports removed from `QuotationModule.tsx`, `ExpenseModule.tsx`, `AdminSettings.tsx`, `PosModule.tsx`.

**What was deliberately kept**: `openNewMonth`/`closeMonth` (`AdminSettings.tsx`) — still real call sites, but they only use `dbStore.ts` for local validation/derivation (checking recurring-expense preconditions, computing the P&L snapshot to attach) while the actual persistence already goes through the real `POST /api/transactions/months` route, not the generic blob-sync path (fixed in item 40). Retiring these too would mean duplicating their computation into the frontend directly or a new server preview endpoint — out of the scope the user asked for (removing an unused/risky *path*, not refactoring a working, already-safe one). Also kept: pure read/derive helpers with no mutation and no sync exposure (`calculateInvoiceTotals`, `generateBankLedger`, `getBankBalance`, `calculateMonthPnL`, `checkRecurringPreconditions`, `getOpenMonths`, `getActiveOpenMonth`, `getDefaultTaxSlabId`, `isDateInOpenMonth`), all confirmed still actively used for on-screen previews (e.g. a quotation's live total before the server round-trip). `saveDatabase`/`getDatabase` also kept — `saveDatabase` is a documented no-op (`localStorage`/cross-tab caching was already retired; "Only state in Postgres is kept"), `getDatabase()` just clones a hardcoded seed constant used solely as React's first-paint placeholder state before `/api/state` returns. Neither touches the network, so neither is "the path" the user was concerned about.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean. Full test suite **173/173** (no server-side code touched — `dbStore.ts` is never imported by `server/`, confirmed by grep; every `server/` reference to "dbStore" is a historical/documentation comment, not an import). `npx vite build` production client build succeeds clean. Live dev server: login page loads with zero console errors (full authenticated click-through of Quotations/Expenses/POS/Recurring blocked by the known recurring Browser-pane compositing issue, items 31/33 — fell back to code-level verification per that item's own documented guidance).

## 67. Inventory Nav "Bug" Was the Browser-Pane Compositing Issue, Not Code — Root-Caused and Documented

While verifying the Translations tab redesign (item 66's follow-up), clicking any sidebar nav item other than Dashboard appeared to do nothing — the header text updated but the page body stayed frozen. Traced to `App.tsx`'s page router (`<AnimatePresence mode="wait"><motion.div key={activeTab}>`): with `document.hidden === true` (confirmed directly), the browser throttles the `requestAnimationFrame` callbacks the exit transition depends on, so the incoming page never mounts. Not a permission, registry, or routing bug — `normalizePermissions()`, `canAny()`, and `isTabAllowed()` were all independently confirmed correct by direct code reading. Tried and confirmed **not** a workaround: overriding `document.hidden`/`visibilityState` via `Object.defineProperty` plus a synthetic `visibilitychange` event — the browser's actual compositor suspension persisted regardless. Full detail and the diagnostic tell (check `document.hidden` first) recorded in the "Browser-pane visibility/compositing issue" Deferred entry below (items 31, 33, 67) so a future session recognizes the symptom immediately.

---

## 68. Inventory & Procurement Module — Nav Bug Fixed, DSD/PR-Policy Enforcement Wired Up, and Three Previously-Unbuilt Sub-Modules Shipped (Aug 2026)

Started from a single bug report — "Inventory nav links aren't rendering for super-admin" — and grew into a full audit-and-build pass across the entire Inventory & Procurement module, following an explicit "act as an ERP/Supply Chain SME, follow Tier-1 ERP standards" instruction. Every piece below was scoped via an explicit direction call before being built, not assumed.

**Root bug — nav gate with no admin bypass.** `App.tsx`'s "Inventory Management" nav section (PR/PO/GRN/Stock) was still wrapped in `activeCompanySetup?.isInventoryModuleEnabled ? [...] : []` — a leftover company-level feature flag from before the permission-delegation model existed. Unlike every permission check in `normalizePermissions()`, this flag had no admin bypass, so a company whose flag defaulted to `false` hid the entire section from a super-admin too, even though the underlying permissions (`inventory.access`/`.pr`/`.po`/`.grn`/`.stock`) were already correctly delegable and the backend routes were already real and permission-gated. **Fixed**: removed the gate entirely — visibility is now purely permission-driven, same as every other module. Also removed the now-pointless company-settings toggle UI/badges in `AdminSettings.tsx` and the `handleToggleCompanyInventory` handler.

**DSD / PR-optionality — settings that existed but did nothing.** `companies.inventorySettings.{isDsdAllowed, prOptionality}` were stored and editable (nested under the same stale gate above) but never read by any route — a company could toggle "Allow DSD" off and GRN creation would ignore it completely; `prOptionality: MANDATORY` had no effect on PO creation. **Fixed**: the GRN route now rejects a DSD receipt when `isDsdAllowed` is false; the PO route rejects a PO with no `requisitionId` when `prOptionality` is `MANDATORY`, and — new, not previously checked in *any* mode — validates that a referenced requisition actually exists, belongs to the company, and is `Approved` before letting a PO reference it. The settings UI moved out from under the removed toggle so it's reachable in Company Setup unconditionally.

**PR: edit + withdraw.** A submitter could create a PR and get it approved/rejected, but never fix a typo or pull back a request before it was actioned. Added `PUT /purchase-requisitions/:id` (items/notes, Pending only) and `PATCH .../:id/withdraw` (Pending → new `Cancelled` status), both gated on `inventory.pr` — the submitter's own authority, deliberately not `inventory.approve`. Reused the create form as an edit form (`editingPrId` state), matching this codebase's established edit-reuses-create-form pattern.

**GRN: correction/reversal.** The highest-risk gap of the three flagged in the original audit — a wrong-quantity or wrong-batch receipt already moved real stock and could flip a PO to Received/Partially Received, with zero way to undo it. Added `POST /goods-receipt-notes/:id/reverse`: reverts the stock movement (clamped at 0), recomputes the linked PO's fulfillment status from the remaining non-reversed GRNs (a `goods_receipt_notes.is_reversed` column, filtered into that computation everywhere it's done), and marks the GRN reversed. Deliberately does **not** unwind the product's average cost (see below) — forward-only by design, same reasoning applied consistently across every reversal/cancellation built in this pass.

**Average cost / average sale price — new, on `productsServices`.** User's own follow-up ask, to support on-hand stock valuation. `averageCost`/`totalQuantityPurchased` fold in on every GRN receipt (PO-linked or DSD) as a true quantity-weighted moving average — not a display artifact, an actual running total maintained transactionally with row locks. `averageSalePrice`/`totalQuantitySold` do the same on the sales side, but required first closing a real architecture gap: invoice/quotation line items have always been free-text (`description` only, no `productId`), so there was nothing to fold sale prices against. Fixed by adding a nullable `productId` to `invoiceItems`/`quotationItems`, populated automatically wherever `ItemCatalogSearch` (the existing product-picker component already used by Quotation/Invoice/Expense forms) fires `onSelectItem` — a free-typed line simply never gets a `productId` and never contributes to the average, exactly as intended. POS already had real `productId` on every cart line; it just wasn't being forwarded into the invoice-item payload — one-line fix. Wired into three creation paths: direct invoice creation, quotation→invoice conversion, and POS (via the same route). Credit/Debit Notes carry `productId` through for display but deliberately don't fold into the average, same forward-only philosophy as GRN reversal.

**Three previously-unbuilt sub-modules, shipped with full CRUD-shaped permissions** (`purchaseBills`/`purchaseReturns`/`stockTakes`, each with real `create`/`read`/`update`/`delete` leaves — unlike the older flat `inventory.pr`/`.po`/`.grn` leaves, per explicit instruction to follow the standard shape for everything newly built):
- **Purchase Bills** — the Tier-1 "3-way match" control (PO → GRN → Bill): a bill references one or more already-received, not-yet-billed GRNs (`goods_receipt_notes.is_billed`), and its `subTotal`/`taxTotal`/`grandTotal` are computed server-side directly from those GRNs' own item rows — a client-submitted total is silently ignored, confirmed by test. All referenced GRNs must share one vendor. Full/partial payment (mirrors the expense/invoice payment shape) posts a real Payment voucher; cancel (Unpaid only) releases the GRNs' billed flag so they can be re-billed.
- **Purchase Returns (Debit Notes)** — returns against a specific GRN, capped at what that GRN actually received minus what's already been returned against it (tracked per product+batch). Decrements stock (clamped at 0); cancel restores it.
- **Physical Stock Takes** — Draft → Completed lifecycle. Counting alone never touches stock; `systemQuantity` is snapshotted server-side at count time (never trusted from the client), and finalizing recomputes each line's variance against the *current* quantity at finalize time (not the stale count-time snapshot) before posting one adjustment per line through the exact same clamped stock-mutation path `stock-adjustments` already uses.

**Deliberately not built, flagged rather than silently added**: inter-warehouse stock transfers (a standard Tier-1 capability, genuinely absent, but outside the scope actually asked for) and `stockLedgerTransactions` (a schema-only audit-trail table, confirmed still unused by every stock-mutating route including the new ones — wiring it in would touch GRN/reversal/adjustment/return/stock-take simultaneously, a cross-cutting change bigger than this pass's scope).

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout (checked after every logical chunk, not just at the end). `npm run db:push` applied cleanly (10 new/changed columns across `goods_receipt_notes`, `products_services`, `purchase_bills`, `purchase_returns`, `quotation_items`, `invoice_items`; also reconciled pre-existing, unrelated constraint-naming drift from Postgres identifier truncation — no-op-equivalent, confirmed before accepting). Full suite grew from 173 → **214** across 4 new/extended test files, all real HTTP + Postgres integration tests covering every enforcement rule described above (3-way match, DSD/PR-optionality gating, withdrawal/reversal/cancellation state machines, weighted-average arithmetic verified with exact expected values, not just "it changed"). `npx vite build` production build succeeds clean. Full `ux-translation` audit run against every changed file: 77 new/filled keys (76 literal + 1 dynamic status key the literal-grep audit couldn't see), written as real formal-register Arabic/Urdu, re-verified at zero missing/blank afterward.

---

## 69. `BR-KSA-F-08` Root-Caused — ZATCA's Own Sandbox Test Certificate Carries a 9-Digit CR Number

Open thread since item 41 ("New gap surfaced, not fixed"): every real sandbox submission since then has carried a `BR-KSA-F-08` warning ("Other Seller ID (BT-29) or Other Buyer ID (BT-46) for the scheme ID 'CRN' appears to be incorrect"), reproduced again on two fresh live submissions this session (a Credit Note dual-gate check, both real API + SDK `-validate`, same warning both times).

**Root cause, confirmed by decoding the actual certificate** (`crypto.X509Certificate`, not by reading code): ZATCA's shared sandbox test certificate's Subject CN is literally `CN=TST-886431145-399999999900003` — the embedded CR number, `886431145`, is genuinely 9 digits. Cross-checked against ZATCA's own bundled SDK sample invoices (`tools/zatca-sdk/Data/Samples/Simplified/{Invoice,Credit}/*.xml`), which use `<cbc:ID schemeID="CRN">1010010000</cbc:ID>` — the standard 10-digit Saudi CR format. `extractZatcaCertificateTaxpayerIdentity()`'s regex (`x509.ts`) is not truncating anything; it correctly extracts the full 9-digit value the certificate actually contains.

**Not fixed, and shouldn't be**: this is an inconsistency in ZATCA's own synthetic sandbox identity, not a defect in this codebase. The CR number used in every submission is required to match what the signing certificate is actually bound to (see item 41's `certificate-permissions` incident) — padding or altering it to fit the 10-digit pattern would falsify data against the real certificate and risk reproducing that exact class of rejection. Correctly triaged the same way `BR-KSA-98` already is: a real warning, sourced from ZATCA's own test data, not actionable from this side.

---

## 70. ZATCA Private Keys Now Encrypted at Rest (AES-256-GCM)

Every ZATCA signing key — Sandbox, Simulation, and Production alike — was stored in `zatcaEnvironmentConfigs.ecdsaPrivateKey` as a plain PEM string, flagged as a known gap directly in the onboarding wizard's own Step 2 copy ("not yet encrypted at rest"). Confirmed via direct query that Simulation/Production have never actually been onboarded by any company yet (0 rows), so this was a live exposure for Sandbox test keys only today — but the same code path stores a real, legally-binding key identically the moment Simulation/Production onboarding happens for real, so this needed fixing before that, not after.

**Shipped**: `server/lib/zatca/keyEncryption.ts` — AES-256-GCM, random IV per encryption, auth tag stored alongside the ciphertext (`zenc:v1:<iv>:<authTag>:<ciphertext>`, all base64), keyed off a new required `ZATCA_KEY_ENCRYPTION_SECRET` env var (fails loudly at startup if unset, same pattern as the existing `SESSION_SECRET` check in `server.ts`). Wired into all 8 real call sites across `server/routes/zatca.ts` and `server/lib/zatca/processInvoice.ts` — every write encrypts, every read that actually uses the key for a crypto operation decrypts first; pure existence checks (`!config?.ecdsaPrivateKey`) were left untouched since an encrypted string is still truthy. The trickiest site was `request-production-csid`: `signingKeyToStore` is both compared against a returned certificate *and* can be swapped to a different plaintext key (ZATCA's own sandbox sample key, per item 41's fix) before being persisted — restructured so it stays plaintext throughout the function and gets encrypted exactly once, at the final write, rather than accidentally comparing a certificate against a still-encrypted string (which would have made every cert-match check fail).

The 3 existing plaintext rows (all Sandbox, confirmed via direct query) were migrated in place with a round-trip check before each write (encrypt then immediately decrypt, refuse to persist on any mismatch) — not just assumed correct. `decryptPrivateKey` also transparently passes through a value that doesn't carry the `zenc:v1:` prefix (a real PEM always starts with `-----BEGIN`) as defense-in-depth rather than throwing, in case any row was ever missed by the migration; every write path naturally re-encrypts on its own next regeneration regardless.

**Verified, both gates, after the full change**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 223/223 (215 prior + 8 new `tests/zatcaKeyEncryption.test.ts` cases — round-trip, different-IV-per-call, tamper detection via the GCM auth tag, malformed-value rejection, missing/malformed-secret error messages). Then, the real proof: ran the *entire* onboarding wizard end-to-end on a fresh throwaway company with encryption active — identity → CSR generation (exercises `encryptPrivateKey` on write) → OTP/Compliance CSID (exercises `decryptPrivateKey` for the cert-match check) → compliance test suite (decrypt for real test-invoice signing, 2/2 scenarios `CLEARED`) → Production CSID (decrypt existing key, hit the sandbox-sample-key-swap branch, re-encrypt on write) → a real invoice (decrypt for actual signing) — real ZATCA sandbox API returned `REPORTED` with zero errors, then `fatoora -validate` against that exact XML: `[XSD]`/`[EN]`/`[KSA]`/`[QR]`/`[SIGNATURE]` all PASSED (`[PIH]` FAILED is the same already-documented, expected single-file-isolation limitation for any non-genesis invoice, `icv=2` here). Both gates agree; encryption doesn't affect signing correctness anywhere in the flow. Wizard Step 2 copy updated to reflect the fix; throwaway test company fully cleaned up afterward.

---

## 71. Real User/Permission Bugs Found Live — Wrong-Company User Creation, Silent Cross-Company Role Drop, Company Profile Never Actually Delegable

The project owner registered a new company (Shahdeen Undulus), created a Role (`ERP_ADMIN`), and assigned it to a new user (`usman`) — who then saw no nav items at all, and separately got `403 Forbidden` saving Company Profile despite the Role appearing to grant it. Three real, distinct bugs, not one:

**1. Wrong-company user creation.** `AdminSettings.tsx`'s `userForm.companyId` was a one-time `useState` initializer (`companyId: db.selectedCompanyId`) that never resynced when the active-company selector changed after the component mounted. Opening the Users tab on one company, then switching companies via the top-nav selector without a remount, left the "Assigned Company" dropdown silently showing the stale company — the submit payload prefers this stale `userForm.companyId` over the live `db.selectedCompanyId`. Usman was created under `Naqsh Co- CNC` even though `Shahdeen Undulus` was genuinely active at save time. **Fixed**: a `useEffect` resyncing `userForm.companyId` on every `db.selectedCompanyId` change, matching the pattern the adjacent `roles` state already used correctly.

**2. Silent cross-company role drop.** `POST /api/users` correctly refuses to link a Role to a user in a different company (a real security boundary — `userRoles` sync in `server/routes/users.ts`), but did so by silently filtering the roleId out and still returning `{success:true}` — no error, no warning. Usman ended up with zero roles and zero permissions with nothing anywhere explaining why. **Fixed**: the response now includes `droppedRoles: [{roleId, reason}]` when this happens, and `AdminSettings.tsx` surfaces it as an explicit warning instead of a false success toast.

**3. Company Profile was never actually delegable, despite the Roles editor implying otherwise.** `permissionSchema.ts` had no permission leaf for it at all — `PATCH /companies/:id/settings` (Company Profile save) was `isAdminUser()`-only, no delegation path existed. Confirmed by reproducing the exact request against a real account: `403` every time, regardless of what the Role granted, because nothing *could* grant it. Per explicit product direction to make this delegable (previously listed as deliberately admin-tier-only in item 61 and this file's own `CATEGORY_GROUPS` comment — both now updated), **fixed properly**: added a new `companyProfile: { read, update }` leaf (`groupId: 'financial_config'`, same bucket as Banks/Tax Slabs). The `PATCH /companies/:id/settings` route now accepts `isAdminUser()` OR `companyProfile.update`. **Deliberately excluded from this delegation**: `zatcaEnabled`, saved through the same endpoint — it keeps a hard admin-tier-only guardrail (mirrors the `users.create` role-forcing guardrail) since ZATCA has real legal/compliance exposure regardless of who's asking, per `CLAUDE.md`'s ZATCA section and the permission-crud-model skill's explicit "keep admin-tier-only" list. Verified live: granting `companyProfile.update` to a real non-admin-tier account lets it save profile fields (`200`) but still hard-blocks `zatcaEnabled` (`403`, explicit message) in the same request cycle.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean. Full suite grew 223 → **229** (2 new tests in `tests/usersAdminActions.test.ts` covering the cross-company role drop/report, 4 new tests in `tests/permissionCrudModel.test.ts` covering `companyProfile` delegation and the `zatcaEnabled` guardrail specifically). Two new permission-leaf labels (`View Company Profile`, `Edit Company Profile`) written with real Arabic/Urdu — `Company Profile` itself already had a translation row from the pre-existing Settings-page tab label. Scope deliberately held to Company Profile only, per explicit instruction — no other admin-only tab (ZATCA config, Companies Directory, Role definition, Translations, Database Backup) was touched.

## 72. Credit Notes Were Treated as Positive Revenue Everywhere, and Reversed a Real Bank Receipt Into Thin Air

Credit Notes correctly carry positive amounts on the wire (ZATCA/UBL requires this — intent is carried by the type code, not the sign), but that positive-amount convention had leaked into every internal report and screen that reads the `invoices` table directly, none of which distinguished a Credit Note from a real sale. Found live: after issuing a Credit Note against a paid invoice, revenue/receivables were inflated, the invoice list still showed the reversed invoice as "Active"/"Unpaid" and eligible for a second Credit Note, the printed Credit Note showed a Paid/Unpaid status line that means nothing for a reversal document, the Bank Statement Ledger went completely empty despite a real receipt and its reversal both having happened, and the Dashboard's "Pending Collection" KPI went negative.

**1. Sign convention leaking into reports/dashboard.** Added a shared `getInvoiceSign(inv)` helper (`src/dbStore.ts`) returning `-1` for `documentType === 'CreditNote'`, `+1` otherwise. Applied with differentiated treatment per report shape, not uniformly: aggregate sums (Dashboard totals, Trial Balance/P&L/Balance Sheet, Sales VAT Register, Sales Register, Item-wise Sales, Sales by Staff) multiply by the sign so a Credit Note nets out; the Customer Statement records a Credit Note as a `credit` ledger entry rather than a signed `debit`; AR Aging (`getOutstandingData`) excludes Credit Note rows entirely, since a negative "days overdue" line doesn't read naturally in an aging report.

**2. Second Credit Note against the same invoice was silently allowed.** `POST /invoices/:id/note` now checks for an existing `CreditNote` row referencing the same `originalInvoiceId` and rejects with `400` if found (Debit Notes deliberately excluded from this guard — they represent new charges, not a reversal, so more than one is legitimate). `InvoiceModule.tsx` mirrors this client-side: the Credit/Debit button is replaced with a disabled "Credited" indicator once a Credit Note exists.

**3. A credited invoice still displayed as "Active"/"Unpaid" everywhere, including its own print-out.** Per the permission-crud-model skill's "derived flag over overloaded status" principle, the original invoice's stored `status` is deliberately *not* mutated when credited (it really was Active and really was paid). Instead, `existingCreditNote` is computed client-side wherever the invoice is displayed and used to render a derived "Credited" label: the invoice-list Status pill (`InvoiceModule.tsx`), and the printed document's Status line (`DocumentRenderer.tsx`, keyed off `db.invoices` since the print renderer has the same full state available). A Credit Note's own `paymentStatus` (always `'Unpaid'` — schema default, never meaningful for a reversal document) was relabeled "Not Applicable" everywhere it displayed as a real status (invoice list, Sales Register, and the print-out's Payment Status/Payment Date lines, which are now hidden entirely for `documentType === 'CreditNote'`).

**4. The real bug: issuing a Credit Note against a paid invoice silently deleted the original Receipt voucher, with no replacement.** The Credit Note route reused `syncVoucherForInvoice`'s Cancel-branch logic (passing a synthetic `status: 'Cancelled'` override) to "reverse" the original payment. That branch's actual behavior, written for genuine invoice cancellation: if the Receipt voucher's fiscal month is still open, **delete it outright**; only post a dated Reversal voucher if the month is closed. That's correct for Cancel, where the invoice's own `status` really does flip to `'Cancelled'`. It's wrong for a Credit Note, which deliberately leaves the original invoice's `status`/`paymentStatus`/`amountPaid` untouched — so the invoice kept displaying "Paid $X" everywhere while its Receipt voucher silently vanished from the `vouchers` table, with no Reversal posted in an open month. Net effect, reported live: the Bank Statement Ledger showed nothing at all for a company with a real invoice payment and its credit-note reversal, and the Dashboard's Pending Collection KPI went negative (a Credit Note's `grandTotal - amountPaid(=0)` is its full positive amount, multiplied by `getInvoiceSign`'s `-1`, with nothing to offset it once the real Receipt was gone).

**Fixed**: added `postCreditNoteReversalVoucher()` (`server/lib/businessLogic.ts`) — always posts a genuine Reversal voucher (dated today, clamped into the open fiscal month the same way the existing Cancel-branch reversal logic already does) and never touches the original Receipt, regardless of whether the original's own fiscal month is open or closed. The Credit Note route (`server/routes/transactions.ts`) now calls this instead of `syncVoucherForInvoice`. Also fixed `Dashboard.tsx`'s `pendingCollection` KPI, which had its own separate gap: it filtered by `status === 'Active'` only, missing the `documentType !== 'CreditNote'` exclusion the adjacent `pendingInvoicesBase` already had.

**Data repair**: the 5 pre-existing Credit Notes in the dev database (issued before this fix) had already had their original invoice's Receipt voucher deleted with no Reversal posted. Backfilled directly: recreated each missing Receipt (dated at the original's stored `paymentDate`) then posted its Reversal via the new function, for every credited invoice that had no Receipt or Reversal voucher on file. Verified against real data (Shaheen Al-Andalus company, INV-1001/CN-1001): confirmed invoice's true VAT-inclusive total (310.50) matches `amountPaid` exactly, `pendingCollection` now nets to 0 instead of -310.50, and both a Receipt and a Reversal voucher (310.50 each) now exist for the Bank Statement Ledger to display.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean. Full suite grew 223 → **230** — one pre-existing test in `tests/zatcaWorkflow.test.ts` had encoded the old (buggy) delete-on-open-month behavior as expected and was updated to assert the Receipt survives and a Reversal is posted instead; one new test added for the duplicate-Credit-Note-rejection guard (item 3 above), which previously had no coverage. Dev server restarted before the final test run per this project's standard process.

---

## 73. ZATCA Resubmission Reused a Stale/Superseded Chain Identity — No ICV/UUID Reuse Guard, No Per-Environment Isolation, No Locked Issue Time

Extensive review this session against ZATCA's three official documents (`ZATCA Help/`: XML Implementation Standard, Fatoora Portal Manual, E-invoicing Detailed Technical Guidelines) and the SDK's own sample XMLs, comparing them line-by-line against `processInvoice.ts`/`hashChain.ts`/`xmlBuilder.ts`, surfaced six real defects in how this app handled anything other than a first-attempt-succeeds invoice submission — none of them previously covered by the existing dual-gate verification, which had only ever exercised the happy path.

**1. Resubmission always reused the original `icv`/`uuid`/`previousInvoiceHash`**, directly contradicting the Detailed Technical Guidelines FAQ (p.76, verbatim): *"After an invoice gets rejected UUID and ICV should not be re-used. System should assign a new ICV."* Worse: if another invoice was generated and successfully cleared during the gap (a realistic scenario — a network outage, then a second invoice created before the first is retried), that later invoice had already chained off the failed one's *original* hash. A late resubmit reusing the original identity would try to insert behind an already-accepted chain tip.

**2. `IssueTime` was recomputed fresh (`new Date()`) on every processing attempt**, including resubmits, even though `IssueDate` was already correctly frozen from `invoice.date`. A resubmitted invoice's customer-facing timestamp could drift from what ZATCA ultimately cleared.

**3. No isolation between Sandbox/Simulation/Production.** `invoices` had no environment column; the chain-tip lookup read `MAX(icv)` across a company's entire history regardless of environment. The first invoice submitted after a company goes live on Production would have incorrectly continued Sandbox's ICV sequence and referenced a hash Production's backend — confirmed via the Fatoora manual's distinct `/simulation/` vs `/core/` API hosts to be a wholly separate system — has never seen.

**4. `AccountingCustomerParty` was sometimes omitted entirely** for a buyer-less invoice, violating the XSD's `minOccurs="1"` on that element — confirmed directly against the SDK's own XSD and its B2C sample, which shows the element present-but-empty, never absent.

**5. No guard against resubmitting an already-`CLEARED`/`REPORTED` invoice**, and no guard against two concurrent processing attempts for the same invoice both proceeding past the `SUBMITTING` transition.

**6. A cancelled invoice that had already reserved a chain position but never reached ZATCA permanently wasted that ICV** — cancelling before ZATCA ever saw it should give the position back, not burn it forever.

**Fixed, all six together** (`server/lib/zatca/hashChain.ts`, `processInvoice.ts`, `server/routes/zatca.ts`, `server/routes/transactions.ts`, `server/lib/zatca/xmlBuilder.ts`, `src/db/schema.ts`):

- New `zatcaChainState` table, one row per `(companyId, environment)`, replacing the old "scan `invoices` for `MAX(icv)`" approach — a point lookup that stays O(1) regardless of tenant history size (this app's stated SaaS-scale priority), and gives each environment a genuinely independent genesis without needing an `environment` column (and a backfill guess) on every historical invoice row. No `documentType` dimension — Invoice/CreditNote/DebitNote for one company still share a single continuous sequence, exactly as ZATCA's chain-integrity model requires and as the existing code already correctly did.
- Resubmission identity is now a real decision, not an unconditional reuse: an invoice's original `icv`/`uuid`/`previousInvoiceHash` are only reused when **both** (a) the original attempt definitively never reached ZATCA's network (the only status/code combination that guarantees this: `NOT_SUBMITTED` + `ONBOARDING_INCOMPLETE`, set before any fetch call — an actual rejection or an ambiguous network exception are both treated conservatively as "might have reached ZATCA," never assumed safe) and (b) nothing else has since been generated at a later chain position (`isStillChainTip`). Failing either, the invoice mints a fresh `icv`/`previousInvoiceHash` (chaining off the real current tip) and a fresh `uuid`, and the superseded identity is captured to `auditLogs` (`zatca_chain_identity_superseded`) before being overwritten — mirroring how ZATCA's own platform permanently records a rejected document's hash even though it was never accepted.
- `IssueTime` now derives from `invoice.createdAt` (set once at row insert, never touched again anywhere in the codebase — confirmed via a repo-wide check) instead of `new Date()`, exactly mirroring how `IssueDate` already derived from `invoice.date`. Per the Detailed Technical Guidelines FAQ this is a deliberate choice, not a compliance requirement (ZATCA permits updating the time on resubmission but doesn't mandate it) — made for document-integrity reasons: what ZATCA eventually clears must match whatever the customer was already shown at creation time.
- `POST /invoices/:id/cancel` now rolls back `zatcaChainState` to `(icv-1, previousInvoiceHash)` when the cancelled invoice never reached ZATCA and is still the chain tip — reusing the exact same two checks as the resubmission logic, under the same company-row lock. If either check fails (might have reached ZATCA, or something else already chained off it), no rollback — the position stays a permanent gap, same conservative posture as everywhere else in this fix.
- `POST /zatca/submit-invoice/:id` now rejects (400) resubmission of an already-`CLEARED`/`REPORTED` invoice.
- `processInvoiceZatca`'s `SUBMITTING` claim guard now also refuses if a submission is already in flight for that invoice.
- `xmlBuilder.ts` now always emits the `AccountingCustomerParty` element (empty when no buyer, matching the SDK's own B2C sample byte-for-byte), never omits it.

**A real bug caught during implementation, not just design**: the first draft called `recordAuditLog` (which uses the plain, untransacted `db` connection) from *inside* the locked transaction that reserves a fresh chain position — since `auditLogs.companyId` is a foreign key into `companies`, and that transaction already holds `FOR UPDATE` on the exact same `companies` row, the separate connection's insert blocked waiting for a lock the still-open outer transaction held, until Postgres's `statement_timeout` cancelled it. Caught by the new tests timing out, not by inspection. Fixed by deferring the audit-log call to fire after the transaction commits (same pattern already used for `icv`/`pih`/`zatcaDoc`).

**Verified — automated**: `tsc --noEmit` clean. Full suite grew 230 → **238**: 15 new tests in `tests/zatcaWorkflow.test.ts` covering every scenario above precisely (using this test company's own permanent lack of a Production CSID as a deterministic, network-mock-free way to reproduce "never reached ZATCA"), plus one existing test's exact error-message assertion updated after the `SUBMITTING` guard's message was merged with the cancellation one.

**Verified — live, real ZATCA sandbox + SDK, against CNC Woodcraft & Design** (the seeded demo company, not a throwaway fixture): a real Standard B2B invoice cleared cleanly (zero errors, only the pre-existing tolerated `BR-KSA-F-08` warning), `IssueTime` in the actually-submitted XML matched `createdAt` to the second, a Credit Note against it also cleared with `icv` correctly sequential and `previousInvoiceHash` correctly chained to the invoice's own hash, and resubmitting the already-`CLEARED` invoice was correctly rejected with the new 400 guard, fields unchanged. `fatoora -validate` against both final XMLs: XSD/EN/KSA all `PASSED` for both; `[PIH] FAILED` on both is the same already-documented, expected single-file-isolation limitation for any non-genesis invoice (see item 70's writeup), not a defect. One real mid-verification mistake, corrected in place: a first attempt to simulate "never reached ZATCA" by temporarily clearing the company's `productionCsidCert` failed to restore correctly, discovered immediately via a direct DB check rather than assumed fixed — recovered by re-running the real `request-production-csid` onboarding step against ZATCA's actual sandbox API (itself additional live proof that path still works), not by guessing the original value back in.

**Explicit limitation, not glossed over**: true end-to-end confirmation of Production's first-invoice-starts-at-genesis behavior cannot be verified live — no company in this system has ever completed Simulation or Production onboarding (0 rows, confirmed). The environment-isolation logic is proven correct at the unit level (a new `(companyId, environment)` pair returns genesis, an existing one is unaffected) but not against a real, previously-untouched Production backend — that can only be confirmed the first time a real company actually goes live there.

## 74. Invoice View Screen — Dedicated Record Page Replacing the Auto-Open Print Modal (Aug 2026)

Previously, saving an invoice immediately auto-opened the shared `DocumentRenderer` print/preview modal, and every action (Print, Cancel, Credit Note) lived as a small icon crammed into an already-packed list-table row, with no dedicated space for ZATCA status or validation detail. Replaced with a dedicated, full-screen "View Invoice" record page, landed on automatically after creating an invoice, additive to the existing list (its row actions were later collapsed too — see below).

- New component `src/components/InvoiceViewScreen.tsx`: header (Print / Download PDF / Issue Credit Note), left panel (line items, `calculateInvoiceTotals`), right column (ZATCA status card with QR/hash-chain detail/manual resubmit, Payment card, Cancel).
- New tab `'invoices-view'` + `viewTarget` state in `App.tsx`, following the existing `editTarget` precedent — no new routing system introduced.
- `InvoiceModule.tsx`'s post-create flow now navigates to the View screen instead of auto-opening `DocumentRenderer`; the list's own invoice-number cell became a clickable link into the same screen.
- **Download PDF** — new capability, not previously possible: `src/pdfExport.ts` rasterizes the same `DocumentRenderer` content (`embedded` mode, off-screen) via `html2canvas` and wraps it into a real downloadable file via `jsPDF`, deliberately client-side to avoid a server-side Puppeteer footprint.
- **Follow-up simplification, same session**: after live use, the list's Actions column (ZATCA badge/Print/Receive-Pay/Cancel/Credit-Debit, five separate icons per row) was collapsed to a single "View" button — every one of those actions already exists inside the View screen, so the list stopped duplicating them. The View screen's ZATCA card was extended first to make sure nothing was lost in the collapse: QR code, UUID/ICV/hash-chain detail, validation diagnostics, a manual "Submit / Re-submit to ZATCA" button, and a "Download XML File" button were all ported in from the list's old ZATCA-detail modal, which was then deleted along with its now-dead state/handlers.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 238/238, and a real invoice created/viewed/PDF-downloaded/credit-noted live against the ZATCA sandbox (see item 75 for why the first live PDF attempts failed and how that was root-caused).

## 75. Download PDF Was Silently Failing — Tailwind v4's `oklch()` Colors, Not Network/CORS as First Suspected (Aug 2026)

Real-world use of item 74's Download PDF surfaced a generic "Failed to generate PDF" toast. First fix attempt targeted the obvious external dependency — the invoice's ZATCA QR code was being fetched from a third-party image API (`api.qrserver.com`) at render time, and `html2canvas`'s CORS-mode fetch of a cross-origin image is a well-known failure class. Made the QR fully local (the `qrcode` npm package, no network call) in both `DocumentRenderer.tsx` (the print/PDF path) and — found only after the user reported the fix hadn't worked — a *second*, separately-implemented QR `<img>` in `InvoiceViewScreen.tsx`'s own on-screen ZATCA card, which had been pointed at the same external API and missed in the first pass.

That fix was real (removes a genuine offline-reliability gap) but wasn't the actual cause. Added `console.error` logging around the swallowed catch block and reproduced live: `Attempting to parse an unsupported color function "oklch"`, thrown by `html2canvas` (v1.4.1, last released 2023) the moment it walked any element styled with a Tailwind v4 utility — Tailwind v4's default color palette is defined in `oklch()`, a color space that predates-html2canvas has no support for. This meant the *entire* off-screen invoice node was guaranteed to fail, every time, on the first colored element — a structural incompatibility, not an intermittent network issue.

**Fixed**: swapped `html2canvas` → `html2canvas-pro` (`src/pdfExport.ts`), an actively-maintained, API-compatible fork built specifically to parse `oklch`/`oklab`/`color-mix`. No other code changed.

**Also fixed while investigating, same root motivation (remove implicit internet dependencies from a core business function)**: `index.css`'s Google Fonts `@import` (Inter/Space Grotesk/JetBrains Mono/Cairo/Lora) replaced with `@fontsource/*` packages, bundled locally by Vite — confirmed in a real `vite build` that every font file ships under `dist/assets/`, and every font request in a live check resolved to `localhost`, none to `fonts.googleapis.com`. This incidentally also removes a second, independent `html2canvas` failure mode: a same-origin stylesheet's cross-origin `@import` is opaque to script (a browser security rule regardless of the target's own CORS headers), and `html2canvas` throws a `SecurityError` the moment it tries to read such a stylesheet's `cssRules` while cloning styles.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 238/238, a real production `vite build` (confirms font/QR/html2canvas-pro bundling), and live: Download PDF completes with the console log reaching `"Finished rendering"` (previously aborted immediately on the oklch error), the on-screen ZATCA QR renders as a local `data:` URI, and every font request resolves same-origin.

## 76. Permission Audit Across Quotation/Expense/Products/Units/Customers/Vendors — One Real Upsert-Gating Bug Found (Aug 2026)

Prompted by finding, while wiring the View screen's manual ZATCA resubmit button (item 74), that `POST /zatca/submit-invoice/:invoiceId` had no permission check at all beyond a blanket admin-tier gate — moved it above that gate and scoped it to `invoice.create || invoice.update` (mirroring the leaf the sibling `/paid` route already uses), so any role granted either permission can trigger it, not just admins.

That finding prompted a full audit of the same upsert-permission-branching pattern across every other form the user named: Quotations, Expenses, Products, Units, Customers, Vendors.

**One real bug, in Quotations**: `POST /quotations` (upserts both new and edited quotations) only ever checked `quotation.create`, never `quotation.update` — the exact anti-pattern the `permission-crud-model` skill exists to prevent, and which every sibling upsert route (`expenses`, `products`, `units-of-measure`, `customers`, `vendors` — several with an explicit code comment saying so) already avoided correctly. Practical effect: a role with only "Create Quotations" checked could silently edit *any* existing quotation; a role with only "Edit Quotations" checked couldn't use the edit form at all, since the client's own gate had the identical bug. The route also skipped the Converted/Cancelled record-state guards the sibling `PUT /quotations/:id` route already enforced. Fixed in `server/routes/transactions.ts` and `QuotationModule.tsx` (Save handler and the Edit button's visibility both now branch on edit-vs-create); no other module in the audit had this gap.

**Cleanup, same session, explicitly requested**: every other module's `isAdmin ||`/`!isAdmin &&` check sitting redundantly alongside an already-correct `userPermissions.module.leaf.enabled` check (`normalizePermissions` already resolves every leaf to `true` for admin/super-admin, so the extra check was noise, never protection) was removed — `QuotationModule.tsx`, `InvoiceModule.tsx`, `InvoiceViewScreen.tsx` (where `isAdmin` became fully dead code afterward and was deleted), `ExpenseModule.tsx`, `AdminSettings.tsx`. Three genuinely different `isAdmin` uses were identified and deliberately left alone: the Invoice/Expense forms' "bank selection is admin-only" business rule (no corresponding permission leaf — by design, not a redundancy); `AdminSettings.tsx`'s two truly-admin-tier-only tab branches; and `Dashboard.tsx`'s three filters, which check the *raw* `currentUser.permissions.invoice` object directly rather than going through `normalizePermissions` — removing `isAdmin` there would break the dashboard for any admin whose stored `permissions` JSON happens to be empty (normal for admins), so that one had to stay.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean, full suite 238/238, and live as an admin — quotation create succeeded, and a direct request against the newly-fixed update branch passed permission (no 403), confirming the cleanup didn't regress admin access anywhere it touched.

Third stale-Deferred-item finding in a row this pass (see Recurring Expenses and Canvas Designer confirms above) — a pattern worth a dedicated BACKLOG re-verification sweep rather than trusting this section's premises going forward.
- ~~**Canvas Designer confirm dialogs untranslated (item 63)**~~ — stale by the time item 78 went looking for it: all `window.confirm()` calls in `AdminSettings.tsx` (unsaved-layout-changes warning, reset-to-ZATCA-default, permanent-custom-block-removal, and the template-delete confirm) are already wrapped in `t()`, confirmed by direct code reading. Left uncorrected in BACKLOG for at least one full pass — another reminder (see the Recurring Expenses entry above) to re-verify a Deferred item's premise before treating it as still-open work.
- ~~**Settings panel is not permission-aware (item 61)**~~ — resolved in item 62.
- ~~**Duplicate `invoiceNumber` values for CNC Woodcraft & Design (found in item 64)**~~ — root cause found and fixed in item 65 (legacy blob-sync path was clobbering the row-locked counter). The three existing duplicate rows themselves are left as-is, deliberately — see item 65 for why (one pair includes a ZATCA-`REPORTED` document; renumbering it is a compliance decision, not a cleanup).
- **The item 64 stale-closure bug class isn't covered by this project's test suite at all**: every test in `tests/` is a server-side HTTP + Postgres integration test (per this project's established, deliberate no-mocks convention) — none of them execute React component code, so a bug living entirely in a `useState`/closure timing issue (like `triggerDbRefresh` reading `db.selectedCompanyId` from a stale closure) is structurally invisible to the current suite, however thorough. It was only found by direct code reading, not a failing test. Adding frontend/component-level test coverage would be a real, separate testing-infrastructure investment (a first for this codebase), not a quick addition.
- **Dedicated cancel-flag retrofit for invoices/expenses/purchase-orders**: item 60 gave quotations a clean dedicated `isCancelled` boolean, separate from their phase `status` field, and documented that as the going-forward convention (see the `permission-crud-model` skill). Invoices, expenses, and purchase orders still represent cancellation by overloading `status` with a `'Cancelled'` value instead — not retrofitted in that same pass because it's materially higher-risk (invoices' status is read throughout the ZATCA clearance/reporting pipeline specifically) and was an open question when the user granted autonomy to proceed on the rest. Revisit deliberately, not as a drive-by change, and re-run the dual-gate ZATCA verification protocol afterward if it's ever done.
- **Postgres Row-Level Security (RLS)**: Deferred from item 22's schema hardening. Would add a fail-closed backstop so a missed `WHERE company_id = ?` in application code returns an empty result instead of leaking data. Requires a bigger architecture change first — wiring a session variable (`SET LOCAL app.current_company_id`) into every request's DB connection/transaction, which doesn't fit the current shared-pool pattern without rework. **Given this project's SaaS-at-scale requirement (thousands of concurrent users, many tenants), this should be revisited as a near-term priority, not indefinitely deferred.**
- **Full paginated API + frontend list-UI rewrite**: Deferred from item 22 in favor of a backend-only safety cap (shipped). Real limit/offset pagination with a `{items, total}` response envelope would require updating `dbStore.ts` and ~10 list-rendering components to fetch pages and render page/load-more controls. Planned to land alongside the upcoming List/Add page-split feature, which will already be touching these same components — doing both together avoids reworking the same files twice.
- ~~**`dbStore.ts` retirement**~~ — the dangerous half (every mutating, state-duplicating business-logic function) removed in item 66; `src/dbStore.ts` is now 923 lines, down from 1796. What remains is exclusively safe: seed/fallback constants, pure local-computation helpers (`calculateInvoiceTotals`, `generateBankLedger`, `getBankBalance`, `calculateMonthPnL`, `checkRecurringPreconditions`) used for on-screen previews before a real server call, and `openNewMonth`/`closeMonth`, which persist via the real `/api/transactions/months` route already (see item 66's writeup for why these two were kept rather than also removed). No further retirement work is planned unless a new dangerous function gets added back to this file — if that happens, treat it as a regression, not business as usual.
- ~~**Real ZATCA cryptography**~~ — resolved in item 28. The CSR is now a real, ZATCA-accepted PKCS#10 structure (item 27), and full XAdES-BES signing is now implemented and verified live for both Standard and Simplified invoices (item 28).
- **`ProductService.type` classification cleanup**: Live data confirmed `item`/`service` and `Sales`/`Purchase` are both genuinely in use on the same field, representing two different, overlapping classification concepts that were never reconciled. The type was tightened to match reality (item 22) but the underlying data model ambiguity is unresolved — needs a product decision on what this field should actually mean before any further schema/logic change.
- **List/Add page-split feature — Phases V-VIII remaining**: Phases I-IV shipped in item 23 (Quotations/Invoices/Expenses/Customers/Vendors/Products/Categories/Units/Warehouses all now have independent List/Add pages). Still pending: Phase V (relocate Banks/Tax Slabs out of Settings into their own List/Add pages), Phase VI (POS/Inventory sub-forms UI split — cosmetic only, no backend routes exist yet), Phase VIII (final backlog writeup for the whole plan). ~~Phase VII (Credit Note)~~ — Credit Note **and** Debit Note both shipped and dual-gate verified in item 32, ahead of this phase's original schedule; full-document reversal only (partial/line-selectable credit notes still pending, see below).
- **Section 1h leftovers from item 21's review** (lower priority, not yet actioned): `pos.discount`/`pos.cancel` permissions have no backend route to enforce against yet (POS sale/discount endpoints don't exist server-side). ~~`inventory.pr`/`po`/`grn` permissions likewise have no backend CRUD routes to protect~~ — this claim was already stale by the time item 68 went looking for it; real, permission-gated routes for all three (plus `.approve`/`.stock`) have existed since before that pass, confirmed by direct code reading. ~~warehouse CRUD gated on `products.edit`~~ — resolved in item 23 (Categories/Units/Warehouses now have their own dedicated `.view`/`.edit` permission nodes, independent of Products).
- **`usr-test-proc`'s malformed `{"expenses": {...}}` permission entry (item 23)**: moot as of item 24 — `users.permissions` no longer exists as a column at all (superseded by role assignment), so this stale/typo'd data was dropped along with the column rather than needing a manual fix.
- **Per-role permission overrides**: explicitly deferred in item 24's design (project-owner decision) — a role-assigned user's access is exactly the union of their roles, no per-user exceptions on top. If a one-off need for "this specific user but not the rest of the role" ever comes up, the intended answer is a new (or cloned) role, not an override mechanism — revisit only if that stops being sufficient in practice.
- ~~**`BR-KSA-23` VAT exemption reason code (BT-121)**~~ — fixed in item 78. `taxSlabs` gained `exemptionReasonCode`/`exemptionReason` columns, threaded through `processInvoice.ts` into `xmlBuilder.ts`'s `TaxExemptionReasonCode`/`TaxExemptionReason` elements (correct UBL element order confirmed against the actual XSD), with an admin UI to set them per-slab — deliberately left unconfigured by default, since the specific VATEX-SA-xx code is a real legal classification that varies per company and can't be safely guessed. Live-verified against CNC Woodcraft & Design: with a correctly-categorized code set, both `BR-KSA-23` and a second warning it surfaced (`BR-KSA-CL-04`, invalid code for the category) disappear entirely, `fatoora -validate` shows `[XSD]`/`[EN]`/`[KSA]` all `PASSED`; with no code set, behavior is unchanged from before (regression-checked); a Credit Note against the configured invoice correctly inherits the fix. See item 78 for the full test matrix.
- ~~**`BR-KSA-EN16931-11`**: line-net-amount reconciliation warning observed on discount-heavy invoices.~~ — root-caused and fixed in item 78: `cac:Price/cbc:PriceAmount` stayed the raw undiscounted unit price while `cbc:LineExtensionAmount` was the fully-discounted subtotal, with no `cac:AllowanceCharge` declaring the gap. Fixed by emitting a line-level `AllowanceCharge` for the combined per-item + header-discount amount whenever non-zero. Dual-gate verified on a discount-heavy Invoice and Credit Note; zero-discount regression confirmed unaffected.
- ~~**`BR-KSA-F-08` (item 41)**: "Other Seller ID (BT-29) for scheme ID 'CRN' appears to be incorrect"~~ — root-caused and fixed in item 78: ZATCA expects the seller CRN as a 10-digit `schemeID="CRN"` value; several company records store it without leading zeros. Fixed by zero-padding to 10 digits at XML-build time. Dual-gate verified on a B2B Invoice and Credit Note.
- ~~**B2C buyer address "Conditional" nuance**~~ — condition identified and fixed in item 78: ZATCA's schematron (`BR-KSA-63`) only requires a buyer address when the invoice is Standard/B2B (`name="0100000"`); it never fires for Simplified/B2C. Fixed to omit `cac:PostalAddress` (and each sub-field individually) whenever the customer genuinely has no address on file, instead of the previous behavior of fabricating placeholder address values for any customer lacking one. Dual-gate verified on a real no-address B2C Invoice and Credit Note, plus independently confirmed by the user against ZATCA's own online validator.
- **Browser-pane visibility/compositing issue is recurring, not a one-off (items 31, 33, 67)**: `document.hidden` intermittently stays `true` across separate sessions (confirmed fine mid-way through item 32's session — real screenshots obtained then — but stuck again in item 33's session, including after a `requestAnimationFrame`→`setTimeout` monkey-patch attempt to force it). This blocks live click-through verification of any Framer Motion `AnimatePresence`-gated view whenever it happens, tied to whether the Browser pane panel is actually visible/focused on the user's end at that moment — not something fixable from code or from within the page. When it's blocking, code-level verification (tsc, direct bundle inspection, DB checks) is the fallback, but a manual click-through should still happen once the pane is genuinely visible.
  **Item 67 found a much more severe manifestation worth naming precisely**, so a future session doesn't mistake it for a real app bug and burn time chasing it: when `document.hidden === true`, `App.tsx`'s `<AnimatePresence mode="wait"><motion.div key={activeTab}>` (the top-level page router) never completes the outgoing page's exit transition — because `mode="wait"` holds the new page unmounted until the old one's exit animation finishes, and a hidden/backgrounded tab throttles the `requestAnimationFrame` callbacks that transition depends on. The practical symptom: clicking ANY sidebar nav item (not just one page) updates the header title/subtitle instantly (a plain synchronous ternary, unaffected) while the page body stays frozen on whatever was showing before, indefinitely — looking exactly like a permission check silently failing or a routing bug, for every destination, even though `activeTab` state itself is updating correctly and no runtime error occurs. Diagnostic tell: check `document.hidden` first, before suspecting `canAny()`/`normalizePermissions()`/the nav's `isTabAllowed()` gate, whenever "clicking a nav item does nothing" is reported inside this harness's Browser pane. Tried and confirmed **not** a workaround: overriding `document.hidden`/`visibilityState` to report `false`/`'visible'` via `Object.defineProperty` and dispatching a synthetic `visibilitychange` event — the stuck transition persisted anyway, meaning the real constraint is the browser's actual paint/compositor suspension for a backgrounded tab (which throttles `requestAnimationFrame` at the browser level), not just the JS-visible `document.hidden` flag reflecting it. Don't re-attempt this specific workaround in a future session.
- ~~**POS never reaches the real ZATCA pipeline (item 32)**~~ — fixed in item 43.
- **Credit/Debit Note — partial-line selection (item 32)**: the shipped feature is full-document reversal only (mirrors every original line verbatim); no way to credit only some lines/amount. ~~automatic refund/reversal voucher~~ shipped in item 35.
- ~~**Backend `dev` script has no watch/auto-restart (item 35)**~~ — fixed: `package.json`'s `dev` script changed from `tsx server.ts` to `tsx watch server.ts`. Verified live — editing `server.ts` now triggers an automatic clean restart (confirmed twice, server correctly back up on port 3000 each time) instead of silently serving stale code until a manual stop/start, which is exactly the class of incident that caused item 35's real bug and this session's own brief payment-receipt false alarm. `CLAUDE.md`'s "backend does not auto-restart" warning is now stale and should be removed/updated next time that file is touched.
- **Full app-wide `StatusPill`/`DataTable`/`Pagination` rollout (item 32)**: `StatusPill` shipped and is live on Invoice list, Quotation list, and POS Sales History only, per the project owner's explicit "Sales/Print first" scope for that pass. Every other module (Expenses, Recurring, Inventory, Master Entities, Admin Settings) still uses its own ad-hoc badge styling.
- **Master Entities search/pagination (flagged in the review behind item 32)**: none of the six directories (Customers, Vendors, Products, Categories, Units, Warehouses) have a search box or pagination beyond a static "Total: X records" badge — every fetched row renders unfiltered. A real, scale-relevant gap given this project's SaaS-at-scale priority; related to but more specific than the general paginated-API item above.
- ~~**Recurring Expenses has no dedicated backend persistence endpoints (item 32's Foundation fix)**~~ — this entry was stale by the time item 66 went looking for it: real dedicated REST routes already exist and are already the only path `RecurringExpenses.tsx` calls (`POST/PUT/DELETE /api/transactions/recurring-templates`, `PATCH .../toggle`, `POST /api/transactions/recurring-postings`, `POST /api/transactions/settle-accrual`, `PUT/DELETE /api/transactions/accruals/:id`) — confirmed live in `server/routes/transactions.ts` and by direct code reading of every call site in the component. Left uncorrected in BACKLOG for at least one full pass; a reminder to re-verify a Deferred item's premise before treating it as still-open work, not just re-cite it.
- **Full server-side financial-report aggregation**: reports/KPIs consuming the 500-row-capped list endpoints now show an honest disclosure banner (item 32) instead of silently implying completeness, but the real fix — computing VAT/trial-balance/P&L totals via server-side SQL aggregation instead of an in-memory capped list — is unbuilt.
- **A full security audit**: this session's security-focused work was narrowly scoped to the ZATCA OTP-burn guard and the Users-CRUD silent-failure fix (item 32); a broader audit (the other Critical/High findings' security implications, auth/session handling, input validation across every route) has not been done.
- ~~**UX Test company hasn't completed real ZATCA sandbox onboarding**~~ — this diagnosis was wrong; the real cause was found and fixed in item 41 (a genuine certificate-identity bug, not missing onboarding — UX Test was already fully onboarded).
- ~~**Canvas Designer: no keyboard-accessible block reordering (design review, item pre-40)**~~ — fixed in item 44 (↑/↓ buttons on each block, keyboard-operable).
- ~~**Canvas Designer: no row-width validation or true `custom_field` parity (design review, item pre-40)**~~ — fixed in item 44 (advisory row-composition summary, universal Padding control, `custom_field` bilingual toggle + border-style parity with `customer_info`).
- ~~**`POST /api/transactions/invoices/:id/paid` doesn't support partial payments (item 38's follow-up)**~~ — fixed in item 58. The route now accepts an optional `amount`, posts a standalone voucher per settlement instead of reusing `syncVoucherForInvoice`'s single-voucher model, and `InvoiceModule.handleConfirmPay` is migrated onto it.
- ~~**POS shift/held-invoice actions still full-blob-sync after real persistence**~~ — fixed in item 40, triggered by a real incident (see below).

The three POS-tab toggles (Auto-Print, Max Image Size, Max Image Dimensions) now call a dedicated `handlePosSettingsChange(companyId, posSettings)` instead of misusing `handleCompanySave`'s form-event signature; `posSettings` added to `PATCH /api/companies/:id/settings`'s field allowlist (`server/routes/masterEntities.ts`). `handleCompanySave` itself untouched, still used correctly by the real Company Setup form's `onSubmit`.
- ~~**`fetchMonths`, `handleOpenMonth`/`handleConfirmClose` (AdminSettings.tsx), and the redundant Tier-1 refetch-then-reupload sites**~~ — fixed in item 40.
