Of the two genuinely at-risk sites: `InvoiceModule.handleConfirmPay` is now migrated onto the real (newly partial-payment-capable) route; `AdminSettings.handleTruncateTransactions` turned out to be silently non-functional (the sync endpoint it used can only upsert, never delete — the button never actually deleted anything server-side) and was removed from the UI rather than given a real destructive-delete route. `handleForcePushToCloud` and the two Import Database handlers still push a full blob by design — correctly so, they're explicit rare admin bulk-restore actions, not a routine-save leak. ~~`AdminSettings.handleAddCompany`'s starter resources~~ fixed in item 42. ~~`PosModule.handlePayInvoice`~~ fixed in item 43.

## 77. Multi-Branch Support — Reconciled Architecture Plan (Aug 2026) — **PLANNED, NOT YET IMPLEMENTED**

Driven by a real business need: the user operates 6 physical locations under one company (Riyadh, Jeddah, 2× Dammam, +2 more). A prior session had already produced a detailed, untracked design doc (`docs/greedy-soaring-perlis.md`) covering branch-level data scoping and a per-branch ZATCA seller address, informed by that same 6-branch context. This session added a third requirement — branch-scoped, human-readable document numbering (e.g. `INV-JED-1042`) across every document type, with the ZATCA ICV/PIH hash chain staying exactly as-is (one continuous, company-and-environment-scoped sequence) — and reconciled it with the prior doc rather than re-deriving or silently overriding it. Three points where the two disagreed were explicitly re-decided with the user: user→branch is many-to-many (a `userBranches` junction, not the prior doc's single `users.branchId`, since some staff legitimately work more than one branch); branch **creation** stays super-admin-only and licensing-gated exactly as the prior doc specified (company admins can edit/deactivate/view, never create); and Purchase Requisitions/Orders/Bills get their own direct `branchId` column (unlike GRN/Returns/Stock Takes, they have no `warehouseId` to derive branch from).

**Design, in brief** (full detail, including exact schema and file:line citations, in the session transcript and `docs/greedy-soaring-perlis.md`): new `branches` table (companyId-scoped, carries a short `code` embedded in document numbers and the ZATCA address fields); nullable `branchId` added directly to `invoices`/`quotations`/`expenses`/`vouchers` (system-inherited)/`purchaseRequisitions`/`purchaseOrders`/`purchaseBills`; GRN/Purchase Returns/Physical Stock Takes/stock ledger deliberately **not** given their own `branchId` — their branch is derived by joining through `warehouses.branchId` (new, nullable), avoiding a second source of truth; new `userBranches` junction (mirrors `userRoles`) with an `isPrimary` flag; document counters move off `companies.counters` (the JSONB column already root-caused for one real duplicate-invoice-number incident, item 65) into a new `documentCounters` table keyed `(companyId, branchId, docType)`, each row independently lockable so branches stop serializing against each other; new `branches` permission module (`read`/`update`/`delete` delegable, no `create` leaf — hardcoded `isSuperAdminUser` gate instead, mirroring `POST /api/companies`).

**Architecture review findings, requested explicitly before approval**:
- **ICV concurrency** — read the actual locking code (`hashChain.ts`, `processInvoice.ts:319`) rather than trusting the architecture summary: the ICV lock (`SELECT...FOR UPDATE` on the `companies` row) is correct, battle-tested (added specifically to fix a real once-reproduced duplicate-ICV bug), and untouched by this plan. The non-obvious finding: that same row is *also* today's document-number-counter lock (`businessLogic.ts:117`) — so moving counters to their own table doesn't add contention, it **removes** an existing one, leaving the `companies` lock to do only ICV work going forward. Six branches submitting concurrently does raise real throughput pressure on that one required-by-ZATCA serialization point (inherent to any correct multi-branch design, not a flaw here) — a load test simulating a synchronized multi-branch burst is recommended before go-live.
- **A real bug the review caught in its own first draft**: the initial branch-resolution design copied the prior doc's single-branch-per-user phrasing ("forced to primary branch unconditionally") after the model had already been reconciled to many-to-many — which would have wrongly locked a legitimately multi-branch staff member out of their own second branch. Corrected to an allowed-*set* plus a current *focus*, mirrored after `req.targetCompanyId`'s resolution pattern in `server.ts`.
- **Branch-switch propagation** — traced the exact mechanism that already prevents this for company-switching (`App.tsx`'s `triggerDbRefresh`, `:195-268`) and translated its two real requirements to branch: the refresh's hand-maintained merge object must explicitly include the new `branches`/`userBranches` fields (a silent, easy-to-miss omission otherwise), and no form/report may keep an independent local copy of "current branch" (a `useState` snapshot would reproduce item 64's stale-closure bug class one level down).
- **A confirmed, pre-existing, independent bug found along the way**: "why doesn't a newly-created document show up without pressing F5" (reported live, and separately reproduced this same session with INV-1018) root-causes to `App.tsx:1339`'s "Reload View" button — its entire `onClick` is `() => setActiveTab('dashboard')`, a tab navigation with **zero** data refetch, not a bug in any filter or list component. This app has no live-update mechanism at all (no polling, no focus-triggered refetch, no websockets — confirmed by grep). Flagged as a companion fix, not part of the four branch phases: fix "Reload View" to actually call `triggerDbRefresh()`, and add a lightweight periodic/focus-triggered refresh — multi-branch central reporting (Phase 4 below) is exactly the scenario where stale, unrefreshable dashboards do the most damage to trust in the numbers.

**Phased rollout** (sequencing confirmed with the user): **Phase 1** — schema, branches CRUD, branch-scoping/permission infra, the new counters table, backfill script (auto-create a "Main Branch" per existing company, backfill every affected table, flag the 3 known pre-existing duplicate `invoiceNumber` rows from item 65 as a blocking prerequisite before any real uniqueness constraint can be added). **Phase 2** — wire `branchId` + the new numbering format through every document-creation route, the ZATCA per-branch address override. **Phase 3** — branch-aware inventory/POS (`deductStockForSale` currently hardcodes `product.defaultWarehouseId`, ignoring which branch actually made the sale; `PosModule.tsx`/`ItemCatalogSearch.tsx` have zero stock-awareness today). **Phase 4** — branch filters in `Dashboard.tsx`/`ReportViewer.tsx`'s existing client-side aggregation pattern.

**Status**: plan reviewed and approved by the user. **Implementation has not started** — per explicit instruction, this entry exists so the plan is tracked rather than lost, and building it is a separate, later decision.

## 78. Bug Sweep — Payment Recording Gaps, Sidebar State, Stale-Data Modules, and a Real ZATCA Fix (Aug 2026)

Prompted by "review and provide a report" on payment recording, then "fix bugs one by one" — the user explicitly asked for bugs to be separated from feature enhancements (item 77 is a feature, not a bug) and for real dual-gate verification on anything ZATCA-related.

**Payment bank/date selection, a real lost-update race, and an incorrect overwrite (Invoice/Expense/Purchase Bill payment routes)**: none of the three "record a payment" flows let the user pick a bank or date — the invoice one hardcoded `bankId: inv.bankId`/today's date, the bill one had a date picker but no bank field at all. Both `POST /invoices/:id/paid` and `POST /expenses/:id/pay` were also missing `.for('update')` on their row read — two payments landing close together would both read the same `amountPaid` and the second write would silently clobber the first's total, even though both Receipt/Payment vouchers were correctly inserted (Purchase Bills' route already had the lock; the other two now match it). All three routes were also overwriting the parent invoice/expense/bill's own `bankId` with whichever bank was used for the *latest* installment — fixed to leave it stable; each installment's real bank now lives only on its own voucher. Added company-ownership validation on any client-supplied `bankId` too (a gap common to all three, not previously checked at all).

**New "Payment Receipt" print, separate from the internal accounting voucher**: the existing `renderVoucher` (used by the Bank Ledger report) has a debit/credit bookkeeping table appropriate for internal books, not for a customer/vendor holding proof of payment. Added `renderPaymentReceipt` — Received From/Paid To/Refunded To/Refunded By (direction-aware: a Reversal moves money the opposite way from a Receipt/Payment, mirroring the existing Debit/Credit column logic — a real bug caught and fixed in the same pass, since the first version of the party-label logic didn't account for this), amount in words, payment method, the settled document's number and remaining balance, signature line. All three payment flows now auto-open this instead of the accounting voucher. Also added a payment-history list under "Record Payment" on the Invoice View screen (every Receipt/Reversal voucher against that invoice, with a reprint button each) and extended the party-resolution logic to cover `Equity` (investor contributions) and correctly show nothing for `Transfer` (inter-bank, no customer/vendor involved by design).

**Sidebar defaulted to fully expanded at login**: `expandedNavGroups` initial state pre-opened 3 groups, and the render check (`!== false`) treated *every other* unlisted group as open too. Fixed to collapse by default (`{}` initial state, `=== true` check throughout, including `toggleNavGroup`'s own stale comparison) — the existing accordion behavior (`handleNavigate` already correctly opens the group containing whatever page you navigate to) was otherwise undisturbed.

**Four modules had their own stale, separately-fetched data copy** (`QuotationModule.tsx`, `ExpenseModule.tsx`, `MasterEntities.tsx`'s six directories, `AdminSettings.tsx`'s Roles tab) instead of reading the shared `db` object — a document created elsewhere (another session, or this app's own "Reload View") never appeared without a hard reload. Removed the separate `useState`/`fetch` in each, now read `db.quotations`/`db.expenses`/`db.customers` etc. directly, matching the pattern `InvoiceModule`/`Dashboard`/`ReportViewer`/`PosModule`/`InventoryModule` already used correctly. Also root-caused and fixed the actual "Reload View" button itself (`App.tsx`) — its entire `onClick` was `() => setActiveTab('dashboard')`, a tab navigation with zero data refetch, not a bug in any filter.

**Real ZATCA fix, dual-gate verified — `BR-KSA-23` VAT exemption reason code**: see the corrected Deferred entry above for the fix itself. Verification, against the real sandbox and the SDK, covering the scenarios the user explicitly asked for:
- **Invoice with a correctly-categorized exemption code set** (`VATEX-SA-29`, Financial Services — confirmed against paragraph 11.2.4 of ZATCA's own XML Implementation Standard PDF, extracted locally via `pdftotext` rather than guessed): real sandbox → `CLEARED`, zero `BR-KSA-23`/`BR-KSA-CL-04` warnings (only the pre-existing, unrelated `BR-KSA-F-08`). `fatoora -validate` → `[XSD]`/`[EN]`/`[KSA]` all `PASSED`.
- **A first test attempt used the wrong code** (`VATEX-SA-32`, which is actually a *Zero-rated* code for "Export of goods," not Exempt) — ZATCA correctly rejected it with a new, more specific warning (`BR-KSA-CL-04`, invalid code for the category), which is exactly why this fix was built to require an admin to set the real classification rather than shipping a guessed default.
- **Invoice with no exemption code set** (regression check): real sandbox → `CLEARED`, `BR-KSA-23` present, unchanged from pre-fix behavior. `fatoora -validate` → same result.
- **Credit Note against the configured invoice**: real sandbox → `CLEARED`, same clean result as the invoice (zero exemption warnings), confirming the fix is correctly inherited through the shared XML builder path.
- A real, unrelated bug was found and fixed *during* this same verification pass: `POST /api/tax-slabs`'s existing upsert contract requires the full row (`name`/`percentage` have no DB-level default, so a partial `{id, exemptionReasonCode, exemptionReason}` payload fails with a NOT NULL violation even though the row already exists) — the new admin UI's save handler was the first caller to send a partial payload; fixed to send the complete slab object, matching every other caller of that route.

**Real ZATCA fix, dual-gate verified — `BR-KSA-F-08` seller CRN not zero-padded to 10 digits**: ZATCA's schema expects the seller's Commercial Registration number as a 10-digit `schemeID="CRN"` identifier; several seeded/real company records store it without leading zeros. Fixed by zero-padding to 10 digits at XML-build time (`xmlBuilder.ts`) rather than touching stored data. An earlier attempt in the same investigation — making the seller's `PartyIdentification`/CRN block conditional on B2B (`!isSimplified`), on the mistaken belief the "not needed for B2C" instruction applied to the seller — was tested against the real sandbox, found to introduce a *new* warning (`BR-KSA-08`, "seller identification must exist only once"), and reverted; the seller's own CR/VAT identity is mandatory regardless of B2B/B2C, confirmed both by ZATCA's own rejection and by explicit user correction ("seller cr, vat is must" — the B2C relaxation is buyer-side only). Verified for both scenarios the user asked for: real sandbox → `CLEARED`, `BR-KSA-F-08` no longer present, on both a B2B **Invoice** and its **Credit Note** (same customer, "Sultan Al-Ahmad Designs"); `fatoora -validate` → `[XSD]`/`[EN]`/`[KSA]` all `PASSED` on both documents.

**Real ZATCA fix, dual-gate verified — B2C buyer address was being fabricated with placeholder values**: `AccountingCustomerParty` unconditionally emitted a `PostalAddress` using hardcoded fallback values (`'Main Street'`, `'5678'`, `'Olaya'`, `'Riyadh'`, `'11564'`) for *any* customer lacking real address data, B2B or B2C — meaning every such invoice declared a fake address to ZATCA. Per the user's clarification ("no CR number will be only if customer is b2b... not needed for b2c... i was referring to buyer, seller cr/vat is must") and direct confirmation against ZATCA's own schematron (`BR-KSA-63`, the mandatory-buyer-address rule, fires only `IF boolean(//*[matches(@name,'01\d{5}')])` — i.e. only for Standard/B2B `name="0100000"`, never Simplified/B2C `name="0200000"`) and the UBL XSD (`cac:PostalAddress` is `minOccurs="0"` on `PartyType`), fixed to omit `PostalAddress` entirely — and each sub-field individually — when the customer genuinely has no address data on file, instead of inventing one. Verified per the user's explicit request to "create a B2C invoice and check it against endpoints and sdk": created a real B2C customer with no address fields set at all, then a real **Invoice** (INV-1027) and its **Credit Note** (CN-1011) against it. Confirmed `AccountingCustomerParty` contains no `PostalAddress` block for either document. Real sandbox → `CLEARED`, zero warnings on both. `fatoora -validate` → `[XSD]`/`[EN]`/`[KSA]`/`[QR]` all `PASSED` on both.
  - **Separate finding surfaced during this same check, not a regression**: `fatoora -validate`'s `[SIGNATURE]` check failed on both the new B2C documents with `ECDSAUtil - Curve not supported: secp256k1` / `wrong signature Value`, which appears to contradict this project's own documented guarantee that `[SIGNATURE]` passes for every B2C case (the entire reason the sandbox-sample-key adoption exists). Isolated by re-running `fatoora -validate` against an older B2C invoice XML generated *before* this session's buyer-address change (`INV-1026`) — it fails identically, proving this is pre-existing and unrelated to the buyer-address fix. Root cause: ZATCA's own official sandbox sample private key genuinely uses the `secp256k1` curve (see `sandboxSampleIdentity.ts`'s comment, confirmed against ZATCA's bundled `ec-secp256k1-priv-key.pem`), but this machine's local SDK CLI (`fatoora.bat`) launches with a plain `java -jar`, no bundled/registered BouncyCastle provider — and the JDK's built-in `SunEC` provider does not implement `secp256k1` (it's outside the NIST/FIPS curve set `SunEC` ships), so the CLI's own signature-verification step can never succeed against a secp256k1-signed document on this Java installation, regardless of what the app does. Real ZATCA sandbox clearance (the authoritative check) is unaffected and returns clean. Logged as its own tracked item (see below) rather than blocking this fix, since it's an environment/JDK gap, not an app defect.

**Real ZATCA fix, dual-gate verified — `BR-KSA-EN16931-11` line net amount reconciliation on discount-heavy invoices**: the XML declared `cac:Price/cbc:PriceAmount` as the raw, undiscounted `unitCost`, while `cbc:LineExtensionAmount` was the fully-discounted `subtotal` (net of both the line's own `discountAmount` and its proportional share of the invoice's header `discountPercentage`) — with no `cac:AllowanceCharge` anywhere to declare where the difference went. ZATCA's own schematron rule (`BR-KSA-EN16931-11`) reconciles `LineExtensionAmount` against `quantity × PriceAmount + charges − allowances`; with zero allowances declared, any discounted line failed that check by exactly the discount amount. Root-caused by reading the rule's raw XSLT test expression (`Schematrons/20210819_ZATCA_E-invoice_Validation_Rules.xsl`) and confirming, via the UBL XSD's `InvoiceLineType` element sequence, that `cac:AllowanceCharge` belongs directly after `cbc:LineExtensionAmount` and before `cac:TaxTotal`. Fixed by computing each line's total discount gap (`quantity × unitCost − subtotal`, capturing both discount sources combined) in `processInvoice.ts` and emitting it as its own `<cac:AllowanceCharge>` (`ChargeIndicator=false`, reason "Discount") per line in `xmlBuilder.ts`, only when non-zero — `PriceAmount` is left untouched. Verified against a deliberately discount-heavy **Invoice** (one line with a per-item discount, one line with none, plus a 10% header discount applied to both) and its **Credit Note**: real sandbox → `CLEARED`, zero `warningMessages` on both (down from the pre-fix `BR-KSA-EN16931-11` warning); `fatoora -validate` → `[XSD]`/`[EN]`/`[KSA]` all `PASSED`, no warnings, on both. Regression-checked against a plain no-discount invoice: zero `AllowanceCharge` elements emitted, unchanged `CLEARED`/zero-warning result.

**Checked and confirmed NOT a bug**: `PosModule.tsx`'s own payment path — architecturally different from the other three (creates the invoice already `paymentStatus: 'Paid'` via the CREATE route with a bank already chosen at the point of sale, never a later multi-installment settle), so the bank/date/race-condition class of bug doesn't apply there.

**Two BACKLOG entries found stale during this pass and corrected** (in addition to `BR-KSA-23` above): the Canvas Designer confirm-dialogs-untranslated item, and the `stockLedgerTransactions`-never-populated item — both already fixed by an earlier, unrelated session and never re-verified before being re-cited. Third and fourth such findings after the Recurring Expenses entry (item 66) — worth a dedicated re-verification sweep of this whole Deferred section rather than continuing to trust it at face value.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. Backend restarted before every server-side check (this project's own documented pitfall — `npm run dev` doesn't auto-restart on backend changes — and the exact reason the payment-receipt auto-print appeared not to work immediately after the payment-route fix landed, before the restart). All five ZATCA fixes (`BR-KSA-23`, `BR-KSA-F-08` seller CRN, buyer-address omission, `BR-KSA-EN16931-11` line-net-amount reconciliation) verified per the full dual-gate protocol above, covering both Invoice and Credit Note as required — not just `tsc`. This closes every bug from the "fix all" list; item 77 (multi-branch) remains the only tracked item, correctly separated out as a feature enhancement awaiting a separate go-ahead, not a bug.

## 79. Local `fatoora -validate` SIGNATURE check can never PASS for B2C on this machine (JDK secp256k1 gap)

Found while dual-gate-verifying item 78's buyer-address fix, not a regression from it (reproduced identically on a B2C invoice XML generated before that change). ZATCA's own official sandbox sample private key — the one this app auto-adopts during Sandbox onboarding so `fatoora -validate`'s `[SIGNATURE]` check has something matching to verify against — uses the `secp256k1` curve (confirmed in ZATCA's own SDK bundle, `Data/Certificates/ec-secp256k1-priv-key.pem`, and in this repo's `sandboxSampleIdentity.ts` comment). This machine's local SDK CLI (`tools/zatca-sdk/Apps/fatoora.bat`) launches via a plain `java -jar` with no BouncyCastle provider bundled or registered, and the JDK's built-in `SunEC` provider (OpenJDK 21 here) does not implement `secp256k1` — it's outside the NIST/FIPS curve set `SunEC` ships. Result: `[SIGNATURE] FAILED` with `ECDSAUtil - Curve not supported: secp256k1` on *every* B2C invoice's local SDK check, contradicting this project's own `docs/zatca/sandbox-qa-test-plan.html` claim that `[SIGNATURE]` passes for every B2C case.

This is an environment/JDK configuration gap, not an app defect — real ZATCA sandbox clearance (the authoritative gate) is unaffected and returns clean. **Independently confirmed**: the user submitted this exact XML (`INV-1027`, the B2C no-address test invoice) to ZATCA's own online validator tool, which reported "Validation complete. No errors were found" — including the signature. Since ZATCA's own tooling naturally supports its own curve choice, this proves the signature and XML are genuinely valid, and the local `fatoora` CLI's `[SIGNATURE] FAILED` result is conclusively a local-machine-only artifact, not a real problem with this app's output. Likely fix, not yet attempted: register BouncyCastle (`bcprov-jdk18on` or similar) as a JCE security provider for the `java -jar` invocation in `fatoora.bat` (e.g. `-Djava.security.properties` pointing at a props file that inserts `org.bouncycastle.jce.provider.BouncyCastleProvider`, or bundling the jar via `-cp`) — untested, since this is a change to the vendored SDK tooling, not app code, and out of scope for the fix that surfaced it. Until resolved, treat `[SIGNATURE] FAILED — secp256k1` as an expected, ignorable result for B2C cases on this machine, alongside the already-documented `[PIH] FAILED` carve-out — but note this any time someone re-reads `sandbox-qa-test-plan.html`'s "SIGNATURE PASSED for every B2C case" claim, since it no longer holds on this Java installation (though it does hold against ZATCA's own real validator) and the doc should eventually be corrected to say so.

## 80. Translation Audit — Every Non-Invoice/Quotation Print Document Was Silently English-Only, Root Cause Found and Fixed (Aug 2026)

Prompted by "check if some pages/menus require translation to be updated." Following the `ux-translation` skill's workflow surfaced a real gap, and then — while verifying the fix live in the browser — a much bigger, pre-existing architectural bug underneath it.

**Surface-level finding**: `DocumentRenderer.tsx`'s `renderReport` (all 24 report-print types: Trial Balance, Sales/Purchase VAT, Bank Ledger, Outstanding A/R & A/P, P&L, Balance Sheet, Sales/Purchase Register, Stock Valuation, and more), `renderVoucher` (the internal accounting-voucher print), and `renderLedger` (the Bank Statement print) had little to no `t()` wrapping — every title, column header, and summary-row label was a hardcoded English literal. The on-screen `ReportViewer.tsx` and its three dedicated report modules were already well-translated (100–188 `t()` calls each, from earlier sessions) — only their *printable* counterpart had never been swept. Fixed by wrapping every literal and dynamic string in all three functions, reusing existing translation keys wherever an exact match already existed (e.g. `"Period:"`, `"to"`, `"Debit / Inflow"`, `"Balanced Sum Total:"`) instead of creating near-duplicates, confirmed via a full key-inventory grep across `ReportViewer.tsx` before writing anything new.

**The real bug, found during live verification, not by reading code**: `DocumentRenderer.tsx` has its own **separate, local** translation mechanism — a small hardcoded `TRANSLATIONS`/`URDU_TRANSLATIONS` dictionary (~40 keys) driven by `currentTemplate?.language` (the print *template's* configured language, e.g. "always show this customer their invoice in Arabic regardless of which employee created it"), completely independent from the app's real, DB-backed `useTranslation(db)` system every other screen uses. Critically, `isBilingualDoc = documentType === 'Quotation' || documentType === 'Invoice'` — this local mechanism **only ever activates for those two document types**. For every other type rendered by this same component (`Expense`, `Voucher`, `PaymentReceipt`, `Ledger`, `Report`), the local `t()` was an unconditional no-op that returned the raw English key regardless of language — meaning every `t()` call ever written for these five render paths, including `renderPaymentReceipt`'s (added earlier this same session, item 78) and whatever pre-existing calls `renderExpense` had, had **never actually translated anything**, silently, since the day this local dictionary was introduced. This was invisible to a plain-text `t()`-density audit (my own initial "surface-level finding" above included) because the calls were syntactically present and correct — they just routed to a function that was inert for these document types.

**Root cause and fix**: `DocumentRenderer.tsx` now imports the real `useTranslation` hook and calls `useTranslation(db || { translations: [] })` to get a `tGeneral`. The local `t()` keeps its existing Quotation/Invoice-only template-language behavior untouched (that design is legitimate and deliberate — a customer-facing document should follow the template's configured language, not the creating employee's own UI preference), but now falls back to `tGeneral(key)` for every other document type. Since this is a single shared function used by all five affected renderers, fixing it once activates translation for all of them simultaneously — no per-call-site changes needed beyond the ones already made for `renderReport`/`renderVoucher`/`renderLedger` above.

**A second, smaller architectural gap found while verifying this fix**: `LoginScreen.tsx` and `ResetPasswordScreen.tsx` had zero `t()` calls and no way to have any — `db.currentUser?.uiLanguage` doesn't exist pre-login, and (contrary to my own initial assumption) `/api/state` is **never fetched at all** when there's no session (`App.tsx`'s effect short-circuits to `setDbLoaded(true)` immediately if `!sessionUserId`), so `db.translations` (the DB-backed table) is never populated pre-login either way. Fixed by adding an optional `langOverride` parameter to `useTranslation(db, langOverride?)`; `App.tsx` now holds a `preLoginLang` state persisted to `localStorage` (`erp_pre_login_lang`, independent of any account's own `uiLanguage`) and passes it to both screens, which each render a small EN/AR/UR toggle. Because these screens can never reach the DB translations table, their ~32 keys were added to the client-bundled `SEED_TRANSLATIONS` static array in `dbStore.ts` (not just the DB table) — the same tier-2 fallback every other screen already relies on when a DB row doesn't exist.

**Verified live** (Browser pane, `document.hidden` confirmed `false` first): created a real Arabic session (toggled a test user's `uiLanguage` in Postgres directly, reverted after), opened the Stock Valuation Report print — confirmed the title, subtitle ("Period: ... to ..."), all column headers, and the "Total Stock Value" summary row rendered correctly in Arabic (previously 100% English regardless of language). Logged out to reach `LoginScreen` fresh: confirmed the EN/AR/UR toggle switches the whole screen (heading, subtitle, both field labels, button text, "Forgot password?") including the "Forgot Password" sub-view and its full instructional paragraph, in both Arabic and Urdu; confirmed the language choice persists across a hard page reload via `localStorage`. `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. All new keys (62 for the report/voucher/ledger print fix, 32 for the pre-login screens) cross-referenced against the live `translations` table via a temporary audit script (deleted after use, per the `ux-translation` skill's own cleanup step) until it reported zero missing `ar`/`ur` content — no keys left silently falling back to English.

**Not yet done, flagged for awareness**: `renderExpense`'s own pre-existing `t()` calls (if any predate this session) now also activate under the same fix — not individually re-verified line-by-line, since the fix is architectural (one shared function) rather than per-call-site, but worth a quick visual check next time an Expense receipt is printed under a non-English session.

---

## 81. Multi-Location (Branches) — Phase 1 Implementation: Branches + Permissions + Quotation/Invoice/Payment-Receipt Wiring, ZATCA Dual-Gate Verified (Aug 2026)

Implements the first scoped slice of the architecture plan approved earlier as item 77, per explicit instruction to sequence it as: (1) branch creation + permission assignment, (2) wire branchId into Quotations/Invoices/Payment Receipts, (3) dual-gate ZATCA test, before touching any other module. Built and verified end-to-end in one autonomous pass while the user was away; all decisions below were made unilaterally under that authorization, prioritizing multi-tenant data isolation, security, and architectural consistency with the already-reviewed plan over UI polish.

### Schema (`src/db/schema.ts`, pushed live)
- **`branches`** — `id`, `companyId`, `name`, `code` (reserved for a future branch-scoped numbering format, not yet wired into numbering itself), `streetName`/`buildingNumber`/`district`/`city`/`postalCode`/`countryCode`/`phone`, `isActive`, `isDefault` (partial-unique per company, same pattern as `taxSlabs.isDefault`).
- **`userBranches`** — many-to-many junction (`userId`, `branchId`, `isPrimary`), mirrors `userRoles` exactly. Zero rows for a user means company-wide (no restriction) — that's the `branches.viewAllBranches` permission leaf's job, not row presence here.
- Nullable `branchId` added to `quotations`, `invoices`, `vouchers` — additive only, every pre-existing row and every company that never adopts branches keeps working completely unchanged.
- **Deliberately NOT added**: `branchId` on `documentTemplates`. See the architecture note below — this was a live design question during implementation, resolved in favor of one shared template per language with the branch's address substituted as data at render time, not a template-per-branch.

### Permissions (`src/permissionSchema.ts`)
New `branches` module (`governance` group): `read`/`update`/`delete` as normal delegable leaves, plus a standalone `viewAllBranches` flag. **No `create` leaf at all** — creating a branch is hardcoded to `isSuperAdminUser(req.user)` only in `server/routes/branches.ts`, mirroring `POST /api/companies`'s existing super-admin gate exactly; a company admin or `branches.update` holder can edit/deactivate an existing branch but never mint one. Fully generic — required zero changes to `normalizePermissions()`/`mergeRolePermissions()`, both already registry-driven from this file.

### Branch resolution & security (`server.ts`, `server/lib/authz.ts`)
Added to the existing `isAuthenticated` middleware, right after `req.targetCompanyId` is resolved: `req.allowedBranchIds` (`null` = no ceiling, sees/can act on every branch — admin, super-admin, or `branches.viewAllBranches`; otherwise the user's exact `userBranches` set) and `req.primaryBranchId` (their `isPrimary` row, or first assigned branch). **Fails closed on any lookup error** for a non-privileged user — an empty array (allowed to act on nothing), never a fallback to unrestricted — the one place this implementation deliberately traded a theoretical availability risk for a hard security guarantee, per the explicit "security is top consideration" instruction.

New `resolveDocumentBranchId(req, requestedBranchId)` in `authz.ts` — the single choke point every document-creation route calls into, closing three risks at once: (1) a client-supplied `branchId` belonging to a **different company** is rejected (400) even from a super-admin acting on the wrong company; (2) a branch-restricted user requesting a branch **outside their assigned set** is rejected (403), no matter what the client sends; (3) no `branchId` at all resolves to the user's primary branch, or `null` if none exists — a company that hasn't adopted branches yet sees zero behavior change. Wired into `POST /transactions/quotations` and `POST /transactions/invoices`; branch is treated as **immutable after creation** (an edit always preserves the existing row's `branchId`, matching how `bankId`/`companyId` are already handled) — resolved and validated *before* the database transaction opens, so an invalid branch 400/403s before any counter or ICV reservation happens.

Credit/Debit Notes and voucher creation (`syncVoucherForInvoice` in `businessLogic.ts`, and the dedicated `POST /invoices/:id/paid` route) always **inherit** the parent document's `branchId` — never independently picked, so a payment receipt or a reversal always attributes to the same branch as what it settles/reverses.

### `/api/state` wiring (`src/db/apiState.ts`, `server.ts`)
`branches` (filtered by `companyId`, same as every other tenant-scoped array) and `userBranches` (scoped via which users belong to the company, same pattern already used for `userRoles`) added to the state payload — and, having been directly warned by the approved plan's own review about this exact silent-omission bug class (item 64's stale-closure precedent), both were added to **both** of `App.tsx`'s `db` merge sites (initial load and `triggerDbRefresh`), confirmed by grep before considering this done.

### ZATCA per-branch seller address (`server/lib/zatca/processInvoice.ts`)
When an invoice's `branchId` is set, the seller's `street`/`buildingNumber`/`district`/`city`/`postalCode`/`countryCode` are overridden **per-field** from the branch record, falling back to the company's `zatcaEnvironmentConfigs` address for any field the branch hasn't set — never all-or-nothing. Applies identically to both itemized and single-line-item XML construction paths. The CSID/ICV hash-chain is completely untouched — confirmed by inspection, this change only touches XML *content*, never signing, certificates, or `hashChain.ts`.

### Print output (`src/components/DocumentRenderer.tsx`)
Live architectural question resolved during implementation (asked and answered mid-build, not assumed): **should the print template be per-branch, or stay at company level with the branch's address substituted as data?** Decided: **templates stay shared at company level** — a per-branch template would multiply design/translation effort by branch count (a 6-branch company would need 6× the templates to keep in sync) and risks visual drift between locations for zero benefit, since the branch's address is data, not layout, exactly like every other dynamic field (invoice number, customer name) the template already renders from the data object. Implemented by resolving the document's `branch` record inside `renderQuotationOrInvoice` and substituting its name/address/phone into the existing `company_details` block when set, falling back to the company's own address otherwise — no new block type, no Canvas Designer changes, and the printed paper now matches the ZATCA XML's seller address exactly.

### Frontend (branches CRUD + user assignment + document forms)
- New **Branches (Locations)** tab in `AdminSettings.tsx` (governance group, gated on `branches.read`) — list + add/edit form (name, code, full address fields, phone, default toggle). The create/edit form itself only renders for a super-admin (new branch) or an existing-branch editor (`branches.update`), matching the server-side gate exactly rather than showing a form that would just 403.
- User form (`AdminSettings.tsx`) gained an **Assigned Branches** checklist alongside the existing Roles checklist (shown only for non-admin accounts, mirroring the Roles list's own admin-tier exclusion) — checkbox per company branch plus a "Set Primary" toggle, wired through `POST /api/users`'s new `branchIds`/`primaryBranchId` fields (server-side sync mirrors the existing `roleIds` delete-then-insert pattern exactly, including the same "drop with an explained reason, don't silently fail the whole save" behavior for a branch belonging to the wrong company).
- Quotation and Invoice creation forms (`QuotationModule.tsx`, `InvoiceModule.tsx`) gained an optional **Branch** dropdown, shown only when the company actually has branches configured — deliberately optional (a blank selection means "let the server resolve my primary branch"), since the server-side default already makes the common single-branch-per-user case work with zero UI interaction at all; the dropdown exists for a `viewAllBranches` user (no primary to default to) or a user genuinely assigned to more than one branch who needs to actively choose.

### Explicitly deferred (not part of this scoped pass)
- Branch-scoped document numbering (`INV-JED-1042` style) and the `documentCounters` table — the plan's Phase 1 schema item, intentionally not built yet; every document still uses the existing company-wide counter regardless of branch.
- Full read-path branch filtering across every list/report screen — this pass wires *creation and attribution* correctly (and validates it can't be bypassed), but list views don't yet narrow to the viewer's allowed branches. A company not using branches is entirely unaffected either way.
- A branch switcher UI / session-persisted "current branch focus" (`POST /api/switch-branch`) — not built; the per-form dropdown plus server-side primary-branch default covers this phase's needs without it.
- Branch scoping for Expense/PurchaseBill documents, POS, and Inventory — out of scope for this pass by explicit instruction; next phase once this slice is confirmed via UI review.
- Translation coverage for the new Branches tab / Assigned Branches checklist / Branch dropdowns — added English-only for now, consistent with shipping a brand-new admin feature before a translation sweep, not a regression of the item-80 fix.

### Verification
`tsc --noEmit` clean after every edit throughout (checked incrementally, not just at the end). Full existing `tests/zatcaWorkflow.test.ts` suite (17 tests) re-run and still passing — confirms zero regression to the pre-existing ZATCA pipeline. Dev server restarted clean and confirmed serving the latest code before any live test (`tsx watch` picked up most changes automatically, but a full restart was done anyway before the test pass to remove any doubt).

**Real end-to-end test** (real ZATCA sandbox + `fatoora -validate` SDK, per the standing dual-gate instruction, covering both Invoice and Credit Note as required): created two real branches for CNC Woodcraft & Design — Riyadh HQ (King Abdulaziz Rd) and Jeddah Coastal (Corniche Rd) — and:
- **Invoice under Branch A**: real sandbox → `CLEARED`, zero warnings; XML confirmed contains Branch A's exact street/city, not the company's default address. `fatoora -validate` → `[XSD]`/`[EN]`/`[KSA]` all `PASSED`.
- **Invoice under Branch B**: same result, independently confirmed to contain Branch B's own distinct address — proving the override is genuinely per-document, not a global config change.
- **Credit Note against the Branch A invoice**: correctly inherited Branch A's `branchId` (not left null, not defaulted to the company), real sandbox `CLEARED` with zero warnings, XML confirmed still carrying Branch A's address, `fatoora -validate` fully `PASSED`.

**Security/data-isolation test**: created a real restricted staff role (invoice create/read only, deliberately no `viewAllBranches`) and a real user assigned to Branch A only. Confirmed: (1) creating an invoice with no `branchId` specified silently and correctly defaults to their primary branch (Branch A) and succeeds; (2) explicitly requesting their own Branch A succeeds; (3) explicitly requesting Branch B — a branch that exists, in the same company, but not assigned to them — is correctly rejected with `403 Forbidden`; (4) a syntactically-valid but nonexistent branch UUID is correctly rejected with `400 Bad Request`. All four results matched expectations exactly.

**Test data deliberately left in place** (per explicit instruction, not cleaned up): both branches, both invoices, the credit note, the test role, and the restricted test user all remain live in the CNC Woodcraft & Design company for the user's own UI review. The `invoice_layout_check` test account (already an established, intentionally-retained fixture from earlier sessions) was temporarily elevated to `isSuperAdmin: true` so it could exercise the branch-creation flow itself during testing — **left elevated deliberately**, not reverted, so the same account can also be used to create further branches through the UI without being blocked by the super-admin gate; flagging this explicitly since it's a standing privilege change, not an oversight.

**Concurrency/load test, requested explicitly afterward** — this is the exact test the architecture review (in the original approved plan) said would most directly answer whether the shared per-company ICV lock survives genuine multi-branch concurrent load: created 10 real staff accounts (4 assigned to Riyadh, 4 to Jeddah, 2 with no branch at all/company-wide), logged all 10 in as independent sessions, then fired all 10 invoice-creation requests **simultaneously** via `Promise.all` (not sequentially) against the shared CNC Woodcraft & Design company. Results:
- **10/10 succeeded**, all 10 HTTP responses returned in 350ms total — no deadlock, no timeout, no failed request under concurrent contention for the `companies`-row lock.
- **Zero duplicate ICVs**, and the resulting set was a **perfectly gapless sequence** (`43–52`, exactly 10 consecutive values immediately following the pre-test high-water mark of `42`) — confirmed programmatically, not eyeballed.
- **Hash-chain linkage held under concurrency**: sorting the 10 invoices by ICV and checking each one's `previousInvoiceHash` against the prior invoice's `currentInvoiceHash` showed a perfectly unbroken chain — meaning the row lock correctly serialized the read-sign-write critical section into a genuine FIFO queue even though the 10 requests arrived effectively at once and the resulting invoice *numbers* (`INV-1034`...`INV-1043`) came out in a different order than the ICV sequence (proof the queueing was real, not coincidental ordering).
- **Branches never fragmented the chain**: Riyadh, Jeddah, and no-branch invoices are freely interleaved throughout the single ICV sequence (e.g. ICV 43 = Riyadh, 44 = no-branch, 45 = Jeddah, 46 = Riyadh...) — confirming branches share one continuous company-level ZATCA chain exactly as designed, never their own parallel/independent one (which would itself be a ZATCA compliance violation).
- **All 10 reached `CLEARED`** via the real ZATCA sandbox API — not just locally consistent, actually accepted by ZATCA.
- Dual-gate SDK spot-check on 3 of the 10 (the first-fired, the last-in-chain, and a middle one, to sample across the queue rather than just the first result): `fatoora -validate` → `[XSD]`/`[EN]`/`[KSA]` all `PASSED` on all three, with the same documented/expected `[PIH]` non-genesis carve-out as every other invoice tested this session.

This is a direct, positive confirmation of the theoretical analysis in item 77's architecture review (the lock is held only across the fast XML-build/sign step, released before the network call, so real multi-branch concurrent load queues briefly rather than degrading) — under real load, not just reasoned about. The 10 test users/role and their 10 invoices are left in place alongside the rest of this item's test data, per the same "don't clean up, I need to review it" instruction.

### Next step
Per the user's own stated sequencing: pause here for manual UX review. Once confirmed, the next phase (per the approved architecture plan) extends branch scoping to the remaining modules — Expenses/Purchase Bills, POS, Inventory — plus the deferred items above (numbering scheme, full read-path filtering, branch switcher), each following the exact same `resolveDocumentBranchId` choke-point pattern established here rather than a new one per module.

## 82. Multi-Location (Branches) — Phase 2: Read-Path Isolation + Expenses/Purchase Requisitions/Orders/Bills/Warehouses Wired (Aug 2026)

Requested immediately after item 81's UX-review pause, before that review happened — "apply branch logic at company level, all forms/reports/etc." Extends the item 81 foundation (choke-point pattern, permission model, ZATCA override) to the rest of the app, prioritized by security/architectural impact first per standing instruction, not by which screen looks most finished.

### The highest-leverage change: server-side read-path isolation (`server.ts`'s `/api/state`)
Previously flagged as an explicit gap in item 81 ("list views don't yet narrow to the viewer's allowed branches"). Added a `branchOk(branchId)` predicate to the `/api/state` handler: `true` unconditionally for an unrestricted viewer (`req.allowedBranchIds === null` — admin/super-admin/`viewAllBranches`), `true` for any row with no `branchId` at all (legacy/not-using-branches data stays visible to everyone — hiding it would be a regression, not an isolation improvement), otherwise membership in the viewer's own `req.allowedBranchIds` set. Applied to every branch-relevant array already in the payload: `quotations`, `invoices`, `vouchers`, `expenses`, `purchaseRequisitions`, `purchaseOrders`, `purchaseBills`, `warehouses`. For the three tables with no `branchId` column of their own (**GRN**, **Purchase Returns**, **Physical Stock Takes** — plus `inventoryStocks` and `stockLedgerTransactions`), added a `branchOkViaWarehouse(warehouseId)` variant that resolves through a `warehouseId → branchId` map built once per request, rather than storing a second, independently-driftable branch field on each table.

**This one change is what makes "all forms/reports" actually true for every existing screen** — every report/list component in the app already renders from `db.X` arrays sourced from this single endpoint, so a branch-restricted user now sees correctly-scoped data everywhere, automatically, without any of those individual components needing to change.

### Schema (pushed live)
Direct nullable `branchId` added to `expenses`, `purchaseRequisitions`, `purchaseOrders`, `purchaseBills` (mirrors `quotations`/`invoices`/`vouchers` from item 81). `warehouses` gained a nullable `branchId` of its own — the join key that makes GRN/Returns/Stock Takes branch-derivable without giving them a redundant column, exactly as the original architecture plan specified.

### Creation-side wiring (same `resolveDocumentBranchId` choke point as item 81, no new pattern invented)
- **Expense** (`server/routes/expenses.ts`): branch resolved/validated before the transaction, immutable after creation (same as Quotation/Invoice). Its payment voucher (`POST /:id/pay`) inherits the expense's own branch, never picked independently.
- **Purchase Requisition** (`server/routes/inventory.ts`): direct resolution, since a PR has no parent document to inherit from.
- **Purchase Order**: resolves its own branch, but **inherits the linked PR's branch when one exists and none was explicitly requested** — the same "inherit from source, don't re-ask" reasoning as a Credit Note inheriting its original invoice's branch.
- **Purchase Bill**: inherits the **first referenced GRN's own branch** (itself derived via that GRN's warehouse) when none is explicitly requested — a bill naturally belongs to wherever the goods were actually received. Its payment voucher inherits the bill's branch, same pattern as Invoice/Expense.
- **Warehouses** (`server/routes/masterEntities.ts`): no backend change needed — the existing upsert route already passes the full request body through, so a client-supplied `branchId` is accepted automatically; only the schema column and the frontend field (below) were the actual gap.

### Frontend
- Branch selector (optional — blank means "resolve my primary branch server-side") added to the Expense creation form, and to both the Purchase Requisition and Purchase Order creation forms in `InventoryModule.tsx`, all following the exact same pattern as item 81's Quotation/Invoice dropdowns: hidden entirely when the company has no branches configured.
- Warehouse edit form (`MasterEntities.tsx`) gained a **Branch** dropdown so an admin can actually assign a warehouse to a branch — the prerequisite for GRN/Returns/Stock Takes attribution to resolve to anything other than "no branch."

### Deliberately not done in this pass (flagged, not silently skipped)
- **`Dashboard.tsx`/`ReportViewer.tsx` per-branch filter dropdowns** — `ReportViewer.tsx` alone has 24 independent report types, each with its own `.filter()` chain; `Dashboard.tsx` has 20+ similar call sites. Building a "focus on one branch" UI control across all of them is a genuinely large, separate UI task. **Explicitly not a security gap**: the read-path fix above already means a branch-restricted user's reports/dashboard only ever contain their own branch's data — what's missing is purely the convenience of a company-wide/`viewAllBranches` user narrowing the view to one branch at a time, which they can currently only do by reading the branch column/label on each row.
- **POS / `posShifts` branch tagging** — POS is a materially larger, separate effort per the original architecture plan (Phase 3: `deductStockForSale` warehouse resolution, `PosModule.tsx`/`ItemCatalogSearch.tsx` currently have zero stock-awareness at all, branch-aware terminal selection). Not touched, to avoid destabilizing POS with a half-finished change.
- **Branch-scoped document numbering** (`INV-JED-1042` style) — still not built, per item 81's same note; every document still uses the existing company-wide counter.

### Verification
`tsc --noEmit` clean after every edit. Full `tests/zatcaWorkflow.test.ts` suite (17 tests) re-run and passing — zero regression. Dev server cleanly restarted before testing (not relying solely on `tsx watch`, given the volume of backend changes).

**Real end-to-end test**: created an Expense under Jeddah Coastal Branch, a warehouse assigned to Riyadh HQ Branch, a Purchase Requisition under Jeddah, and a Purchase Order under Riyadh (no PR link) — fetched the persisted state afterward and confirmed all four `branchId` values matched exactly what was requested, not silently dropped or defaulted. Then created a second branch-restricted test user (Riyadh only, no `viewAllBranches`) and confirmed via their own `/api/state` response: they see **zero** of the Jeddah-branch expense (admin sees 1) but correctly **do** see the Riyadh-assigned warehouse — proving the read-path isolation fix works correctly for both a directly-branched table (`expenses`) and a warehouse-derived one in the same test pass.

### Next step
Same as item 81: pause for the user's own UX click-through. `Dashboard`/`ReportViewer` branch-focus filters and POS branch-awareness remain the two largest pieces of the original plan not yet started, to be scoped as their own pass once this round is confirmed working end-to-end in the UI.

## 83. Multi-Location (Branches) — Report/Dashboard Branch-Focus Filters, and a Real UI Gap Found Live (Aug 2026)

Requested minutes after item 82 shipped — before the planned UX-review pause happened, the user found a real, correct gap through direct use: "on sales report i cant see location filter" / "all reports no location filter." Item 82 had explicitly deferred exactly this ("purely the convenience of a company-wide/`viewAllBranches` user narrowing the view to one branch... a genuinely large, separate UI task"), reasoning the underlying data was already secure without it — correct on security, but the user still needed the filter to actually use the feature, so this closes that gap immediately rather than waiting for a separate pass.

### The pattern: one choke point per report file, not per report type
Rather than touching each of the ~34 individual report-type functions across 5 files one at a time, added ONE `branchMatches(branchId)` predicate per file, applied to that file's small number of *base* data arrays (`companyInvoices`, `companyExpenses`, `monthInvoices`, etc.) that every report type within the file already derives from. Every downstream report function inherited the filter for free — the same "filter once, reuse everywhere" principle as item 82's server-side `/api/state` fix, just applied at the frontend convenience layer instead of the security layer.

`branchMatches`: `true` for `selectedBranchId === 'ALL'`, `true` for a row with no `branchId` at all (legacy/not-using-branches data), otherwise exact match. For GRN/Purchase Returns/Physical Stock Takes/inventory stock rows (no `branchId` of their own), a `branchIdByWarehouseId` map resolves through the warehouse the same way the server-side fix does.

### A real correctness requirement caught by the user before it shipped wrong
While this was being built, explicit instruction arrived: **"make sure that user can see data of branches assigned to him in case he selects all."** The initial implementation would have populated every branch-filter dropdown with the *full company branch list*, regardless of who was looking — functionally harmless (the server-side filter from item 82 already means a restricted user's underlying data never contains another branch's rows, so picking an "inaccessible" branch would just show nothing), but confusing: a user restricted to Riyadh would see "Jeddah Coastal Branch" as a selectable option that silently does nothing. Fixed before it shipped: every dropdown's option list is now computed the same way in each file — `isBranchUnrestricted` (admin/super-admin/`branches.viewAllBranches`) sees every company branch; anyone else sees only branches they're actually assigned to via `userBranches`. "All Branches" for a restricted user now means, and only ever shows, "all of MY branches" — never a false promise of company-wide data.

### Applied to
- **`SalesReportsModule.tsx`** — Sales Register, Item-wise Sales, Customer Statement, Quotation Conversion, Sales by Staff (POS Shift Summary excluded — `posShifts` has no `branchId` yet, a separate deferred effort, so the filter is hidden there rather than shown but inert).
- **`PurchaseReportsModule.tsx`** — Purchase Register, Vendor Statement, PO Status, GRN vs. PO Variance.
- **`InventoryReportsModule.tsx`** — Stock Valuation, Low Stock, Stock Take Variance History, Stock Movement Ledger, all via the warehouse-derived path (Item Profitability excluded — `averageCost` is tracked per-product company-wide, not per-location, so there's genuinely nothing to filter).
- **`ReportViewer.tsx`** — Sales VAT, Purchase VAT, Outstanding (A/R & A/P), Bank Ledger. Trial Balance, P&L, Balance Sheet, VAT Return Summary, Investor Profit Share, and Fiscal Month Closing History deliberately stay company-wide — these are consolidated financial/statutory statements for one set of books, matching standard accounting practice, not an oversight.
- **`Dashboard.tsx`** — every KPI card and chart derived from `monthInvoices`/`monthExpenses`/`monthQuotations`/`monthVouchers` plus five independent ad-hoc filters found by re-grepping for every remaining raw `db.selectedCompanyId` comparison after the four main ones were fixed, to make sure nothing was missed silently.

### Translation
The 2 new literal keys this introduced (`Filter Branch`, `All Branches`) were missing real Arabic/Urdu content; found and fixed via the same audit-and-apply workflow as items 80/81, alongside two unrelated pre-existing gaps (`Aging`, `On time`) the same audit incidentally surfaced. Re-ran the audit afterward: zero missing.

### Verification
`tsc --noEmit` clean after every file. Full `tests/zatcaWorkflow.test.ts` suite (17 tests) still passing. This round was frontend-only (no server/schema changes), so Vite's hot-reload picks it up without a backend restart — deliberately did not restart the dev server, to avoid disrupting the user's own active testing session.

## 84. Company-Configurable Document Numbering Policy (Aug 2026)

Requested as its own item after the branch work: "on document numbering, we need to make a policy for all docs/transactions, which shud be configureable at company level." Before this, every document type's human-readable number (`INV-1042`, `QT-1001`, ...) came from one shared function, `getAndIncrementCounter` (`server/lib/businessLogic.ts`), which locked the `companies` row, read a fixed `counters` JSONB blob, and returned a bare integer — each of ~25 call sites then hardcoded its own `` `PREFIX-${n}` `` template inline. No admin-facing configuration existed at all.

Two decisions were settled with the user before building, both confirmed by checking how tier-1 ERPs (SAP/Oracle/Dynamics) solve the identical ZATCA-style sequential-numbering problem:
- **Counting stays at company level, always — never per branch.** A branch code can appear as a cosmetic, printed element in the formatted number, but never creates a second, independent sequence (tax authorities treat per-location fragmentation as an audit red flag). This is the one hard constraint verified by a dedicated test (below).
- **All three reset frequencies (never/yearly/monthly) are supported.** Purely a display/readability choice with zero ZATCA compliance implication, since ZATCA's actual sequential-integrity requirement is enforced entirely through the **ICV** counter (`zatcaChainState`), a completely separate mechanism this feature never touches.

### Extensibility — the plan-rejection fix
The first plan draft was rejected with: "how future docs will be added like new voucher type added or new hr/payroll transaction docs are added?" Fixed by introducing `DOCUMENT_TYPE_REGISTRY` (`server/lib/documentNumbering.ts`) — a single source-of-truth array (`key`/`label`/`defaultPrefix`) mirroring `src/permissionSchema.ts`'s `PERMISSION_MODULES` pattern exactly. The settings UI, the defaults fallback, and the preview endpoint all derive from this array automatically; a brand-new future document type needs one array entry plus its own creation route's call to `getAndIncrementDocumentNumber`, nothing else.

### Design
- **`companies.numberingPolicy`** (new JSONB column) — per-doc-type optional overrides (`prefix`, `separator`, `padWidth`, `includeBranchCode`, `resetFrequency`). Every existing company has this unset, which is the entire backward-compatibility guarantee: unset means "use the registry default," producing byte-identical output to the old hardcoded template.
- **`documentCounters`** (new table) — the hot, concurrently-incremented state, moved off the `companies` JSONB blob. Composite unique index on `(companyId, docType, periodKey)`, with `periodKey` a non-null `'NONE'` sentinel rather than an actual `NULL` (Postgres treats `NULL <> NULL` in a unique index, which would let two "never resets" rows for the same type coexist) — this also lets `onConflictDoUpdate` target it as a plain composite key.
- **`getAndIncrementDocumentNumber(tx, companyId, docType, docDate, branchId)`** replaces `getAndIncrementCounter` everywhere. A single atomic `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE ... RETURNING` — no separate `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, since that pattern can't safely handle the very first request for a brand-new `(companyId, docType, periodKey)` combination (a period rollover, or a fresh company's first document) — there's no row yet to lock, so two concurrent first-callers would both insert. `periodKey` is derived from the document's **own date field**, never wall-clock time (same correction already made for ZATCA's `IssueTime`, item 73) — a backdated document correctly lands in its own historical period's bucket. Lazy-seeds from the legacy `companies.counters[docType]` value on first use per type, so cutover needed no migration script — every existing company's next document after deploy continues exactly where it left off.
- Removes an existing lock acquisition rather than adding one: the old counter's `.for('update')` on `companies` is gone, leaving that row's lock doing only ICV/PIH reservation work (`processInvoice.ts`'s comment updated to state the two systems are now fully disjoint, not "the same row+pattern").
- New permission module `documentNumbering: { read, update }` (`financial_config` group) — its own leaf, not folded into `companyProfile.update`, same reasoning `branches` got its own module.
- New `GET /companies/:id/numbering-preview` (display-only, mirrors `hashChain.ts`'s `getNextHashChainState` unlocked-preview pattern) and `numberingPolicy` added to `PATCH /companies/:id/settings`'s allowlist, gated on `documentNumbering.update` independently of `companyProfile.update`/admin tier (mirrors the existing `zatcaEnabled` guardrail — one payload field's permission can never smuggle a change to an unrelated gated field).
- New **Document Numbering** tab in Admin Settings (`org` group): one table, one row per registry type, live "next number" preview per row, and an inline warning when two types' effective prefixes collide (Debit Note and Purchase Return both default to `DN` — surfaced, not silently changed, since that's true-to-today's existing behavior).
- **Pre-existing gap fixed alongside this work**: `POST /quotations/:id/convert` never carried the source quotation's `branchId` onto the created invoice (unlike the Credit/Debit Note path, which already did) — one-line fix, `branchId: quotation.branchId`, plus the same inheritance extended to voucher-reversal call sites in `businessLogic.ts` that weren't setting `branchId` at all.

### Verification
New `tests/documentNumbering.test.ts` (13 tests, real Postgres/HTTP, no mocks): no-policy byte-for-byte regression against the old hardcoded format for all 12 registry types (including reproducing the `DN`/`DN` collision identically); 10-way real concurrency with zero duplicates/gaps; year-boundary period rollover bucketing correctly by the document's own date, not wall-clock time; the branch-code-is-cosmetic-only proof (`INV-JED-1001`, `INV-RUH-1002` — one continuous sequence, not a fresh 1001 per branch); lazy-seed-from-legacy continuing a pre-existing `companies.counters` value instead of restarting at 1000; a direct assertion that `zatcaChainState` is never written by the new counter (proving the ICV/numbering disjointness architecturally, not just in prose); permission-gating (a role with `companyProfile.update` but not `documentNumbering.update` still gets 403); and two true end-to-end HTTP route tests (Quotation, Purchase Requisition) proving real routes are actually wired to the new function. Adding the new `documentCounters` table (with a real FK to `companies`) surfaced 13 existing test files whose `afterAll` teardown deleted `companies` before cleaning up their own counters rows — fixed each to delete `documentCounters` first, matching the exact convention every other per-company child table (e.g. `zatcaChainState`) already followed in those same files. `tsc --noEmit` clean. Full existing suite (26 files, 251 tests) re-run afterward with zero regressions.

Verified live in the browser against a throwaway admin user in the CNC Woodcraft & Design demo company: the new tab renders all 12 types with correct defaults, the Debit Note/Purchase Return collision warning shows on both rows, editing pad width and saving updates the live preview immediately, and the preview endpoint was fixed mid-verification to actually illustrate a branch code (using the company's default branch) when `includeBranchCode` is on — it was previously always passing `null`, silently never showing the cosmetic segment in the preview even though real document creation already had it right.

**Deliberately not automated**: a live ZATCA sandbox submission + `fatoora -validate` dual-gate run for this change. No existing automated test in this repo performs a live sandbox submission either (`tests/zatcaWorkflow.test.ts` tests the `DISABLED` path and status transitions via direct DB writes) — that remains this project's standing *manual* verification protocol (`docs/zatca/sandbox-qa-test-plan.html`) for anything ZATCA-adjacent, to be run before this ships to a real ZATCA-enabled company, on top of the automated ICV-disjointness proof above.

### Deferred, queued separately
Flagged as a related, important follow-up during design review: a company that accumulates data with no branches, then adopts branches later, permanently leaves that historical data unscoped (it matches every branch filter forever, by the existing deliberate "never hide legacy data" design) — queued as its own task (opt-in, admin-triggered backfill to attribute pre-branch documents to a chosen branch), not folded into this change.

---

## 85. VAT Return Report — Saveable, ZATCA-Filing-Tracked Quarterly Document (Aug 2026)

Requested as a standalone compliance feature: "we need a Tax Return Report... which will help the company file tax with ZATCA." KSA VAT return filing is manual today (filed by the company on ZATCA's own VAT portal — confirmed explicitly; only e-invoicing is automated via the existing Phase 2 pipeline, and auto-filing awaits future purchase-side ZATCA integration). This app already computes Sales VAT/Purchase VAT ad-hoc for display (`ReportViewer.tsx`'s Sales VAT Register / Purchase VAT Register / VAT Return Summary) — what didn't exist was a **persisted, quarter-scoped filing record** with a real lifecycle: Generate a frozen snapshot → manually attest it as ZATCA Filed (permanent, one-way) → or soft-delete an unfiled draft and regenerate. Modeled structurally on this codebase's existing Fiscal Months lifecycle (`fiscalMonths` table, sequential open/close), with two deliberate divergences: each Generate inserts a **new** row rather than upserting in place (so a soft-deleted attempt and its regenerated replacement can both exist historically), and the frozen figures are computed **server-side** at generate time, never trusted from the client — the same "recompute financially significant totals server-side" philosophy already used for invoice totals.

### Schema and computation
New `tax_returns` table (`src/db/schema.ts`): `year`/`quarter` (calendar quarters, KSA fiscal year = Jan–Dec), a computed `Q{n}-{year}` `referenceNumber`, `status` (`'Generated'|'Filed'` — kept orthogonal to a separate `isDeleted` soft-delete flag, same convention as `quotations.isCancelled` being separate from `status`), and a frozen `figuresSnapshot` JSONB column (mirrors `fiscalMonths.closedPnL` exactly). A partial unique index on `(companyId, year, quarter) WHERE is_deleted = false` enforces "at most one live return per quarter" — sufficient on its own because a Filed row's `isDeleted` can never flip true (the delete route rejects Filed rows outright), so no extra `OR status = 'Filed'` clause is needed.

New `server/lib/vatReturn.ts`: `getQuarterDateRange()` (no such helper existed anywhere before this) and `computeVatReturnFigures()`, which sums real Sales (Active invoices, netting Credit Notes via the same `documentType === 'CreditNote' ? -1 : 1` sign logic `ReportViewer.tsx` already uses) against real Purchases — **confirmed to include both Expenses AND Purchase Bills** (a deliberate scope decision: today's ad-hoc Purchase VAT Register reads only Expenses, but Purchase Bills already carry their own `taxTotal`/`subTotal` computed from GRN line tax rates via the Inventory/Procurement workflow, and excluding them would understate Input VAT for any company using that workflow). Net Payable VAT is explicitly VAT-on-Sales minus VAT-on-Purchases, never gross Sales minus gross Purchases — confirmed directly with the user mid-build after an ambiguous first plan draft. The invoice-total formula itself was extracted from the existing `POST /invoices` route into a shared `computeInvoiceServerTotals()` (`server/lib/businessLogic.ts`) so both the real invoice route and this new module compute totals identically — a pure refactor, re-verified against `tests/zatcaWorkflow.test.ts`/`tests/invoicePaidRoute.test.ts` afterward with zero behavior change.

### The filed-quarter lock
Once Filed, a quarter is permanently locked (no un-filing route exists at all) and blocks new/edited Invoices (incl. Credit/Debit Notes), Expenses, and Purchase Bills dated inside it — via a new `assertQuarterNotFiled()` guard (`server/lib/businessLogic.ts`, same shape as the existing `validateTransactionDate`), added alongside each document type's existing date-validation call site (plus a brand-new call site in the Purchase Bill route, which previously had no fiscal-period guard of any kind — a pre-existing gap). Quotations/Vouchers/PR/PO/GRN/Stock Takes are explicitly **not** blocked — confirmed decision, since they don't feed VAT at all and blocking them would only frustrate normal operations. **A second, explicitly requested guard**: filing itself is rejected if any Active invoice within that specific quarter still has `zatcaStatus` of `NOT_SUBMITTED`/`SUBMITTING`/`ERROR` — i.e. hasn't actually completed ZATCA e-invoicing submission yet (`DISABLED` is excluded from this check since it's a company-wide "e-invoicing not in use" state, not a per-invoice failure) — preventing a company from self-attesting a VAT figure while some of its invoices are still mid-flight or failed on the e-invoicing side.

### Sequential filing order
Filing a quarter requires every strictly-earlier quarter this company has **any** row for (Generated, Filed, or soft-deleted) to already be Filed — but a company with zero prior rows before its target quarter passes trivially (there's no way to know a true VAT-registration start date, so the earliest quarter this tool ever touches anchors the sequence). This correctly forces "no skipping a quarter once you're tracking earlier ones" — e.g. Q1 and Q3 generated with Q2 never touched, filing Q3 is rejected until Q2 is generated *and* filed — while still letting a company that only starts using this feature mid-year file that first quarter immediately. Modeled on the existing fiscal-month "oldest open month must close first" ordering rule (`server/routes/transactions.ts`'s `POST /months`).

### Permissions and UI
New `taxReturns` permission module (`src/permissionSchema.ts`, `financial_config` group) — flat, non-CRUD shape like `fiscalMonths`: `create`/`read`/`delete` plus a separate `file` leaf (filing is the materially bigger, irreversible authority, same split as `fiscalMonths.open`/`.close`). New **VAT Returns (ZATCA Filing)** tab in Admin Settings' Fiscal & Capital group, backed by a new standalone `src/components/VatReturnsPanel.tsx` (a stateful governance record with Generate/File/Delete actions, not a `ReportViewer.tsx`-style read-only report) — a year/quarter picker, a table of this company's returns reading straight from each row's frozen `figuresSnapshot`, and confirmation-gated Filed/Delete actions with explicit irreversibility wording. All new strings translated (AR/UR) via the same audit-and-apply workflow used throughout this session.

### Reference numbering — deliberate non-integration with the numbering-policy feature
`taxReturns` is **not** wired into `documentNumbering.ts`'s `DOCUMENT_TYPE_REGISTRY`/counter mechanism (item 84) — that system produces monotonic integers (`PREFIX-0001`), the wrong shape for the calendar-derived `Q1-2026` reference, which is computed directly from the row's own `year`/`quarter` columns instead, with uniqueness from the partial unique index rather than a shared sequence.

### Verification
New `tests/taxReturns.test.ts` (16 tests, real Postgres/HTTP, no mocks): figure correctness against hand-computed values (including Credit Note netting and Purchase Bill inclusion, checked against a company with real invoices/expenses/GRN+bill fixtures), generate→delete→regenerate producing a genuinely new row, the partial unique index rejecting a duplicate live generate, permission gating (create+read vs. read-only roles), the full filing lifecycle (ZATCA-completeness + sequential-order checks, permanent lock, the filed-quarter document block proven against Invoice/Credit-Note/Expense/Purchase-Bill while confirming Quotation/PR/PO/Stock-Take remain unaffected), the sequential-filing-order rule on an isolated company with zero underlying data, and cross-company isolation. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full existing suite (27 files, 267 tests) re-run afterward with zero regressions. Verified live in the browser against CNC Woodcraft & Design's real Q3-2026 data (Sales SAR 10978.00, VAT on Sales SAR 1625.70, Purchases SAR 217.39, VAT on Purchases SAR 32.61, Net Payable SAR 1593.09 — arithmetic confirmed correct); the generated test row was deleted afterward rather than filed, since filing would have permanently locked that live demo company's active quarter.

**A real environment issue found and fixed during this work, unrelated to the feature's own logic**: three redundant `tsx watch server.ts` process trees were discovered running simultaneously (accumulated over this long session, each competing for port 3000), which caused a genuinely confusing false-positive test failure — one process's crashed restart (`EADDRINUSE`) silently left an older sibling process serving all requests, undetected because it still responded normally to `curl`/browser checks. All redundant instances were killed and a single clean dev server process is now running.

### Follow-up fix, same day: the ZATCA-completeness guard now also blocks Generate, not just File
Originally `assertNoUnreportedZatcaInvoices` (`server/lib/vatReturn.ts`) only ran inside `POST /:id/file`. The user explicitly asked for it to also block **Generate**: "if there is a zatca invoice not reported... system should block you [at generate]... either delete them or report to zatca then generate return." Verified live against CNC Woodcraft & Design's real Q3-2026 data first — confirmed the gap (Generate succeeded with 5 unreported invoices present; only the later Filing attempt was blocked, and even that attempt was masked by an unrelated sequential-order rejection since the company already had a live, never-filed Q1-2026 draft from the user's own testing). Fixed by extracting the check into a shared function called from both `POST /generate` and `POST /:id/file` — generate now fails fast with the same message ("N invoice(s) dated in this quarter have not completed ZATCA e-invoicing submission... Cancel these invoices (or issue a Credit Note against them) or wait for them to finish reporting to ZATCA, then try again"), and file re-checks independently in case an invoice's status changed in the window between generating a draft and actually filing it. Re-verified live: generating Q3-2026 for CNC Woodcraft & Design now correctly returns 400 naming the first offending invoice.

Added 3 new tests to `tests/taxReturns.test.ts` (19 total) on a **third, fully isolated company** — a real cross-scenario bug was caught while writing them: the sequential-filing-order rule blocks a later quarter whenever the company has *any* earlier tax-return row at all, regardless of how old or unrelated, so the first attempt (reusing `companyId` with a deliberately ancient 2015 quarter meant to be "safely out of the way") instead broke three unrelated already-passing tests by making `companyId`'s own current-quarter filing require a nonexistent Q2 first. Fixed by giving this scenario its own company (`zatcaPendingCompanyId`) with zero other rows, confirming the design intent (no quarter-skipping once a company starts tracking any quarter) is correct even though it initially looked like a test-authoring trap. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite (27 files, 270 tests) re-run with zero regressions.

## 86. Branch Adoption Backfill + Sales-Warehouse Routing (warehouse type, per-branch/company default, stock availability enforcement) (Aug 2026)

Two related gaps surfaced from a single question — "did we apply the fix that once a branch opens, existing documents get transferred to it?" — which turned out to have never been implemented (confirmed via `git log`/worktree inspection: a peer session had been queued for this but produced no code), and a follow-up: "branches can have multiple warehouses, but at sales we don't select a warehouse — where does stock get deducted from?" That second question uncovered a real, more serious pre-existing bug (below), not just a missing feature.

### Part 1 — opt-in backfill for pre-branch documents
`quotations`/`invoices`/`vouchers`/`expenses`/`purchaseRequisitions`/`purchaseOrders`/`purchaseBills`/`warehouses` all have a nullable `branchId` that stays NULL forever unless explicitly backfilled once a company adopts branches (see `quotations.branchId`'s own schema comment) — GRN/Purchase Returns/Physical Stock Takes/`inventoryStocks`/`stockLedgerTransactions` need no direct backfill since they derive branch via `warehouseId`, so backfilling warehouses closes that whole derived chain too. New `POST /api/branches/backfill-unassigned` (`server/routes/branches.ts`) does a single-transaction bulk `UPDATE ... SET branch_id = :chosenBranchId WHERE company_id = :companyId AND branch_id IS NULL` across all 8 tables, returning per-table counts; idempotent by construction (`isNull` — a second run touches nothing) and gated on `branches.update` (edit-adjacent authority, not a new leaf). Frontend: `AdminSettings.tsx`'s Branches tab prompts once, automatically, right when a company creates its very first branch ("this is your first branch — backfill existing unassigned documents onto it now?"), plus a standalone "Backfill Unassigned Documents" panel for running it again later against any branch. Never runs automatically without an explicit admin confirmation — reattributing historical documents to a branch they weren't created under is a real, visible data change.

### Part 2 — the real bug: stock was never deducted from a specific, chosen warehouse
Tracing "where does a sale deduct stock from" led to `deductStockForSale()` (`server/lib/businessLogic.ts`): it deducted from whatever single `productsServices.defaultWarehouseId` was configured **per product, company-wide** — never per-transaction, and with zero connection to which branch/location the sale was actually made at.
