Worse, tracing further revealed `InvoiceModule.tsx`'s manual invoice-creation form's `cleanItems` mapping silently dropped `productId` (and `unit`) before ever reaching the server — meaning a regular (non-POS, non-quotation-converted) invoice **never deducted stock or updated `averageSalePrice` at all**, regardless of `defaultWarehouseId`, since the server's `item.productId` check always failed. POS was unaffected (it already sent `productId` correctly) — this bug was specific to the primary manual Invoice form, silent, and pre-dated this session's branch work entirely. Both fields are now included in the payload.

### The redesign: warehouse becomes an explicit, resolved property of the sale itself, not the product
- `warehouses.type: 'sales' | 'backend'` (default `'sales'`, so every pre-existing warehouse keeps today's behavior). A 'backend' (distribution/storage) warehouse can never be a sale's source — enforced server-side (`assertSalesWarehouse`), not just a UI filter.
- Two-tier default, mirroring `branches.isDefault`'s existing swap pattern: `warehouses.isCompanyDefault` (one company-wide fallback, partial unique index) and `branches.defaultWarehouseId` (per-branch override, must be a `'sales'`-type warehouse, validated in the branch upsert route). A company's very first warehouse auto-becomes its default (same "first X becomes default" convention as the first branch/tax slab/bank) unless it's created as `'backend'`.
- `invoices.warehouseId` (new nullable column, mirrors `branchId`'s additive pattern) — one warehouse per document, not per line (matches `goodsReceiptNotes.warehouseId`'s existing shape; a sale split across two physical warehouses isn't a real scenario here). Resolved server-side in `resolveSaleWarehouse()` (`server/lib/businessLogic.ts`): explicit client choice → the invoice's branch's own default → the company's default → required-and-rejected only if the invoice actually contains a stock (`'item'` type) line — a services-only invoice never needs one, and a company with zero warehouses configured can still issue services-only invoices exactly as before. Credit/Debit Notes inherit `warehouseId` from their original invoice, same as `branchId` (display/reporting only today — a Credit Note still doesn't restock inventory at all, a separate pre-existing gap left untouched).
- `deductStockForSale()` now takes the resolved `warehouseId` explicitly instead of reading `productsServices.defaultWarehouseId` — this is what actually fixes the original branch-blindness bug: the warehouse stock is deducted from is now exactly the one the sale was recorded against.
- New opt-in company setting `inventorySettings.enforceStockAvailability` (in the existing Inventory Module Configuration section of Company Setup, alongside `prOptionality`/`isDsdAllowed`) — when on, a sale is rejected with a clear "Insufficient stock" 400 instead of the default clamp-at-zero behavior, checked against the exact same `inventoryStocks` row `deductStockForSale` itself reads (the unbatched bucket), so the pre-flight check and the actual deduction can never disagree.
- Mandatory-branch-for-new-warehouse (built earlier in this same work): once a company has at least one active branch, a brand-new warehouse must specify one (`POST /warehouses`); a company that has never adopted branches keeps `branchId` fully optional.
- UI: `MasterEntities.tsx`'s Warehouse form gained a Type selector and a "set as company-wide default" checkbox (plus a Type column/badge in the list); `AdminSettings.tsx`'s Branch form gained a "Default Sales Warehouse" dropdown (only 'sales'-type, active warehouses); `InvoiceModule.tsx`'s create form gained an auto-selected, still-editable "Sales Warehouse" dropdown that re-derives whenever the branch selection changes. POS deliberately got **no** new picker — it never exposed branch selection either, so it now fully auto-resolves via the server (explicit warehouse is simply never sent), keeping the till flow exactly as fast as before.

### Verification
New `tests/salesWarehouseRouting.test.ts` (17 tests, real Postgres/HTTP, no mocks): two-tier resolution order (branch default overriding company default), explicit-override, rejection of a `'backend'`-type warehouse (both as an explicit invoice choice and as an attempted branch/company default), Credit Note warehouse inheritance, the "zero warehouses configured" boundary (services-only succeeds with `warehouseId: null`, a stock-item invoice is rejected with a clear message), first-warehouse auto-default, mandatory-branch-for-new-warehouse, and the opt-in stock-availability enforcement (default-off oversell succeeds/clamps, on-oversell correctly rejected pre-deduction). A pre-existing test file, `tests/averageSalePrice.test.ts`, broke as a direct and correct consequence of the new rule (its fixture company had no warehouse at all) and was fixed by adding one — not a regression in the new logic. 3 more tests added for the backfill route itself (attributes only currently-NULL rows, idempotent re-run, rejects a branchId from another company). `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite (28 files, 287 tests) re-run with zero regressions. Verified live in the browser: created a throwaway company/admin, confirmed the Warehouse form's Type/default fields render and persist correctly (first warehouse auto-defaulted), confirmed the Branch form's Default Sales Warehouse selector saves correctly, and confirmed the backfill route itself via a direct authenticated request (1 invoice + 1 warehouse correctly attributed) since the confirmation dialog it sits behind is a native `window.confirm()` the browser-automation tool cannot drive; all throwaway data deleted afterward.

## 87. Packaging / Alternate Units of Measure — "Cell 4 AMP sold individually or as a Carton-12" (Aug/Sep 2026)

Requested directly: an item like "Cell 4 AMP" (base unit: Piece) also needs to be bought/sold as a fixed packaging multiple — "a pack/carton, each having 12 cells" — with its own barcode and its own price (never simply 12× the piece price), on *any* stock-touching document (GRN, PO, Purchase Return, Physical Stock Take, Invoice, POS), with barcode scanning resolving straight to the right unit. Confirmed before building: (1) all six document types need this, not just sales; (2) barcode scanning is required; (3) a packaging unit's price/cost is always independently set, never auto-derived from the base price × factor. Before this work, `unitsOfMeasure` was a flat, unconverted company picklist and **no** stock-mutating item table (`purchaseOrderItems`/`goodsReceiptNoteItems`/`purchaseReturnItems`/`physicalStockTakeItems`) had a per-line unit column at all — confirmed via a full read of `server/routes/inventory.ts` and `src/db/schema.ts`.

### The model: one product, alternate units — not a second catalog row
Modeled as SAP/Odoo/NetSuite do case-pack conversions: one product master with a base/stocking unit (`productsServices.unit`, unchanged) plus any number of packaging units in a new `productUnitConversions` table (`productId`, `unitOfMeasureId`, `conversionFactor`, its own `barcode`/`sku`/`purchasePrice`/`salePrice`, `isActive`, partial-unique per `(productId, unitOfMeasureId)` while active) — deliberately **not** a second full product/kit row, since this is one physical item packaged differently, not a bundle of distinct products. `unitOfMeasureId` (nullable — null = base unit) was added to all six item tables plus `invoiceItems`/`quotationItems`. Inventory (`inventoryStocks`/`stockLedgerTransactions`) and `averageCost`/`averageSalePrice` always stay in the product's own base unit; a document's own stored `quantity`/`unitCost` is always exactly what was entered in whichever unit was picked — conversion happens once, server-side, only at the point something touches inventory or a weighted average (new `server/lib/uomConversion.ts`: `getConversionFactor`/`toBaseQuantity`/`toBaseUnitCost`, plus `loadZatcaCodesByUnitId` so a packaging unit's own ZATCA code, not the base product's, reaches the e-invoice XML when it's the one actually sold).

**A real, related mid-build correction**: the plan initially added a `unitsOfMeasure.zatcaCode` column, reasoning `unitsOfMeasure.code` was an arbitrary business label ("Pcs") disconnected from ZATCA's UN/ECE codes. Reading `POST /units-of-measure` proved this wrong — `code` is *already* validated via `isValidZatcaUnitCode` at creation time, so it already **is** a real ZATCA code. The new column was redundant and was dropped again (`npm run db:push --force`, confirmed empty/safe to drop) before it shipped; `loadZatcaCodesByUnitId` reads `unitsOfMeasure.code` directly instead.

### Every existing stock-mutating call site got one inserted conversion step
GRN create + reversal, Purchase Return create + cancel (including the "remaining-returnable-from-this-GRN" check, now compared in base-unit terms since a receipt and its return need not share a unit), Physical Stock Take create + finalize (a count can itself be entered in a packaging unit — "3 full cartons on the shelf" — `physicalQuantity` is stored exactly as counted for display, `variance`/the actual stock write are base-unit-converted), PO↔GRN fulfillment-status comparison (ordered-in-Cartons vs. received-in-Pieces now compares correctly), and the Invoice/POS sale path (`deductStockForSale` plus the `averageSalePrice` fold in both the direct-invoice and quotation-convert routes in `server/routes/transactions.ts`).

**Two more pre-existing bugs found and fixed while wiring the sales side**, same class as the drizzle/pg error-code issue below: `InvoiceModule.tsx`'s and `QuotationModule.tsx`'s `cleanItems` mapping silently dropped `productId`/`unit`/(quotation's `taxSlabId`) before ever reaching the server — meaning a catalog-linked line on a manually-created Invoice or Quotation never actually deducted stock or folded into `averageSalePrice`/carried its ZATCA unit, regardless of any of this feature's new logic, because the server's own `item.productId` branch never ran. Both fixed alongside adding `unitOfMeasureId` to the same payload.

### Barcode resolution — client-side, not a server round-trip
Rather than add a server endpoint nothing in the app would call, barcode/SKU resolution against `db.productUnitConversions` (packaging unit's own code first) then `db.products` (base product) is done entirely client-side, since the full catalog is already loaded in state: `ItemCatalogSearch.tsx` gained exact barcode/SKU matching (previously name-substring only) plus a resolved-unit return shape, consumed by Invoice/Quotation's flattened catalog rows (one row per product + one extra row per active packaging unit); `PosModule.tsx`'s existing barcode-matching grid got the same flattening; the four `InventoryModule.tsx` forms (GRN/PO/Purchase Return/Stock Take), which had **no** search/scan capability at all before this, each gained a barcode-input that resolves via a small shared client-side helper plus a Unit `<select>` next to quantity.

### New UI
`MasterEntities.tsx`'s product edit form gained an "Alternate / Packaging Units" panel (add/deactivate, mirrors the existing Warehouse Extension panel's own list+add shape) backed by new `GET/POST /api/product-unit-conversions` + `PATCH /:id/toggle-active` routes (`server/routes/masterEntities.ts`, gated on the existing `products.create`/`.update` leaves — product-master data, no new permission leaf).

**A real state-merge bug found live during UI verification**: after saving a new packaging unit through the form, it never appeared in the list — `App.tsx`'s `/api/state` response merge has an explicit per-key whitelist (the same shape of gap this project has hit before with roles/tax slabs), and `productUnitConversions` was missing from it. `src/db/apiState.ts` was already correctly including it in the server response; the client was just discarding it on merge. Fixed by adding the one line to `App.tsx`, in both places the merge happens; re-verified live afterward — the packaging unit appeared correctly.

**Another pre-existing bug found while writing this feature's tests**: the friendly-409/400 unique-constraint-violation pattern already used in `roles.ts` (`if (error.code === '23505')`) turned out to be dead code — this project's drizzle-orm version wraps the real pg error under `error.cause.code`, not `error.code` directly (confirmed live against a real unique-violation: `error.code` is `undefined`, `error.cause.code` is `'23505'`). Fixed in the new `product-unit-conversions` route (`error.code === '23505' || error.cause?.code === '23505'`); the identical pre-existing bug in `roles.ts` was left as a flagged follow-up rather than fixed inline (out of scope for this feature).

### Verification
New `tests/uomConversion.test.ts` (11 tests, real Postgres/HTTP, no mocks): conversion CRUD + validation (positive-factor requirement, duplicate-active rejection, the drizzle/pg error-code fix), a GRN receipt of 2 Cartons correctly multiplying `inventoryStocks` by 24 and folding `averageCost` at 100/12 SAR/unit (never 100 SAR/unit), GRN reversal undoing the same conversion, a PO ordered in base units fully satisfied by a GRN received in Cartons, Purchase Return's remaining-returnable check enforced correctly across mixed units, a Stock Take counted in Cartons finalizing to the correct base-unit stock level, an Invoice sale of 1 Carton deducting 12 base units while the invoice itself still bills exactly "1" at its own packaging price, and a deactivated packaging unit correctly blocking further use. `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. Two pre-existing test files broke as a **direct and correct** consequence of this feature's warehouse/stock rules interacting with clock timing, not regressions in the new logic: `tests/posSaleZatcaPipeline.test.ts` needed a warehouse fixture (same class of gap as `tests/averageSalePrice.test.ts` earlier in this session) and separately had a latent flake — deriving its fiscal-month id from local time (`getFullYear()`/`getMonth()`) while deriving the invoice's own date from UTC (`toISOString()`), which silently disagree for a few hours around local midnight in any timezone ahead of UTC (this session happened to cross exactly that boundary, in AST/UTC+3) — fixed by deriving both from UTC consistently. Full suite (29 files, 298 tests) re-run with zero regressions. Verified live in the browser end-to-end: added a "Carton-12" packaging unit with its own barcode/price to a real product through the new UI panel, confirmed it persisted and displayed correctly after the `App.tsx` merge fix; all throwaway data deleted afterward.

**A genuine environment incident, unrelated to this feature's logic**: partway through browser verification, the dev server's `tsx watch` process had silently died (its child node process gone, `net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` on every request) — same class of incident as BACKLOG item 85's redundant-processes finding, this time a single crashed instance rather than duplicates. Diagnosed via `netstat`/`Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`, killed the stale wrapper, started one fresh instance, confirmed a single clean process tree before continuing.

## 88. "Walk-in Customer" wrongly typed B2B — silently blocked ZATCA submission (Sep 2026)

Found by the user directly against real ZATCA sandbox data: CNC Woodcraft & Design's INV-1048 sat at `zatcaStatus: NOT_SUBMITTED` with a `BUYER_INCOMPLETE` validation error demanding a 15-digit VAT number and full street/building/district/city/postal fields — for its "Walk-in Customer." A walk-in/anonymous retail buyer is a B2C case by definition (name-only, no business registration), so it should never be asked for a B2B identity at all.

**Two distinct causes, evaluated separately before either was touched (per explicit instruction to discuss before fixing):**

1. **Stale data on exactly two companies, not a live seeding bug.** The current new-company provisioning code (`AdminSettings.tsx`'s starter-resources block, ~L906) already creates the auto-seeded "Walk-in Customer" as `buyerType: 'B2C'` — confirmed correct today. A system-wide query (`isSystem = true AND buyerType = 'B2B' AND vatNumber IS NULL`) found only two customers with the drift: CNC Woodcraft & Design's "Walk-in Customer" and Naqsh Co-CNC's "Walk-in Customer (Laser)" — both old seeded demo companies predating this correct logic. Fixed with a direct, targeted `UPDATE customers SET buyer_type = 'B2C'` on exactly those two rows (not a blanket rule — nothing else matched the query).
2. **A separate, still-live footgun**, independent of the stale data above: the *manual* "New Customer" form (`MasterEntities.tsx`, ~L95/184) defaulted `buyerType` to `'B2B'` for every brand-new customer, regardless of intent. Anyone hand-adding a genuinely walk-in/retail customer through the real UI without noticing the toggle would hit the identical trap — B2B with no VAT/address, silently blocking any invoice against them at submission time. Fixed by changing the default to `'B2C'`: the safer failure mode either way — a wrongly-B2C business customer still submits fine as Simplified, while the old wrongly-B2B default's failure is a confusing, silent block that only surfaces once someone tries to invoice them.

**Blast radius checked before fixing anything further**: five invoices referenced the two affected customers. Three on CNC Woodcraft (INV-1007/1008/1009) and one on Naqsh Co (INV-1023), all dated 2026-06-01, sat at `zatcaStatus: DISABLED` — neither company had ZATCA enabled yet at that date, so this bug never actually affected them and they were deliberately left untouched (re-processing them now would mean backdating a first-ever ZATCA submission for 3-month-old invoices, a materially bigger and separate decision). Only INV-1048 (2026-08-31) was genuinely blocked by this bug on a company with ZATCA currently active — the user re-ran its ZATCA processing manually after the customer fix landed; it correctly reclassified as `invoiceTypeCode: 0200000` (Simplified) and reached `zatcaStatus: REPORTED` with zero errors.

No schema or route change — this was a two-line UI default fix plus a two-row data correction, verified directly against the real invoice it was blocking rather than via a new automated test (the underlying `validateBuyerFields` B2B/B2C branching logic itself already has coverage in `tests/zatcaWorkflow.test.ts`; this was a data/default-value bug, not a validation-logic bug).

## 89. HR Foundation — Employees, Job Titles, Employee Numbering, and a Sales Associate field on Invoices (Sep 2026)

Requested as a small, concrete need — an optional "Sales Associate" field on the Sales Invoice, since some companies pay staff a bonus on their invoiced sales — but deliberately built on a real HR foundation instead of a flat picklist of names, so a later Timekeeping/Attendance module has a real `employees` entity to anchor against instead of overloading `users` (login accounts) with HR meaning they don't have. Confirmed before building: this app had zero HR concept anywhere (no `employee`/`staffNumber`/`jobTitle` table or column).

**First plan was rejected and revised.** The initial version put Sales Associate behind a flat, company-wide employee list. Caught directly: *"if user has access to single branch — only branch related staff is shown... if a company has 30 branches, it will be difficult for Cashier to search from a long list... employeeNumber+name should be in the list as names can be same."* Revised to add branch-scoped visibility (with an explicit Head-Office/company-wide option employees can be assigned to) and the `"{employeeNumber} — {name}"` display convention everywhere an employee is listed, before implementation began.

### Design
- **`employees` is independent of `users`, one-directional FK only** (`users.employeeId → employees.id`; `employees` never references `users`) — not every employee needs ERP access (a shop-floor worker onboarded for payroll may never log in), and not every login predates this feature. A future Timekeeping module can key clock-in/out records on `employeeId` regardless of login status.
- **New `jobTitles` table**, deliberately distinct from the existing `roles` table (RBAC permission bundles) both in schema and naming — a Job Title (e.g. "Sales Associate") is who someone is for HR/business purposes; a Role is what an ERP login account is allowed to click. `isSalesRole` is a flag on the *title*, not a hardcoded string match against "Sales Associate," so a company phrasing it differently ("Sales Rep") still works.
- **`employees.branchId` is nullable = Head Office (company-wide)**, mapped explicitly in the UI ("Head Office (Company-wide)" as the first, real dropdown option — not an implicit blank default), same nullable/additive convention as `quotations.branchId`/`warehouses.branchId`. Moving someone from HO to a branch later is a plain edit, no separate transfer workflow in v1.
- **Employee numbering reuses `documentCounters`' exact atomic-upsert mechanism**, not a parallel counter. `getAndIncrementDocumentNumber`'s reservation step was extracted into a shared `reserveNextCounterValue(tx, companyId, docType, periodKey, seedValue)` in `server/lib/documentNumbering.ts` (zero behavior change, re-verified against `tests/documentNumbering.test.ts`); new `server/lib/employeeNumbering.ts` calls it with `docType: 'employee'`, `periodKey: 'NONE'` (never resets by period) and **no** prefix/separator — just zero-padded digits (4 or 5 wide, via `companies.hrSettings.employeeNumberPadWidth`) — so "employee number, digits only" holds by construction, never free-typed. An optional `employeeNumberStart` seeds the sequence for a company migrating off another HRIS.
- **Mandatory-once-adopted, not mandatory-always**: `POST /users` requires `employeeId` for a genuinely new account only once the company has onboarded at least one active employee — the exact same pattern already used for warehouses requiring a branch once a company has branches. A company with zero employees keeps the old, fully-optional behavior.
- **`invoices.salesAssociateId`** is a pure attribution field — validated (belongs to company, active) when set, immutable after creation like `branchId`, never affects totals/tax/ZATCA XML. The Invoice form's picker is filtered to `isActive && jobTitle.isSalesRole && (employee.branchId === null || employee.branchId === formBranchId)`, re-derived via a `useEffect` keyed on `formBranchId` — the same reactivity pattern `formWarehouseId` already uses — so switching the invoice's branch live-narrows the list, and a stale selection that's no longer eligible is cleared rather than silently submitted.

### New/changed files
`src/db/schema.ts` (`jobTitles`, `employees`, `users.employeeId`, `invoices.salesAssociateId`, `companies.hrSettings`), `server/lib/employeeNumbering.ts` (new), `server/routes/employees.ts` (new), `server/routes/masterEntities.ts` (job-titles CRUD), `server/routes/users.ts` and `server/routes/transactions.ts` (validation described above), `src/permissionSchema.ts` (`jobTitles`, `employees` modules under `governance`), `src/components/EmployeesModule.tsx` (new — Job Titles panel + Employee onboarding panel, mirrors the existing Units-of-Measure list+form shape), `src/App.tsx` (new "Human Resources" nav group, its own group rather than folded into Master Registries since Timekeeping will grow into it later), `src/components/AdminSettings.tsx` (User form's Linked Employee picker), `src/components/InvoiceModule.tsx` (Sales Associate selector).

**A residual bug from an earlier feature this same session was found and fixed while extending the same code**: `App.tsx` merges `/api/state` into local state in two separate places (initial load, and the refresh path after most create/update actions), each requiring its own explicit per-key whitelist entry. The Packaging/Units feature (item 87) had only patched one of the two locations for `productUnitConversions` — an exact-string `replace_all` silently missed the second spot due to differing indentation. Caught and fixed both while adding `jobTitles`/`employees` to the same two blocks.

### Verification
New `tests/employees.test.ts` (18 tests, real Postgres/HTTP, no mocks): Job Title CRUD including the duplicate-active-title rejection and toggle-active; employee onboarding mints the correct zero-padded number (4-digit default, reformats live to 5-digit on a `padWidth` change without restarting the sequence), a fresh company's sequence correctly seeds from `employeeNumberStart`, a duplicate employee number is rejected at the DB level (unique index), onboarding to Head Office when no branch is given, rejection against a deactivated job title; `POST /users` allows no `employeeId` on a zero-employee company, rejects a new account with no `employeeId` once the company has an active employee, rejects linking a deactivated employee, accepts a valid one; an invoice's `salesAssociateId` persists correctly and is rejected for a wrong-company or deactivated employee. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Two test-fixture bugs found and fixed while building this suite (not application bugs):** (1) three companies created back-to-back in `beforeAll` using `userId.slice(0, 8)` for usernames collided, because a UUIDv7's leading hex characters are time-based and identical for IDs minted in the same millisecond — since `username` has no DB-level uniqueness constraint, `login()` silently matched an arbitrary row, aliasing every session onto the wrong company and producing confusing false failures ("empty" company reporting an employee that didn't belong to it). Fixed by using the full id, not a slice. (2) the `afterAll` cleanup originally deleted `employees` before `users`, violating the new `users.employeeId` FK and aborting cleanup for every company still left in the loop — reordered to delete `users` first.

**A pre-existing, unrelated gap found while running the full suite for regression-checking**: `vitest run` (no path filter) was also picking up every `*.test.ts` file checked out into a Claude Code worktree (`.claude/worktrees/<name>/tests/...`) — a live, real copy of the same suite running concurrently against the *same* shared dev server/Postgres database. Two copies of `zatcaWorkflow.test.ts` racing the same real hash-chain state produced dozens of nondeterministic ICV/status mismatches with nothing to do with any actual regression (confirmed by running the same files with the worktrees excluded, and separately in isolation). Fixed by adding a `test.exclude` in `vite.config.ts` for `.claude/worktrees/**`. After that fix: full suite green — 30 files, 316 tests, zero regressions.

Verified live in the browser end-to-end against a throwaway company: Human Resources nav group renders; created a sales-role "Sales Associate" and a non-sales "Cashier" job title; onboarded "0001 — Alice Sales" to a branch and "0002 — Bob Cashier" to Head Office, both displaying in the required `{number} — {name}` format; the User form's Linked Employee picker correctly lists both in that format; the Invoice form's Sales Associate selector correctly showed only Alice when her branch was selected, correctly excluded Bob (non-sales job title) throughout, and correctly disappeared entirely (rather than showing a stale/invalid option) when switching to a branch with no eligible associate — proving the branch-scoped live re-filtering behaves as designed. All throwaway data deleted afterward.

## 90. Two Claimed-Fixed Bugs Were Still Broken — `products`/`units-of-measure` Id Generation and `roles.ts`'s Unique-Violation Catch (Sep 2026)

A peer/background session had reported both of these as fixed earlier in the session ("just got message it is fixed"). Re-verified directly against the actual files before trusting that — both were still broken, unchanged from their original state. Read this as a standing reminder: a memory or another session's claim that something is fixed is not the same as it being fixed; check the file.

**`POST /products` and `POST /units-of-measure`** (`server/routes/masterEntities.ts`) never generated `data.id` server-side when the caller omitted it, and never returned the created `id` in the response — unlike every sibling lookup-table route (warehouses, branches, job-titles, product-unit-conversions), which all do `else { data.id = generateId(); }` and `res.json({ success: true, id: data.id })`. Invisible in the real UI (the React client always generates a UUID before calling the API), but a direct API call with no `id` would 500 on a Postgres NOT NULL violation, and even a supplied `id` was never echoed back for a caller that needed it. Fixed to match the established pattern exactly; verified live against the running server (both routes now correctly mint and return an id).

**`roles.ts`'s duplicate-role-name 409 handler** checked only `error.code === '23505'`, never `error.cause?.code === '23505'` — the exact same drizzle/pg wrapping gotcha documented and fixed in the product-unit-conversions route back in item 87 (this drizzle-orm version wraps the real Postgres error under `.cause.code`, not `.code` directly). The check was dead code: a real duplicate-name insert fell through to the generic 500 handler instead of the intended friendly 409. Fixed with the same `error.code === '23505' || error.cause?.code === '23505'` check; verified live (first role creates 200, duplicate name now correctly 409s with the friendly message instead of 500).

## 91. Test-Database Cleanup — 233 Leftover Test Companies Purged, One Real Cross-Company Data Bug Found Along the Way (Sep 2026)

A direct audit (every `company_id` foreign key checked against `information_schema`, every company-scoped table LEFT JOINed against `companies` for orphans) confirmed referential integrity was already sound — zero orphaned rows anywhere — but surfaced that the database held 235 companies, only 2 of them real (CNC Woodcraft & Design, Naqsh Co- CNC). The other 233 were accumulated test-run fixtures — normally each test file's own `afterAll` tears its throwaway company down, but a long session's worth of interrupted runs, an earlier username-collision bug (see item 89's own test-fixture section) causing `afterAll` to abort mid-cleanup, and manual debug scripts left hundreds of orphaned test companies behind.

Purged all 233 in one transaction, in dependency order (line items → `password_reset_tokens`/`user_roles`/`user_branches` → non-document company-scoped tables → documents in their real FK order — `purchase_returns` before `goods_receipt_notes` before `purchase_orders` before `purchase_requisitions`; `invoices` before `quotations`; everything before `pos_shifts` — → `users` before `employees` → master/lookup data → `companies`), confirmed via the same orphan-scan afterward: 2 companies remain, zero orphans anywhere. (128 `audit_logs` rows with a `NULL company_id` were flagged by the scan and investigated separately — all 128 are `LOGIN_FAILED` entries, which legitimately can't be attributed to a company since auth never succeeded; left alone, not a bug.)

**A real, pre-existing bug found while auditing for cross-company references before deleting anything** (the right discipline before any bulk delete: confirm nothing live depends on what's about to disappear): two genuine invoices on CNC Woodcraft & Design (INV-1008, INV-1009 — the same two flagged in item 88's blast-radius check) had both their header `tax_slab_id` and three `invoice_items.tax_slab_id` rows pointing at tax-rate rows actually owned by a test company ("UX Test") instead of CNC Woodcraft's own identical-rate slabs (0% and 15%). Deleting "UX Test" without fixing this first would have left two real invoices with a dangling tax reference. Repointed all four references to CNC Woodcraft's own matching-rate rows (same 0%/15% rates — a pure ownership correction, no amount or rate changed) before proceeding with the purge.

## 92. Invoice Font System Extended (Helvetica/Calibri) + a "Tier-1" Template for CNC Woodcraft, Seller/Buyer Card Parity (Sep 2026)

Requested after dissatisfaction with the default invoice font: research into what's actually globally standard for invoices (not just this app's existing options) pointed at Helvetica/Arial as the universal, can't-go-wrong choice — nearly every major invoicing platform (QuickBooks, Xero, FreshBooks, Stripe Invoicing) defaults to a clean sans-serif, and Helvetica/Arial specifically are the two names virtually every Word/Excel/PDF-generated invoice on earth already uses.

**Added as system fonts, not webfonts** (`src/index.css`'s `@theme` block: `--font-helvetica`, `--font-calibri`) — deliberately not another self-hosted `@fontsource` import like Lora/Cairo. Helvetica ships on macOS and Calibri ships on Windows; each platform's stack falls back to the other's metric-compatible cousin (Windows has no Helvetica → falls back to Arial, drawn metric-compatible for exactly this reason; macOS has no Calibri → falls back through Candara/Segoe). Zero network dependency, so neither carries the "could still be mid-download at print time" risk the print-ready-documents skill warns about for the app's other, self-hosted fonts. Wired into both font-selection points in `DocumentRenderer.tsx` (`getGlobalFontClass()` for the whole-template choice, and the per-block `fontFamily` prop) and both matching dropdowns in `AdminSettings.tsx`'s Canvas Designer (global template font, per-block font) — the existing RTL guard (Arabic/Urdu documents always render in Cairo regardless of Latin font choice) already applies generically to any Latin font, so it covers the two new ones with no separate change needed.

**A real structural asymmetry found and fixed while reviewing the invoice layout for a "Tier-1" professional look**: `customer_info` has always supported a bordered-card treatment (`borderStyle: 'solid'|'dashed'`, its own "Customer / Buyer" header row), but `company_details` — the seller side, sitting in the exact same row for comparison — had no such option at all; it could only ever render as plain, uncarded text, so a template pairing them side-by-side was structurally one-sided by construction. Added the same `borderStyle` support to `company_details` in `DocumentRenderer.tsx` (undefined preserves the exact old plain-text rendering for every existing template — zero visual change unless explicitly opted into), plus the matching "Box Frame Style" selector in the Canvas Designer's Seller block properties panel. Any template can now give seller and buyer genuine visual parity, not just Tier-1.

**New "Tier-1" template created for CNC Woodcraft & Design** (`document_templates`, inactive by default — previewable and activatable from the Designer whenever they're ready, doesn't touch their live invoice output until then): `globalFontFamily: 'helvetica'` for the document body, `fontFamily: 'calibri'` on the `doc_details`/`totals_summary` blocks for a distinct header/totals pairing, `company_details.borderStyle: 'solid'` matching `customer_info`'s existing card for seller/buyer parity, and `accentColor: 'slate'` on the grand total (more formal/executive than the default indigo). Verified live in the browser via the Designer's Preview (the same `DocumentRenderer` render path the real print/PDF output uses, per this app's one-render-path rule) against a throwaway viewer account scoped to CNC Woodcraft's real company id — confirmed the Helvetica body font, the matched Seller/Customer cards, and the slate grand total all render correctly with zero console errors; throwaway account deleted afterward.

**A pre-existing, unrelated flakiness found while re-running the full suite for regression-checking**: 29 of this project's 30 test files generate throwaway usernames via `someId.slice(0, 8)` (a UUIDv7's leading hex characters are time-based, not random) — since `username` has no DB-level uniqueness constraint and vitest runs test files in parallel by default, two files' fixtures created in the same millisecond can produce colliding usernames, silently aliasing one file's login onto a different file's company (the exact bug already found and fixed in this session's own `tests/employees.test.ts`, item 89). Confirmed nondeterministic, not a real regression: the same failing tests pass 100% reliably in isolation, and the specific set of files that fail changes between consecutive full-suite runs. Flagged as a separate follow-up task (mechanical fix: drop the `.slice(0, 8)` truncation across the remaining 28 files) rather than fixed inline here, since it's unrelated to this item's actual change and touches most of the test suite.

### Follow-up (same day): real design pass on the invoice structure itself, plus the actual ZATCA-field/compactness fix

The Tier-1 font/card work above was a first pass; a second round pushed on the actual invoice *structure*, informed by real ZATCA field requirements rather than assumption. Verified directly against `server/lib/zatca/validators.ts`: `validateBuyerFields()` requires VAT (15 digits, first **and** last digit `3` — `^3\d{13}3$`, a stricter rule than "starts with 3," confirmed against a real sandbox rejection per BR-KSA-44) plus street/building/district/city/postal **only for B2B** buyers (B2C needs none of it); `validateTaxpayerIdentity()` requires the same set unconditionally for the seller, plus CR number. Also confirmed directly in code: Arabic on the printed invoice is **not** a ZATCA legal requirement anywhere in this codebase or its docs — `isBilingual` is this app's own toggleable convention, not a compliance mandate.

The first design draft (mocked externally, not yet in the codebase) itemized every address field into a labeled grid per party. Turned out to be reinventing a mistake already made and reverted here: `customer_info`'s own code comment (`DocumentRenderer.tsx`) documents that an itemized labeled grid was tried before and was "the single biggest contributor to the header spilling the items table onto page 2" — it was already collapsed to one flowing, comma-joined address line specifically to fix that. Corrected the plan to match the existing pattern instead of rebuilding it: no itemized grid, address/VAT/CR/phone stays exactly as compact as `customer_info` already makes it.

**The one real, small gap found**: `company_details` (seller) had no equivalent to `customer_info`'s compact combined "VAT Reg: X | CR: Y" single line — it always rendered VAT and CR as two separate lines even in `compact` mode, making the seller card taller than the buyer card sitting right next to it for no reason. Added the matching compact-combined-line branch to `company_details`, mirroring `customer_info`'s exact existing pattern (including correctly omitting the ` | ` separator when only one of VAT/CR is present). Branch-level seller address/phone override (`branchAddressLine`/`docBranch?.phone`) already existed and needed no change. Tier-1's own template updated to set `compact: true` on both `company_details` and `customer_info`.

Verified live via the Designer's Preview (same `DocumentRenderer` render path as the real print/PDF output): the full Tier-1 invoice — header through footer — now renders comfortably within one page's height with room to spare, seller/buyer cards are visibly matched in height, and the seller card correctly shows VAT alone (no dangling separator) when no CR is on file. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite re-run shows the same pre-existing cross-file test flakiness described above (already flagged as a separate follow-up task), no new regressions from this change.

**Caught immediately by the user against a real invoice (INV-1048) rendered through Tier-1**: the fix above only wired props onto data that already had layout code to consume them — it never actually closed the visible gap between the mockup's design language and the real template. Two real, structural gaps remained: (1) `doc_details` was never given `compact: true`, so Invoice Number/Date/Status/Payment Status still rendered as five separate lines instead of the compact template's own existing two-line format (`compact` mode already existed in `DocumentRenderer.tsx` — it just wasn't turned on for Tier-1); (2) the seller name/VAT/CR and the "INVOICE" title were still hardcoded to `theme.primaryText`/`theme.accentText` — the company's portal-wide UI theme color — with no way for a print template to set its own independent brand accent, so Tier-1's intended consistent copper/amber identity never actually appeared; it just showed whatever color CNC Woodcraft's UI theme happened to be.

Fixed for real this time: extracted the existing `totals_summary` accent-color mapping (indigo/emerald/slate/amber) into a shared `resolveAccentTextClass()` helper and wired it into both `doc_details`' title and `company_details`' name/VAT/CR as a new optional `accentColor` block prop — falling back to the exact previous theme-driven colors when unset, so every existing template is visually unchanged. Added the matching "Accent Highlights" selector to both blocks' panels in the Canvas Designer (mirroring `totals_summary`'s existing one). Set Tier-1's `doc_details`/`company_details`/`totals_summary` all to `accentColor: 'amber'` (one consistent brand color, replacing the earlier ad-hoc 'slate' pick on just the totals block) and `doc_details.compact: true`, plus tightened the template's own `gridGapY` from 'normal' to 'compact' to close the large header-to-cards gap. Re-verified against the exact same real invoice (INV-1048) the user's screenshot showed: compact two-line header, consistent amber accent on the title/seller identity/grand total, and the header now sits close to the seller/buyer cards instead of leaving a large empty band between them.

### Follow-up: notes/QR/totals anchored to the page bottom (preview-only), and a real print bug found to be pre-existing

Separately, the user asked for the printed page to genuinely look like a full A4 sheet, with the notes/QR/totals row pinned to the bottom rather than sitting directly under a short items table. The existing `#printable-inner` wrapper already had `flex flex-col h-full justify-between`, apparently intended for exactly this — but it only ever had **one** direct child (the entire block grid), so `justify-between` had nothing to distribute space against and was fully inert.

Fixed by extracting the giant per-block render switch (previously an inline arrow function passed straight to `.map()`) into a named `renderBlock` function, then splitting `activeBlocks` into two groups — `footerBlockIds = ['notes', 'qr_code', 'totals_summary', 'custom_footer']` vs. everything else — rendered as two separate grids, both siblings under the flex wrapper. Opt-in only, via a new `anchorBottom: true` prop on any footer-group block (added a matching checkbox in the Canvas Designer's Totals panel) — every pre-existing template with no `anchorBottom` set renders through the exact same single-grid path as before, byte for byte.

The height needed for `justify-between` to have room to push against is **preview-only**: `min-h-[11in] print:min-h-0` — the `print:` variant unconditionally zeroes it back out the moment the same DOM is cloned into the real print popup (`handlePrint()` clones `outerHTML` verbatim), so this can never reintroduce the exact forced-min-height phantom-blank-page bug this file has already fixed once (documented further up this same section). Verified via direct DOM measurement against the real Tier-1 preview (JS `getBoundingClientRect()`, not just a screenshot): the wrapper is exactly `1056px` (`11in` at 96dpi) tall, the main content grid ends at `y=577`, and the footer grid runs `y=968`–`1218`, flush against the bottom — confirmed working correctly.

**A real, pre-existing print bug surfaced independently while investigating this**: the user reports every printed invoice — regardless of template or how short the content is — produces 2 pages with the second one blank, when using Chrome's "Save as PDF." This browser sandbox blocks the print popup entirely (`window.open` is refused, confirmed via console: `"Please allow popups..."` fires every time `handlePrint()` runs), so it could not be reproduced or root-caused from this environment. Static review found no forced minimum height anywhere in the current print path (already fixed once, still absent) and no `page-break-*` rules anywhere in `src/`; the one structural oddity worth checking is that both this file's and `index.css`'s `@page` rules are nested inside `@media print` rather than declared at the stylesheet's top level. A minimal standalone repro file (matching the exact popup HTML/CSS structure, same nested-`@page` pattern, same short 2-line-item content) was generated and handed to the user to test directly on their own machine, since that's the only way to isolate whether the bug lives in this shared CSS pattern or somewhere specific to the live popup — not yet resolved as of this entry.

**Immediate correction**: the user clarified the actual complaint was that the footer still isn't anchored to the bottom in the *real* printed output — not the still-open blank-page bug above. The preview-only scoping (`print:min-h-0`) was intentionally conservative, but too conservative: it canceled the anchoring entirely at print time instead of using a *correct* print-time height. Fixed by replacing `print:min-h-0` with `print:min-h-[10.89in]` (A4: `11.69in` raw paper minus both `0.4in` `@page` margins) — the real printable area, not the raw paper height that caused the original bug — with a matching `5.8in` (`6in - 2*0.1in`) for the thermal receipt size. Confirmed the new Tailwind utility actually compiled (present in the live `@layer utilities` CSS, not silently dropped) via direct inspection of `document.styleSheets`; could not verify the real print/PDF pagination itself (this sandbox blocks the print popup entirely, as established above).

### The actual root cause: `handlePrint()` was silently printing the wrong element

The user reported that this correction still didn't show up in a real print. Digging in (prompted directly — "dig it") surfaced a genuinely serious, previously-undiscovered bug, unrelated to page height entirely: **`id="printable-document-content"` is not unique on the page.** The Invoice View screen mounts its own background `DocumentRenderer` instance (rendering the company's *active* template) at the same time this component's own "Document Print Engine" preview modal is open on top of it (rendering whatever template the user actually picked in the modal's dropdown) — two real DOM elements sharing the same id simultaneously. `handlePrint()` located its content via `document.getElementById('printable-document-content')`, which always returns the *first* matching element in document order — confirmed live to be the hidden background instance, not the modal's own preview. **The template selected in the print modal's dropdown was never what actually got printed; the background instance's own active template silently won every time**, regardless of any customization made to whatever template a user picked to preview and print.

Verified directly: with Tier-1 selected in the modal, `document.getElementById('printable-document-content')` returned an instance whose inner classes were `font-sans` (the *other*, non-Tier-1 template) — while the visibly-displayed modal preview's own instance (the second DOM match) correctly showed `font-helvetica`. Fixed properly — not by trying to de-duplicate the id (both instances legitimately need to coexist), but by giving `DocumentRenderer` a `React.useRef` (`printableRef`) attached directly to its own `#printable-document-content` div, and having `handlePrint()` read `printableRef.current` instead of doing a global id lookup. A ref is scoped to this exact component instance and cannot accidentally resolve to a different mounted copy elsewhere on the page, however many share the same (still-present, cosmetic-only-now) id. Confirmed no other code anywhere in `src/` did a `getElementById` lookup on either `printable-document-content` or `printable-inner`, so nothing else needed the same fix.

**Verified without needing a real print popup**: patched `window.open` to return a mock object capturing whatever HTML `handlePrint()` would have written to it, then triggered a real click on the modal's Print button with Tier-1 selected. The captured HTML correctly contained `font-helvetica`, the `10.89in` anchor height, and the seller card's border classes — everything that had never actually reached a real print/PDF before this fix, for any template, from the day the "Document Print Engine" modal was built. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite re-run shows only the already-flagged, unrelated cross-file test flakiness (7 failures this run, same nondeterministic set as before — the background fix for it is a separate, already-running task).

**One more real correction after this actually reached print for the first time**: with the wrong-element bug fixed, the anchor height was for the first time actually applied to a real printed page — and the user immediately caught the last line (`custom_footer`'s thank-you text) spilling onto a genuine second page, even with clear space to spare on page one. The `10.89in` value used was the exact theoretical printable-area maximum with zero safety margin — precisely the kind of "forced height calculated too precisely" mistake this same file has already been bitten by once (documented above). Reduced to `10.5in` (A4) / `5.5in` (thermal), deliberately undershooting the theoretical maximum by roughly a third of an inch rather than hugging the exact edge. Verified live (bypassing the print-popup block again the same way) that the actually-rendered element carries `print:min-h-[10.5in]`, not the old value.

## 93. Left-Nav Navigation Never Actually Refetched From The Server — Stale Listings App-Wide Until a Hard Reload (Sep 2026)

**Reported directly by the user**: created an invoice, clicked "Sales Invoices" in the left nav, and it wasn't there — only appeared after a full browser refresh (F5). Immediately generalized correctly: "this means if someone from headoffice is logged into system, he will not be able to see branch invoices till he forcefully refreshes browser, and this is not only for invoices, all transactional forms/listings."

**Root cause**: `db` (`App.tsx`'s top-level `DatabaseState`) is populated from `GET /api/state` exactly once, in a `useEffect` keyed on `sessionUserId` — i.e. once at login. Every other update to `db` comes from `triggerDbRefresh()`, called only from specific post-write call sites scattered through `InvoiceModule`/`QuotationModule`/etc. (after a create/update succeeds). `handleNavigate(tabId)` — the function every single left-nav click and sidebar item calls — only ever flipped local UI state (`activeTab`, `editTarget`, the accordion's `expandedNavGroups`); it never called `triggerDbRefresh()`. Every listing component (`InvoiceModule`, `QuotationModule`, `MasterEntities`, `RecurringExpenses`, `PosModule`, `InventoryModule`, the Employees module) renders straight off that cached `db` prop and has no fetch-on-mount of its own — confirmed by grepping each for `useEffect` blocks that call a fetch or `onRefreshDb` on mount; none exist. So the *only* thing that ever repopulated `db` from Postgres after initial login was one of those specific post-write refreshes — a record created by another user, another branch, another browser tab, or (as in this diagnosis) directly in the database, was invisible to an already-open session until a full page reload re-ran the one-time login fetch. This affected every transactional listing app-wide, exactly as the user generalized, not just invoices.

**Corrected a wrong claim along the way**: initially believed (per user's own recollection) that this had already been fixed previously — "loading will be from server each time menu item is clicked from left nav bar" was described as an established, previously-verified behavior. A full search of this file and `App.tsx` turned up no such fix ever existing; the closest previously-fixed bugs in this area (the `dbStore.ts` full-blob `/api/migrate` overwrite hazards, the sidebar accordion fixes in items 78/79) solve the *opposite* class of problem — bad/stale **writes** clobbering the server — not missing **reads** on navigation. Whatever earlier claim this was based on was inaccurate; corrected here rather than left standing.

**Fix**: `handleNavigate()` (`App.tsx`) now calls `triggerDbRefresh()` — the same `/api/state` refresh already used after every write — on every navigation, fire-and-forget (navigation itself stays instant; the render below already tolerates `db` updating a moment after mount, exactly like every existing `triggerDbRefresh()` call site already does elsewhere in this file).

**Verified live**: with the fix in place, inserted a new invoice directly into Postgres (simulating a write from another branch/session) while a browser session sat idle on a different tab, then clicked "Sales Invoices" in the left nav with no page reload — confirmed via `read_network_requests` that a fresh `GET /api/state` fired on the click, and the user independently confirmed on their own screen that the new invoice appeared immediately. Test invoice deleted afterward. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Known tradeoff, not addressed here**: this refetches the entire per-company `/api/state` blob (all invoices, quotations, expenses, customers, etc.) on every single nav click, not just the module being opened. Given this app's stated priority of scaling to thousands of concurrent SaaS users, a future pass should move toward per-module fetch endpoints instead of one monolithic blob re-pulled on every click — this fix intentionally matches the existing `triggerDbRefresh()` pattern already used everywhere else in this file rather than introducing a new fetching architecture, but it is not the final scalable shape of this data layer.

## 94. Cross-Tenant Data Leak: `employees`, `jobTitles`, `productUnitConversions` Sent Unfiltered To Every Company (Sep 2026)

**Found while investigating item 93 above** (the left-nav refetch fix), not separately reported — tracing exactly what `GET /api/state`'s `filteredState` object (`server.ts`, ~line 1019) does and does not scope by `companyId` surfaced that three tables were never added to that filter list at all: `employees`, `jobTitles` (both new from the HR Foundation feature, item 89), and `productUnitConversions` (packaging/barcode/bulk-pricing rows, no `companyId` column of its own — scoped only indirectly via `productId -> productsServices.companyId`). All three fell through the endpoint's `...state` spread completely unfiltered, meaning **every company's employees, job titles, and product packaging/pricing data was sent to every other company's users on every `/api/state` call** — a genuine cross-tenant data isolation break, the exact invariant this app's `CLAUDE.md` states as non-negotiable ("every business table carries a `companyId`... resolved per-request").

**Why this didn't visibly show up in the UI**: every client-side consumer of `db.employees`/`db.jobTitles` (`EmployeesModule.tsx`, `InvoiceModule.tsx`'s `eligibleSalesAssociates`) already filters by `companyId` itself before rendering anything, so no other tenant's names ever appeared on screen. That client-side filtering masked the leak visually but did nothing to stop the raw data from being transmitted to the browser — inspectable via devtools/network tab by any logged-in user of any company, for every other tenant on the platform. `productUnitConversions` had no client-side filter checked at all as part of this pass, since it isn't currently consumed by name anywhere obvious — the leak there was purely at the transport layer.

**Fix**: added explicit `companyId`-scoped filters for all three to `filteredState` in `server.ts`, matching the existing pattern used for every other table (`productCategories`/`unitsOfMeasure` for the company-only, non-branch-restricted shape). `productUnitConversions` required an indirect scope (build a `Set` of the current company's `productId`s first, same technique already used for `userRoles`/`userBranches` scoping via company user ids) since it has no `companyId` column to filter on directly.

**Verified live**: inserted a job title + employee for the *other* real company in this database ("Naqsh Co- CNC") directly via SQL, then called `fetch('/api/state')` from a browser session logged into "CNC Woodcraft & Design" and confirmed zero rows matching the other company's test data came back (`leakedEmp: [], leakedJt: []`) — before the fix this would have shown up in the raw response. Test rows deleted afterward. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Immediate follow-up requested by the user**: given this pattern (a table quietly missing from the one endpoint responsible for company-scoping) already happened twice for genuinely different reasons (`employees`/`jobTitles` newly added and simply never wired in; `productUnitConversions` structurally different — no `companyId` of its own — and apparently missed for that reason), the user asked for a full sweep of every `server/routes/*.ts` file for the same two bug classes: (1) any list/read endpoint missing a `companyId` filter, and (2) IDOR — any fetch/update/delete-by-`:id` endpoint that doesn't also verify the target row's `companyId` matches the caller's, which would let one tenant read or mutate another tenant's record directly by guessing/enumerating its UUID even if the correlating list endpoint is itself correctly scoped. See item 95 below for the results.

## 95. Full Route-Level Tenant-Isolation Audit — One Critical IDOR, Systemic Write-Side FK Spoofing, Two Minor Leaks

**Scope**: every `db.select`/`update`/`delete`/`insert` across all 12 files in `server/routes/*.ts`, checked against three bug classes: (1) a list/read endpoint missing a `companyId` filter, (2) IDOR — a fetch/update/delete-by-`:id` endpoint not verifying the target row's `companyId`, (3) an INSERT trusting a client-supplied `companyId` instead of `req.targetCompanyId`. Standard set by the user directly: *"from server route there should be no request without company ID except companies directories in Admin Settings"* — the super-admin company switcher is the one legitimate exception (navigation metadata, not tenant data), everything else must be scoped. Read every route file in full (not sampled) via a dedicated audit pass; each finding below was independently re-verified live before being called fixed, not trusted from the audit report alone.

**1. CRITICAL — IDOR: `GET /api/zatca/company-status/:companyId` leaked any tenant's ZATCA identity to any admin** (`server/routes/zatca.ts`). The file's shared `router.use()` ownership gate reads `req.body?.companyId || req.params?.companyId` to compare against `req.targetCompanyId` — but for a path-less `router.use()` middleware, Express has not yet matched the specific route below it, so `req.params.companyId` is always `undefined` at that point for a GET route carrying its target purely in the URL (every other route in this file passes `companyId` in the POST body, which *is* already populated when the middleware runs — this GET-with-URL-param route was the only one exposed). The mismatch check silently never fired, and the handler itself did zero ownership checking of its own, returning TIN, CR number, full address, which environments are onboarded, and the next ICV/hash-chain state for *any* company id an admin of *any* company cared to try. Fixed by adding an explicit `!isSuperAdminUser(req.user) && companyId !== req.targetCompanyId` check directly inside the handler (mirroring the identical pattern already used correctly by `submit-invoice`'s own explicit check a few lines above it). **Verified live**: `fetch('/api/zatca/company-status/<other-company-id>')` from a non-super-admin session now returns `403 Forbidden: company mismatch` (previously would have returned the full profile).

**Dead code found alongside it**: a second, later `router.post('/submit-invoice/:invoiceId', ...)` handler (near the end of the file) duplicated the route already defined earlier in the file — Express always routes to the *first* registered handler for a given method+path, so this second copy could never actually execute. It also lacked the first copy's company-ownership check entirely. Harmless today only because it's unreachable; a landmine if the routes were ever reordered. Deleted.

**2. Systemic — write-side FK spoofing: `customerId`/`bankId`/`taxSlabId`/`vendorId` never checked against the caller's company on document creation** (`server/routes/transactions.ts`). This exact risk was already correctly guarded in several *payment-recording* routes (e.g. `/invoices/:id/paid`'s own code comment: *"A client-supplied bankId must actually belong to this company — otherwise a malformed/malicious request could post a disbursement against another tenant's bank account"*) but was never applied to the actual *document-creation* paths: `POST /invoices`, `POST /quotations`, `PUT /quotations/:id`, `POST /quotations/:id/convert`, `POST /recurring-postings`, `POST /settle-accrual`, `POST /recurring-templates`, `PUT /recurring-templates/:id`, `PUT /accruals/:id` all took one or more of `customerId`/`bankId`/`taxSlabId`/`vendorId` straight from the request body and inserted/used them with no ownership check. A user of Company A could set any of these to a UUID belonging to Company B — the resulting row still carries Company A's `companyId`, but with a live foreign-tenant reference embedded in it (e.g. a Receipt voucher pointing at Company B's real bank account, or an invoice attributed to Company B's customer), corrupting bank reconciliation/customer-statement reports and potentially exposing Company B's customer's name/address/VAT number wherever that FK gets joined and displayed.

Fixed with one shared `assertDocumentRefsOwnedByCompany()` helper (new, top of `transactions.ts`) — checks `customerId`/`vendorId`/`bankId` belong to the company, and `taxSlabId` (including every line item's own `taxSlabId`, not just the header's) belongs to the company *or* is one of the global company-agnostic defaults (same convention already established for `taxSlabs` in `GET /api/state`) — applied at all 9 call sites above, always before the transaction/insert. Fields already re-derived from an already-company-checked parent row (e.g. `quotation.customerId`/`quotation.taxSlabId` as fallbacks in the convert route, `template.vendorId`/`template.taxSlabId` in recurring-postings) were left alone — only the *client-suppliable overrides* needed the new check. **Verified live**: a spoofed `POST /transactions/invoices` with another real company's `customerId` now returns `400 "Selected customer was not found for this company."`; the same request with a legitimate same-company `customerId` proceeds normally (a full end-to-end success wasn't re-confirmed with a complete payload, but the ownership check itself demonstrably passes through valid input and only rejects the cross-tenant one — full regression coverage came from the existing test suite, see below).

**3. Minor — two unscoped lookups in `server/routes/inventory.ts`** feeding a default-branch inference: `POST /purchase-orders` read a linked purchase requisition's `branchId`, and `POST /purchase-bills` read a linked GRN's `warehouseId` (then that warehouse's `branchId`), both keyed on a client-supplied id with no `companyId` filter — before the transaction's own real ownership check on the same row a few lines later. Impact was low (only a UUID leaks, and the actual document creation already fails downstream once the transaction's real check runs), but both were tightened to scope by `companyId` at the point of the read, not just later.

**Not a finding, corrected in the audit report instead of acted on**: the audit's draft also flagged `GET /api/state`'s `employees`/`jobTitles`/`productUnitConversions` gap — that's item 94 above, already fixed before this audit ran; excluded here to avoid double-counting.

`tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. Full test suite re-run after all fixes: the only failures are pre-existing and unrelated — confirmed by re-running each failing file in isolation (all pass alone): the already-flagged cross-file test-pollution flakiness, plus a September-1 date rollover mid-session that pushed "today" outside several tests' hardcoded fiscal-month fixtures (unrelated to any code changed here).

## 96. Full Route-Level Branch/Location-Isolation Audit — Privilege Escalation, ~20 IDOR Endpoints, Write-Side Warehouse Spoofing, Three More Cross-Tenant Bugs Found Along the Way

**User's standard, stated directly**: "data leakage across location is a crime same as tenant data leakage" — every route response and mutation must respect `req.allowedBranchIds` (a branch-restricted user's exact assigned set; `null` = unrestricted), with the same rigor item 95's tenant audit applied to `companyId`. A dedicated audit agent read `server.ts`, `src/db/schema.ts`, `server/lib/authz.ts`, and every file in `server/routes/*.ts` in full, checking three bug classes (read/list leaks, IDOR-by-branch on action-by-id routes, and write-side branch spoofing on creation). Every finding was independently re-verified (live API calls, not the report alone) before being called fixed, matching item 95's protocol.

**Headline finding**: `req.allowedBranchIds` was referenced in exactly **one place in the entire server** before this fix — `GET /api/state`'s own `branchOk`/`branchOkViaWarehouse` closures. Every other route file — `transactions.ts`, `expenses.ts`, `inventory.ts`, `pos.ts`, `masterEntities.ts`, `branches.ts`, `users.ts`, `zatca.ts` — never checked it at all. Essentially every mutating route in the app was open to this, not one file.

**New shared helpers** (`server/lib/authz.ts`): `branchAccessOk(req, branchId)` mirrors `server.ts`'s `branchOk` byte-for-byte (unrestricted caller always passes; a restricted caller must have the row's own branchId in their set — including the same inherited asymmetry that a `null` branchId, a pre-branch-adoption document, is only visible/actionable to an unrestricted caller, matching the read-path's existing rule rather than inventing a new one). `branchAccessOkViaWarehouse(dbOrTx, req, warehouseId)` is the async variant for the five tables with no `branchId` of their own (GRN, Purchase Return, Physical Stock Take, Inventory Stock, Stock Ledger) — resolves the warehouse's own branch first.

**1. Privilege escalation, most severe finding — `POST /api/users` branch self-escalation** (`server/routes/users.ts`). `data.branchIds` was validated to belong to the target company but never checked against the caller's own `req.allowedBranchIds`. Since `users.create`/`.update` are delegable to non-admin roles, a branch-restricted holder of that permission could edit their **own** user record and add themselves to every branch in the company — self-escalating their own `allowedBranchIds` on their very next request. Fixed: out-of-scope branch ids are now silently dropped (same reported-back shape as the existing cross-company drop a few lines above) rather than accepted.

**2. Backfill-unassigned bulk action gated on the wrong authority** (`server/routes/branches.ts`). `POST /branches/backfill-unassigned` reassigns every unassigned document *company-wide* onto one branch — a company-wide bulk action — but was gated only on the ordinary `branches.update` leaf. A branch-restricted holder of that leaf could annex every other branch's unassigned history onto their own. Fixed: now requires `req.allowedBranchIds === null` (unrestricted) outright. Branch create/edit/toggle-active (same file) also gained `branchAccessOk` checks — a branch-restricted holder of `branches.update` can edit their own branch's details but not a different one's.

**3. ~20 IDOR-by-branch findings, fixed with the same `branchAccessOk` check inserted right after each row's existing company-scoped fetch, before any mutation**:
- `transactions.ts`: `PUT/POST /quotations/:id` (edit, cancel, convert), `POST /invoices/:id/note` (Credit/Debit Note — a real ZATCA document, and this route didn't even have the list endpoint's own-documents restriction), `POST /invoices/:id/paid`, `POST /invoices/:id/cancel`. The quotation PUT route also had its own smaller bug fixed alongside: it spread the client's full body into the update `SET`, silently allowing `branchId` to be changed on edit despite being documented as immutable elsewhere in the same file — pinned back to the existing value.
- `inventory.ts`: PR edit/withdraw/approve-reject, PO cancel, GRN reverse, purchase bill edit/pay/cancel, purchase return create/cancel, stock take finalize/cancel.
- `expenses.ts`: upsert-by-id, pay, cancel — plus `GET /` gained a branch filter (it had none at all, unlike its list-scoping siblings).
- `pos.ts` (see item 4 below for the schema change this needed): shift close, held-invoice delete/create.
- `zatca.ts`: `POST /submit-invoice/:invoiceId` — explicitly documented as usable by ordinary line staff, not admin-gated, so this one mattered.
- `masterEntities.ts`: warehouse edit.

**4. `posShifts`/`posHeldInvoices` had no `branchId` column to check against at all** — the audit's specific POS callout. Added `branchId` to both (`src/db/schema.ts`, pushed via `db:push`), resolved/validated at shift-open time through `resolveDocumentBranchId` (same choke-point every other document type uses) and denormalized onto held invoices from their owning shift (never trusted from the client). `GET /api/pos/shifts` and `GET /api/pos/held-invoices`, plus their `GET /api/state` fields, now scope by it.

**5. Write-side branch/warehouse spoofing, and three MORE real cross-tenant bugs found while fixing it**: `POST /goods-receipt-notes`, `POST /stock-adjustments`, and `POST /stock-takes` (`inventory.ts`) all took a client-supplied `warehouseId` and used it directly in inserts with **no check that it even belonged to the caller's own company** — not just a missing branch check, a missing tenant check entirely, in the same file items 95's audit had already passed as clean. A crafted request from Company A could receive stock into, adjust stock in, or open a stock take against Company B's warehouse, with the resulting `inventoryStocks`/`stockLedgerTransactions` rows filed under Company A's own `companyId` — corrupting both tenants' inventory records simultaneously. All three fixed with an explicit `companyId` + `branchAccessOk` check before use. `POST /warehouses` (`masterEntities.ts`) had the same gap on `data.branchId` itself (written verbatim, zero validation) — fixed the same way.

**6. The quiet one — `resolveSaleWarehouse`'s explicit-override path** (`server/lib/businessLogic.ts`, called from `POST /invoices` and `POST /quotations/:id/convert`). An invoice's own `branchId` was already correctly resolved/validated, but a client could still pass an explicit `warehouseId` belonging to a *different* branch — the visible document looks correctly attributed to the caller's own branch (passes every other check, looks legitimate in every report), while the actual stock deduction silently drains a different branch's inventory. Harder to notice than every other finding here specifically because the document itself looks right. Fixed by threading `req.allowedBranchIds` through `resolveSaleWarehouse`/`assertSalesWarehouse` (kept as an explicit parameter, not the whole `req`, so this business-logic file stays decoupled from the request layer) and checking the resolved warehouse's own branch.

**7. Read-leak findings**: `GET /api/state`'s `productWarehouses` gained `branchOkViaWarehouse`; `GET /api/employees`, `GET /api/warehouses`, `GET /api/product-warehouses` (previously `companyId`-only, no branch check at all) now scope by branch too.

**8. Judgment call — Employees, resolved as a bespoke rule, not the standard helper**: the audit flagged that `EmployeesModule.tsx`'s actual HR admin screen shows every company employee (name, job title, branch, email, phone, hire/termination date) to any viewer regardless of their own branch restriction — real PII exposure across branches, though not a financial/control gap. Fixed in both `GET /api/employees` and `GET /api/state`'s `employees` field — but deliberately *not* with `branchAccessOk`'s usual null-means-unrestricted-only rule: a Head-Office employee (`branchId: null`) must stay visible to every branch (that's the whole point of the Head-Office pattern this app already relies on for the Invoice Sales Associate picker). The new rule is bespoke: visible if the caller is unrestricted, OR the employee's own `branchId` is null, OR it's in the caller's `allowedBranchIds` — only *other* branches' staff are now hidden from a restricted viewer.

**9. A serious regression caught by the test suite, fixed at its actual root cause**: after wiring in all the `branchAccessOk` checks above, two previously-passing tests broke — a permission-scoped user with no `userBranches` row could no longer cancel their own quotation or approve a purchase requisition, even in a company with **zero branches configured at all**. Root cause: `req.allowedBranchIds`'s existing resolution (predates this session) treats "zero rows in `userBranches`" as "restricted to nothing," which is correct for a company that actually uses branches, but wrong for the vast majority of ordinary users in a company that has never adopted branches — every one of that company's documents carries `branchId: null`, and an empty array never matches `null`, so those users were silently locked out of acting on anything the moment a branch check was added to a route they used. Fixed at the source (`server.ts`'s `isAuthenticated`): branch restriction now only applies once the caller's company has at least one real branch — the same "optional until adopted" rule this app already applies to every other branch-related requirement (warehouses, users, invoices). Two test assertions in `tests/posShiftsAndHeldInvoices.test.ts` were also updated separately — not a regression, an intentional behavior *improvement*: those two specific routes previously silently no-op'd (200 "success" on a WHERE clause matching zero rows) for a cross-company id; they now correctly 404, since this pass added an explicit existence+ownership check they didn't have before.

**Verified live** (not just via the test suite): a spoofed `GET /api/zatca/company-status/<other-company>` returns 403 (item 95, re-confirmed still correct); the branch self-escalation attempt on `users.ts` now drops the out-of-scope branch id with an explanatory reason instead of applying it; opening a POS shift now persists a resolved `branchId`. `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. Full test suite: 311/316 passing, the remaining 5 confirmed to be the pre-existing, unrelated fiscal-month date-rollover issue (passes 17/17 in isolation).

**Known limitation carried forward, not fixed here**: this pass covered `server/routes/*.ts` with the same rigor as item 95's tenant audit, plus a supplementary grep sweep of every route file for the specific "foreign-key-shaped field from the client used in a write with no ownership check" pattern that caused items 5's three cross-tenant bugs to slip past the *original* tenant audit — that sweep came back clean on every file not already covered above. It is not a formal guarantee against every possible variant of this bug class; the discovery of item 5's bugs during a supposedly-unrelated branch pass is itself evidence that a single audit pass, however thorough, is not proof of completeness.

## 97. A Mandatory Third Look — Live Black-Box Pen-Test (11/11 Pass) Plus a Second Mechanical Audit That Found 5 More Real Gaps

**User's instruction, verbatim**: "another look is mandatory before declaring full isolation is achieved for tenant and branch level." Given the track record — item 95's audit found real bugs, then item 96's *separate* audit found real bugs item 95 had missed in the exact same files — the user was right not to accept a second clean report at face value. This item covers two genuinely independent verification passes run in parallel, not a repeat of the same method.

**Pass A — live black-box penetration test, not code review.** Created a real branch-restricted user (assigned only to Riyadh HQ Branch, broad permissions) and fired real attack requests at the running server: approve/withdraw another branch's purchase requisition, reverse a GRN on a company-wide (null-branch) warehouse, create a GRN/stock-adjustment/stock-take in the *other real company's* warehouse, create a warehouse in a branch the user isn't assigned to, edit another branch's details, run the company-wide backfill action as a restricted user, and confirm the employees list hides other branches' staff while keeping Head-Office staff visible — plus one deliberate sanity check (cancel a PO in the user's *own* branch) to confirm the fixes don't over-block legitimate access. **11/11 passed** — every attack rejected, the sanity check succeeded. Test user/role and the sanity-check PO's status were fully cleaned up afterward.

**Pass B — a second mechanical audit, deliberately different methodology.** Prior passes read a file and formed a holistic judgment ("does this look scoped correctly?") — a method that had already proven to miss things twice. This pass instead built and filled in an explicit per-route checklist (five yes/no questions per handler: is a foreign-key-shaped field read from the client; is it checked against `companyId` before use; is a `branchAccessOk`/`branchAccessOkViaWarehouse` check present when the table is branch-relevant; does the GET/list WHERE clause include `companyId` and, where relevant, branch; is `companyId` always server-set on insert) across all 12 route files again, explicitly told which 11 checks Pass A had already empirically confirmed so it could focus elsewhere. **Found 5 more real gaps**, all the same underlying pattern (a foreign-key field from the client used in a write with no ownership check) that had already caused item 96's three cross-tenant bugs — confirming that pattern was still not fully closed:

1. **`productId` never validated against company, across every inventory-item and sales-item route** (`server/routes/inventory.ts`, `server/routes/transactions.ts`, `server/lib/businessLogic.ts`). PR create/edit, PO create, GRN create, stock-adjustment create, stock-take create, plus invoice/quotation/quotation-convert item insertion all took `item.productId` straight from the client with zero check it belongs to the caller's company. A crafted request could reference a real product id from a different company, filing stock/ledger/line-item rows under the caller's own `companyId` that point at a foreign product — corrupting the caller's own inventory records and leaking the other company's product name/pricing through any error message or report that joins `productId` back to `productsServices` without its own company filter (e.g. `assertStockAvailable`'s own error text names the product). Fixed with one new shared helper, `assertProductsOwnedByCompany(tx, companyId, productIds)` (`server/lib/businessLogic.ts`), applied at every one of those creation/edit sites — the same "one helper, many call sites" shape as item 95's `assertDocumentRefsOwnedByCompany`. Stock-take *finalize* reads `productId` back from already-validated `physicalStockTakeItems` rows (inserted at create time), so fixing create alone closes finalize too — verified by reading the finalize code path, not assumed.
